## Changelog
| Date | Sections Updated | Summary |
|------|-----------------|---------|
| 2026-11-10c | Build & Runtime, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Upgrade Drizzle ORM to 0.45.4.** Pin the approved ORM release without changing schema or applying a migration. The isolated Node 24 verification group passed all 1,445 regression checks and TypeScript checks before the next framework upgrade. No forced audit fix. |
| 2026-11-10b | Build & Runtime, Environment Variables, Pages, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Harden deployment and image handling.** Nuxt Security 2.7.0 adds per-response SSR script nonces and security headers without cross-origin isolation. CSP starts report-only and supports runtime enforcement after staging checks; runtime allowed origins supply WebSocket endpoints. Changed image URLs require HTTPS and invalid legacy images use a local fallback. Node 24 multi-stage container runs only production output as node; secrets/caches are excluded. Operator deployment, ingress diagnostics and credential repair are documented. |
| 2026-11-10a | API Schema, Data Models, Pages, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Secure account-free campaign play.** Shared HTTP/WS admission, signed player credentials, runtime field/link validation, scoped reads and public/owned Workshop policy. Authoritative combat/movement/door/turn intents, atomic Charge/Pass, bounded receipts and one-use Charge-after allowances; notification authentication, expiry, Origin and rate controls. Async versioned password upgrades and offline-only verification; no migration. |
| 2026-11-09l | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Apply Naturewalk environments and require supported jump launches.** First Naturewalk element is free, the second costs 1 DP, with at most two distinct elements. Map elements/materials determine qualifying environments and the GM can add environments or disable automatic terrain detection in Hazards. Shared conditional bonuses cover damage, dodge, armor, movement, Armor Piercing, Poison duration, effect resistance, Fire Burn immunity and Darkness sight; Advanced Swimmer gains Water's two temporary wounds once per combat. Participant environment/grant markers and settings use existing JSONB, with authoritative refreshes after movement, terrain edits, roster/form changes and GM overrides. Jump searches retain actual launch points: dry solid support permits jumps, water requires Extra Movement: Swimmer, and unsupported air cannot launch another jump. Mixed movement retains cumulative speed costs and normal turn-end gravity. No migration. |
| 2026-11-09k | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Add a gently flickering light-source material.** The map picker offers Flickering Light for blocks, ramps and repainting. Its warm light varies smoothly by at most six percent and illuminates nearby terrain. Persistent local lights combine matching adjacent cells within terrain chunks; existing batches update source colors without rebuilding geometry each frame. Clipping/removal/unmount release lights and stop idle animation. The existing Light material, transparent sorting, selection and gameplay sight/movement rules remain compatible. Material IDs save in existing map JSON; no migration. |
| 2026-11-09j | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Apply movement qualities and add digging materials.** Default swimming/jump height and length use half Basic Movement; Extra Movement uses full Movement. Advanced swimming/digging/climbing/flight add actual RAM, while Advanced Jumper adds CPU×5 height/CPU length. Shared weighted paths combine movement modes within one normal move. Swimmers move vertically and surface from depth; only Extra Swimmer treats water as a fall floor, with applicable damage to its surface. Digger enters Earth/Sand/Snow; Advanced Digger also enters neutral/wood/ice/glass and Soft Metal at double cost, with Steel blocked. Wallclimbers need vertical support; Advanced Wallclimber can cling to ceilings. Sand, Snow and Soft Metal are selectable block/ramp materials, preserving valid element affinities and undo/redo. Normal action costs and saved formats remain compatible; no migration. |
| 2026-11-09i | Pages & Components, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Stabilize token images during rotation and lift attack range rings above floors.** Camera world/view matrices refresh after controls update, azimuth changes and GM move-height scrolling, so portrait positions/sizes use the same pose as the WebGL map. Ranged attack rings sit 0.015 units above the actual floor slab, retaining their radius, colors and targeting rules. |
| 2026-11-09h | Pages & Components, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Keep token portraits visible through transparent voxels.** Terrain portrait occlusion skips blocks and ramps whose effective opacity is below one, including transparent materials, voxel windows and custom opacity. Fully opaque voxels still occlude, including behind translucent terrain and opacity-one material overrides. Picking, movement and gameplay targeting/LoS retain their existing rules. |
| 2026-11-09g | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Place stairs like ramps.** Stairs draw on map cells in Line/Square/Cube modes with a three-tread ghost and scroll-wheel Uphill rotation, sharing the ramp direction brush. One atomic stair stroke supports replacement, undo/redo and queued saving. Shared closed stair geometry renders the real stairs and preview; legacy east/west face encoding is mapped explicitly so existing saved stairs keep their appearance. No saved-map schema change. |
| 2026-11-09f | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Scale large maps without reducing graphics.** Editor strokes publish one immutable map root with indexed lookups, client-only geometry revisions and 50 delta undo commands. Terrain uses incremental 16×16 chunks, opaque instancing, shared resources and a globally sorted RGBA transparent batch; picking/occlusion checks nearby chunks. Canvas frames and pointer work run on demand with explicit preview/resource cleanup. Autosaves coalesce changed collections at 300ms/1500ms, serialize one request per map, acknowledge compactly and notify peers after commit; failures retain edits and block navigation until retry succeeds. Library editors reuse their loaded map. No migration, dependency or gameplay-rule change. |
| 2026-11-09e | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Rotate the ramp brush with the mouse wheel.** Scrolling over the canvas while the GM Ramp tool is active cycles uphill direction clockwise/down or counterclockwise/up. The editor owns direction state, so the toolbar and placed ramps agree; hover/drag previews refresh after Vue applies the new direction. Canvas capture consumes rotation before camera zoom, with movement/attack elevation keeping priority and overlay scrolling retaining its existing behavior. |
| 2026-11-09d | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Add ramp voxels to map editing.** The Ramp tool shares element painting and Line/Square/Cube drawing, with four uphill directions. Optional voxel shape/direction fields persist in existing JSONB and undo/redo snapshots; older voxels remain blocks. Shared Three.js geometry renders closed wedges in the map and hover previews, keeps transparent ramps out of cube surface merging, and angles spawn indicators onto slopes. Integer grid movement, support and placement retain existing voxel rules; no migration. |
| 2026-11-09c | Pages & Components, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Use player-style action dots in the GM map roster.** `MapPlayerHUD` replaces the GM's remaining-action text with the existing yellow unspent/grey spent dots beside each compact card. It shows at least two dots and expands for bonus actions, retaining an accessible count and hover title. Existing health bars and placement controls remain intact. All 13 HUD rendering tests pass. |
| 2026-11-09b | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Control player turns from the GM map radial.** Clicking the active Tamer or any of that Tamer's partners opens normal player actions alongside GM Move and Manage; other turns and noncombat phases retain GM controls only. Direct/Bolster Direct uses normal range, action and once-per-turn checks and the clicked Tamer's identity. Orders expose unlocked Special/Skill Orders, including Fateful Intervention with that Tamer as payer. Partner evolution checks the Tamer's action budget; Mode Change and clash actions reuse existing handlers. Remove the floating Fateful payer selector/button while retaining the roll dialog and attack rider. No API or migration changes. All 1,012 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-09a | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Complex Lifesteal/Shield options, mandatory Haste/Lag costs and next-round Lag.** The third radial tier offers Double Lifesteal and Triple Shield only for their effects, replacing either Bolster choice. Healing is capped by damage and CPU (twice CPU when upgraded); Shield grants BIT (three times BIT when upgraded), shares one use per caster/round, expires after combat and never adds Combat Monster damage. Haste grants one Simple Action with a single action-credit marker rather than a scaled duration. Haste and Lag always cost two Simple Actions. Lag automatically rolls dBIT, keeps the strongest same-round application, moves the target's turn slot next round and restores its base order afterward, including grouped partners. GM/player, single/area, NPC, defense and intercede paths retain the chosen upgrades. No migration. All 1,000 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-09 | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Move Charge timing into the third radial tier and remove duplicate map attack options.** Only non-Pass Charge attacks offer mutually exclusive Move Before/Move After toggles; neither selected means attack without moving. Existing riders can be combined with a timing choice at no additional action cost. The map descriptor carries attack identity/timing, and EncounterMap starts the existing movement flow without a second picker or blocking ordinary Charge targeting. Equivalent polling snapshots preserve the charge step. Both roles retain a compact target hint/Cancel and their card-dialog options. All 958 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08u | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Use a GM-owned Total XP budget and calculate Available XP from spending.** The Library's sole XP editor adjusts the total and unspent balance together; player and Workshop sheets have no XP-setting control. Purchases/refunds preserve the budget, known consumed Inspiration remains spent, and totals below already-spent XP are rejected. Tamer APIs enforce campaign GM permission for budget changes and nonzero starting/imported XP, reject independent wallet edits and lock the current Tamer row through validation/save against simultaneous rewards. Legacy unknown history remains explicitly incomplete until a GM initializes a budget from recorded purchases. No migration. All 945 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08t | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Grant Bonus DP per partner across evolution stages.** The rewards roster groups linked forms into one partner, with separate choices for multiple partners belonging to one Tamer. Grants deduplicate stage aliases, reconcile the shared pool from the highest saved stage value and update every linked owned form once. Receipts preserve resolved partner counts and affected stage IDs for retries; live refresh covers every changed form. Failed roster loads offer Retry instead of claiming there are no Tamers, and committed grant results survive refresh failures. User confirmed the database errors resolved; no further migration changes were made. All 940 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08s | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **DM Library Encounters and Grant Rewards.** New cards link to encounters and a rewards page supporting one, several or all campaign Tamers. XP, free Inspiration and Bonus DP apply per selected recipient; exact partner forms can be chosen. One transaction updates balances, known lifetime XP, active-encounter Inspiration and a durable request receipt, so retries cannot award twice. Players receive campaign reward pushes and GMs refresh encounter records after commit. GM accounts or server-verified GM-password credentials protect both reward endpoints. Migration 0025 creates the grant receipts table; 0024 is also required for lifetime XP. All 931 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08r | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Correct Inspiration, track lifetime XP, identify the digivolution payer and show GM map action balances.** Player panels and GM cards include XP-enhanced Willpower in the Inspiration maximum. Nullable `tamers.total_xp` stores lifetime XP earned independently of spending; older characters/imports show Not recorded until initialized. New XP awards increase it while purchases, refunds and consumed Inspiration preserve it. Forms and character export/import retain the value. Partner evolution costs identify the Tamer, and the GM map roster shows live remaining Simple Actions. Apply migration 0024 before running the new schema. |
| 2026-11-08q | Pages & Components, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Attack options occupy a separate third concentric radial tier.** The earlier nested branch reused the second ring; `MapRadialMenu.vue` now keeps the action ring and attack ring visible while drawing options farther out. The chosen attack is highlighted. Each choice tier has independent paging, Back/keyboard navigation closes one tier at a time, stale choices prune only invalid descendants, and clip IDs include the tier. The full three-ring radius is reserved up front to keep parent geometry stable and fit the fan inward near viewport edges. The redundant None choice is removed: Continue with no selected riders performs an ordinary attack. |
| 2026-11-08p | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Radial attack options and visible action costs.** Selecting an attack opens a third layer with None, compatible Bolster/Huge Power/Lifesteal/Inspiration toggles and Continue. Both roles preserve the chosen riders through targeting and recheck live eligibility. Costs show on root and child choices, including Inspiration totals and free GM/clash/Mode Change actions. GM outgoing Fateful waits for chosen dice using the owning Tamer; player attacks open the existing review's Fateful chooser. Direct GM attack deductions now match Haste, Lifesteal, Pass and Combat Monster costs. |
| 2026-11-08o | API Schema, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **AoE Knockback/Pull now displaces recipients after intercede decisions.** Area resolution receives the live map and current positions, accumulates returned movement patches, and applies each target against earlier displacements. Hit interceptors receive the effect instead of protected targets. Instant support attacks use the NPC defense/displacement path; submitted player support defenses also resolve forced movement. Claim-side movement remains deferred until transaction commit, while Skip publishes the completed patch. Core displacement respects Immunity. Thirty-one new regressions cover all six shapes, support/damage, player defenses, interceptors, sequential occupancy and deferred broadcasts. All 854 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08n | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Expose NPC evolution in the GM map radial.** NPC Digimon with an assigned evolution line now get Digivolve/Devolve choices from the same unlocked-form/local-stage policy as card view. The GM action-selected handler uses the normal action endpoint, without force or a fabricated Tamer roll. Live guards reject off-turn, exhausted, locked, removed and already-attempted choices; devolution retains its existing eligibility. Four new real computed/handler regressions plus existing evolution/radial coverage pass (48 targeted tests); typecheck passes. |
| 2026-11-08m | API Schema, Pages & Components, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Correct Cleanse request labels and legacy NPC Immunity.** The GM Pending Player Requests list labels Cleanse as waiting for defenses or choosing effects; unrelated request types no longer fall back to Roll Initiative. `npc-attack.post.ts` loads target qualities before support/damage resolution, blocks immune effects and their Stun/displacement side effects, skips immune Cleanse prompts, and passes qualities to the shared application helper. Ordinary hit/damage behavior and existing effects are preserved. Fifteen new real-endpoint regressions cover positive, negative and instant effects plus deferred Stun. All 819 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08l | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Cleanse selection and combat map controls.** Cleanse now gathers per-target defense outcomes and offers one GM/player effect-selection dialog per cast. The shared cap is the highest leftover Accuracy, and each chosen individual effect loses the caster's BIT turns; Selective Targeting offers mutually exclusive Dispel/Purify filters. Normal support/damage, NPC/player defenses, intercede claims and the legacy GM endpoint participate. GM Manage can unlock individual used attacks without refunding actions or other costs. Placed-token dragging is limited to setup/initiative, while radial GM Move remains available. Movement previews show the actual token at 20% opacity and a measured gap to the nearest floor/voxel beneath its centre. All 804 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08k | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **GM map Manage and source-aware Taunt/Fear controls.** GM radial menus expose the existing participant Manage modal on any turn, including clashes. EffectManager requires a participant source for Taunt/Fear in Quick Apply and custom entry, auto-fills the source's CPU/BIT, stores Taunt potency doubled, and lets GMs repair an existing effect's source. Automatic Fear effects now also use participant IDs and BIT potency, and shared attack Accuracy applies Fear only against its source. |
| 2026-11-08j | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Taunt and deferred support effects.** Intercede offers now snapshot the stored attack definition alongside roll data; single/area decisions recover missing definitions in older pending offers. This fixes support effects silently disappearing after Skip for player and GM attacks. Taunt is Negative, records the source participant ID and CPU x2 potency, and applies CPU -2 (minimum zero) for an area attack including its source. Swagger uses the same debuff classification. Regression coverage includes real offer/Skip flows, player defenses, NPC attacks, old pending data, other support effects and Selective Targeting. All 773 tests pass. |
| 2026-11-08i | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **AoE previews use translucent voxel volumes.** `MapCanvas.vue` renders full-height cells with 30% fill opacity and only the combined volume's exposed faces and outer crease edges. Shared faces and flat grid seams are removed, including between hit and blocked regions; their exposed faces retain orange/red colors. Preview replacement, cancellation and unmount dispose geometry/materials. All six AoE shapes keep their existing gameplay cells. Validation: all 748 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08h | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Reciprocal size occupancy, selectable shared tokens and unrestricted GM aiming.** Tiny pairings may share squares; Small pairings may pass but not finish together; other pairings block, including allies and Tamers. Shared policy covers full-footprint client paths, placement, charge/Pass landings and server intercede/displacement/throw checks. Single-target claims revalidate routes before saving; area claims validate throws against the interceptor's new position. Clicking shared tokens first opens a character chooser for health, owned actions or targeting. GM Move uses an unlimited aim plane, height scrolling, destination ghost and camera height following, with no action cost or movement-capability checks. Validation: all 734 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08g | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Keep flying/digging movement paths above a flat floor when an equally short level route exists.** The screenshot symptom was a valid distance label plus yellow endpoints with intermediate markers hidden underneath ground tiles: neighbour-order ties selected unnecessary negative-Y steps. Both cached BFS predecessors and fallback A* in `utils/pathfinding.ts` now minimize vertical travel after minimizing movement steps. Required 3D routes, lower floors, budgets and reachability remain supported. Four real path/render visibility regressions reproduce the failure before the fix; all 695 tests and typecheck pass afterward. |
| 2026-11-08f | Pages & Components, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Cache the movement watcher's map signature independently.** `EncounterMap.vue` serializes the reactive map in a map-only `computed`; position, participant, character-record and destroyed-structure changes reuse that signature. Watch inputs, invalidation and `flush: 'sync'` remain unchanged. Counting-serialization tests verify cache reuse and synchronous voxel/wall/door/replacement invalidation. Position-only synthetic benchmarks at 85/518/1831 KB improved from 7.431/57.363/213.859 to 0.512/3.348/11.126 ms per trigger; the outer key still copies the cached string, and map edits still serialize the map. No size cap. Validation: all 689 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08e | Pages & Components, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Keep partner movement previews visible.** `EncounterMap.vue` compares movement inputs by value so equivalent encounter snapshots and health/action updates preserve the hovered path; post-charge movement uses the moving partner's participant ID. `MapCanvas.vue` centers path markers, destination ghosts and distance labels on the mover's actual footprint. Regression coverage includes polling, charge moves, size changes and non-square gigantic footprints. Validation: all 687 tests and typecheck pass. |
| 2026-11-08d | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Canonical automatic devolution and consistent partner stage resolution.** `partnerDevolution.autoDevolveParticipant` now owns knockout eligibility, history/fallback restoration, wound totals, Combat Monster cap, history consumption, partner shared-line versus NPC local-stage tracking, and the Auto-Devolve log. Single/area/intercede/response/legacy attack, PUT and shared knockout paths delegate to it while retaining aura/turn/save ordering. `resolveCombatEvolutionIndex` resolves partner entity IDs before direction, warp, costs and history decisions in manual evolution and option building; the fallback resolver uses the same rule. Digivolve keeps an explicit Digimon-only row lookup and reuses the loaded parent sheet. No API wire-format or migration change. |
| 2026-11-08c | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Partners may devolve without in-combat evolution history.** Real non-enemy partners can return to a linked direct parent even when they entered combat already evolved or their Tamer is absent. Saved combat history takes precedence; otherwise the parent sheet supplies saved wounds and calculated wound totals. Shared parent validation and server state resolution cover manual choices, attack/intercede knockouts, turn effects, movement/throw/reflect knockouts and nested falls. Root forms and broken links have no fallback; NPCs retain history-only automatic devolution. No migration or wire-format change. |
| 2026-11-08b | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Reduce End Turn database and token-rendering work.** The encounter PUT shares one request-local combat-record scope, preloading current/incoming Digimon and wound-history forms plus partner Tamers. Cached bulk loaders reuse pending and missing records across gravity, damage over time, knockouts/devolution, round/encounter-start triggers and Guiding Light without changing processing or aura ordering. Map tokens rebuild only for geometry changes; turn changes refresh existing outline colors and close the radial. HTTP/WebSocket payloads and gameplay rules are unchanged. |
| 2026-11-08 | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Share map attack adapters, combat evolution choices and actor/partner turn eligibility.** Both encounter pages use `utils/mapAttackDescriptor.ts` for selected-attack metadata, effect-adjusted map stats, size offsets and Reach; `EncounterMap` and `MapCanvas` share its descriptor type. `utils/combatEvolutionOptions.ts` owns unlocked-child, history-based devolution and warp-grandchild choices, preserving the GM NPC stage override and player line stage. Five attack/Clash routes use `server/utils/participantTurn.ts` for ordinary actor-or-partner turn checks, retaining their errors and specialized action exceptions. No API shape, schema or gameplay rule changes. |
| 2026-11-07o | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Shared attack option rules and canonical combat participants.** New pure `utils/combatAttackOptions.ts` owns used-attack/signature/Battery filtering, ordinary attack affordability, Bolster eligibility and Huge Power availability for both encounter pages. Page wrappers retain their own record lookup and ownership context; player Bolster now shares the GM's Haste exclusion. New `types/combat.ts` is the single declaration for `CombatParticipant`, `ActiveEffect` and `ClashState`; existing imports through `types/index.ts` and `useEncounters.ts` are re-exported. The JSONB schema uses derived `StoredCombatParticipant[]`, keeping legacy wound totals optional without copying the fields. Simple actions remain authoritative; optional legacy `complex` is deprecated. No database migration or API wire-format change. |
| 2026-11-07n | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Reuse stat calculations and share participant names (enemy-only numbering).** The player sheet's `tamerStats` now calls the existing `useTamers.calculateDerivedStats`; its missing-value zero fallback is preserved in that shared calculator. New pure `utils/movementQualities.ts` owns effective base movement (Blue Digizoid Armor's +4 is added before the minimum-of-1 clamp) and Speedy rank limits for `useDigimonForm` and `useDigimonStats`. **Behavior change:** the stage-change Speedy cap in `useDigimonForm` now includes Blue Armor, so it agrees with the live cap (it previously omitted it and could clamp Speedy ranks below the displayed cap). New pure `utils/participantDisplayNames.ts` (`buildParticipantDisplayNames`) is shared by `MapCanvas`, `MapPlayerHUD`, `encounters/[id].vue` `getDisplayName` and `player/[tamerId].vue` `getParticipantName`. **Behavior change:** only enemy Digimon are numbered (stable `seq`, or sorted participant ID for legacy ones); tamers and partner Digimon always show their base name, so two same-name partner Digimon or NPC tamers are no longer told apart by number. `server/utils/participantName.ts` `resolveParticipantName` follows the same rule for battle-log names. No schema or package changes. |
| 2026-11-07m | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Attack details fit their radial wedges.** Outer-ring text now receives the sector's radii and angles, measures the current font, and fits each row inside the available annular slice. Long text is shortened within that space while full titles and accessible descriptions remain available. Font loading refreshes measurements; per-menu SVG clip paths prevent fallback fonts from painting outside an action. Regression coverage includes diagonal sectors, narrow/edge fans, long names and multiple menu instances. No API, schema or dependency changes. |
| 2026-11-07l | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius | **Map radial attacks show range type, AoE type, effect and tags.** The Attack ring on both encounter pages used to show only the attack name; range, AoE, Charge, AP and effect were buried in a hover tooltip, and the other tags were not shown at all. Outer-ring attack sectors now stack, under the name, the range type (Melee/Ranged), the AoE type (Burst, Blast, Close Blast, Cone, Line, Pass) and the effect (bold orange), then the remaining tags. The `Area Attack…` tag is dropped from the tag line because the AoE type already shows it; the tooltip still lists everything. New `attackRadialItem(attack, disabled)` in `utils/attackBadges.ts` replaces the duplicated attack-to-radial-item mapping in `encounters/[id].vue` and `player/[tamerId].vue`. `MapRadialItem` gains optional `range`, `aoe`, `effect` and `tags`. `utils/radialMenu.ts` gains `wrapRadialTags` and `radialDetailRows` (pure row layout, max 2 name lines, effect and tags clipped with an ellipsis, range and AoE split onto two rows when too wide for one). `MapRadialMenu.vue` renders those rows for outer-ring choices that have details; stances, direct, digivolve, mode-change and door choices keep the original label-only layout. No API, data model, env or dependency changes. |
| 2026-11-07k | Pages & Components, Blast Radius | **Fix: a player could place a unit larger than medium with part of its footprint outside the placeable area.** Players' spawn-area rule only looked at the one surface under the pointer, so a 2×2, 3×3 or gigantic unit anchored on a green square could overhang cells that are not spawn points (click placement had the same hole; dragging made it easy to hit). `evaluatePlacement` (`utils/placementRules.ts`) now requires **every cell of the unit's footprint** to be a spawn cell for players, and the GM is still exempt. New `makeSpawnCellChecker(groundTiles, voxels)` defines a spawn cell as a flagged ground tile at the cell (a unit stands on a tile at the tile's own y) or a flagged voxel directly below it (a unit stands on top of a voxel, at y + 1) — the same cell a hit resolves to; `MapCanvas.vue` builds it in a `computed` that rebuilds only when the map changes. Footprints are now real `width × depth` (`PlacementFootprint`, from `getParticipantFootprintDims`, the same dimensions movement and occupancy use) instead of a square `tileCount`, so a gigantic unit with unequal width and depth is validated, blocked-checked and overlap-checked over the cells it really covers; `footprintsOverlapXZ` takes footprints, and the red/green drop preview draws the true shape. Added 12 checks to `tests/placementRules.test.mjs` (verified to fail with the spawn check removed). The server still accepts any `unit-moved`, so this remains a client-side rule. Validation: typecheck and the full 458-test suite pass; not exercised in a browser. |
| 2026-11-07j | Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **The "My Characters" HUD is now the placement control, and characters can be dragged onto and around the map.** The separate left-hand placement panel ("Place Your Digimon" for players, "Place Units" / "Deploy to Map" for the GM) and its collapsed pill are gone. `MapPlayerHUD.vue` cards (the GM's "All Participants" cards too) become buttons and drag sources while `EncounterMap`'s `placementActive` (was `showPanel`, same condition) is on: click a card then a tile, or drag the card onto the map. HUD additions: ✓ / 📍 placed badges, an orange armed card, a footer hint, a minimised-icon count badge, and **auto-expand whenever placement becomes active** (a reserve deployment used to need the collapsed panel expanded first). **Drag.** A HUD card can be dropped on the map and a token already on the map can be dragged to a new cell. Players are held to spawn areas (tiles/voxels with `isSpawnPoint`), the GM is not; solid voxels and other units block everyone. Click placement and GM free-move now go through the same `evaluatePlacement`. Who may drag what: a card — exactly the HUD's placeable set (a player's own characters; everything unplaced once combat starts); a token — the GM any placed unit in any non-`ended` phase, a player only their own and only during setup/initiative (mid-combat movement stays on the radial Move). A drop sends one `unit-moved`, never one per cell crossed. New pure `utils/placementRules.ts` (`getPlaceableParticipantIds`, `getDraggableParticipantIds`, `dropRoute`, `surfaceAllowsPlacement`, `placementCellFromHit`, `footprintsOverlapXZ`, `evaluatePlacement`, `createDragTracker`) and adapter-injected `utils/tokenDrag.ts` (`createTokenDragController`: 4px threshold, camera lock for the press and always restored, Escape, click swallowing, offer re-checked at the drop). `MapCanvas.vue` gains `draggableParticipantIds` / `externalDragParticipantId` props and a `unit-dragged` emit. **Client-side only:** `ws.ts` applies any `unit-moved`, so the player spawn-area boundary, own-units-only and setup-only rules are UI rules, not server enforcement (same trust model as the rest of the map). No API, schema, env or dependency changes. Added `tests/placementRules.test.mjs`, `tests/tokenDrag.test.mjs`, `tests/mapHudPlacement.test.mjs` (48 checks). Validation: typecheck and the full 446-test suite pass. The WebGL canvas wiring (pointer listeners, raycast, ghost) and live-database interaction were not exercised in a browser. |

| 2026-11-07d | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **One defense panel per area attack for the player's characters it hit.** An `[Area]` cast that hits a player's Tamer and partner creates one `dodge-roll` request per target; they used to be defended one modal after another. New pure `utils/areaDefense.ts`: `groupDodgeRequests` groups a player's dodge requests by the cast (`data.intercedeGroupId`, in server order; a request without a cast id — single-target attack, counterattack — is its own group, so unrelated attacks never share a panel), `computeDodgeDefense` is the Dodge pool formula extracted unchanged from the page (stance, successive-attack penalty, effects, Directed, Black Digizoid Armor, stat swaps, Dark Evolution, clash block, counterattack half, 3-dice fallbacks) and also returns the modifier breakdown, `isDodgeBlocked` and `summarizeDodgeResult` (the target's latest `Dodge` log entry). The single modal's `dodgeDicePool`/`dodgeBlockedByClash` now call it. New `composables/useAreaDefenseBatch.ts` (adapter-injected) holds one entry per target — `idle → rolled → submitting → submitted`, plus `failed` and `unavailable` — with its own pool, roll, Act of Inspiration choice and status. Rules: a roll is kept and cannot be rolled again for free; re-rolls, +1 die and Act of Inspiration happen only **after** the server accepted the Inspiration spend (the old `RollInterceptPanel` changes the dice whether or not the spend worked), an Act is paid once per target, and all spends are made one at a time so two targets cannot both take the last point; a failed send keeps its roll and resends exactly that roll (no re-roll, no spend), a submitted defense never changes, double clicks are guarded; `reconcile` follows each encounter update — rolls survive, a protected/removed target or a vanished request becomes `unavailable` (nothing sent), a request the server already answered (`answeredAt`/stored response) counts as submitted, late requests of the same cast join. New `components/AreaDefensePanel.vue`: a card per target (pool, modifier chips, Act of Inspiration, Roll, dice, Re-roll/+1 Die, per-target Submit/Retry, status, result) plus **Roll all unrolled** and **Submit ready defenses** (defense order, partial failure tolerated, failed ones retried by the next press). `player/[tamerId].vue` opens it when two or more unanswered dodge requests of one cast are waiting, keeps those requests off the one-at-a-time modal, closes it five seconds after the last defense is in (or on Close, only once everything is settled) through `useAutoDismiss` and the shared `cleanupDodgeExchange` for every request/response, and `handleSpendInspiration` now returns whether the spend went through. **Server:** `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup` adds `intercedeGroupId` to the dodge-roll requests it creates after the intercede window (the no-offer area path already had it); order, NPC auto-resolution and the window are unchanged. Per-target idempotency is Workflow 1's `answeredAt` + queue. No migration. Added `tests/areaDefenseBatch.test.mjs` (33 checks). |
| 2026-11-07c | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **"Pass until my next turn" for interceding (players only).** A player can stop being asked to intercede for the rest of the turn cycle without opting out target by target. New `POST /api/encounters/[id]/actions/intercede-pass` (`{ ownerId, action: 'start' | 'resume' }`) runs in `serializeIntercedeClaim`: `start` stores `intercede_opt_outs.temporaryPasses[ownerId] = { characterId, turnsStarted, createdAt }` under the row lock (`setTemporaryPass`; combat only, the owner's Tamer must be in the fight and not in reserve), then `applyActivePasses` answers every offer already waiting through the normal Skip flow; `resume` clears it. New `resolvePassedOffers` (`intercedeDecision.ts`) removes the owner's **whole** offer (unlike an opt-out, an area offer is not kept open for other targets) and calls the same `resolveIntercedeGroupIfDecided` a Skip does, so single-target, area, NPC and support attacks continue identically (the tests compare the outcome with a hand-clicked Skip). New offers are covered too: `intercede-offer.post.ts` is now a thin wrapper over `createIntercedeOffers` and, when the row it returns has a passing owner with a waiting offer, applies passes (queued) before the attacker's response goes back. **Expiry is derived, not scheduled:** every turn start bumps `CombatParticipant.turnsStarted` through `recordTurnStart` (client `activateParticipant`, server `startTurn` for knockouts, the legacy `npc-attack` activation — a static test guards that each `applyDeferredIntercedeCost` call is paired with one) and a pass is in force only while its Tamer's counter equals the stored one (`getActiveTemporaryPass`, `utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts`). So the Tamer's next real turn ends it, including one started by a knockout, a round wrap or removing the active participant, while a reorder, an added participant or an unrelated removal cannot; a removed or reserved Tamer, or a phase other than `combat`, voids it. The PUT never lowers a stored `turnsStarted` (a stale client copy cannot revive an expired pass) and clears all passes once the resulting phase is not `combat`; a **fresh** duplicate drops passes, a **snapshot** copy keeps them with the counters. Permanent opt-outs (`targets`, `gmCharacters`) are never written by a pass and survive its start, expiry and resume. **Deliberate scope choice:** an offer that carries a usable Quick Reaction (`data.canUseQuickReaction`) is left standing — Quick Reaction is a once-per-day order the GM applies for the player, not interceding. Client: new `composables/useIntercedePass.ts` (state derived from the synced encounter; double-click guard), `components/IntercedePassNotice.vue` ("Passing on interceding until your next turn" + **Resume prompting**) in the card-view turn header, the map-mode toolbar and the Intercede Options dialog, and a **Pass until my next turn** button beside Skip / Skip All in all three prompt views of `player/[tamerId].vue`; its offer watcher now uses `isOfferSuppressed` (permanent opt-out or pass) and skips quietly. The GM page has no pass. No migration (jsonb keys). Added `tests/temporaryIntercedePass.test.mjs` (34 checks). |
| 2026-11-07b | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Combat actions apply the encounter they return instead of reloading the whole page.** Roughly 30 `loadData()` calls on `player/[tamerId].vue` (each one 6+ requests) and ~18 `fetchEncounter()` calls on `encounters/[id].vue` now apply the encounter the action endpoint already returned. New pure `utils/encounterSnapshot.ts`: `createEncounterSnapshotGate()` drops any snapshot whose `updatedAt` is older than one already applied (pushes also keep the monotonic WS `version` guard; equal stamps pass), so a slow action response can no longer overwrite a newer push; `shapeActionResponse` gives a raw row the push shape (log trimmed to `BATTLE_LOG_WINDOW` = 50 with `_battleLogTotal`, legacy `actionsRemaining` migrated, and the page's live `participantPositions`/`destructibleStates`/`version` kept because the row lags the room); `needsFullReload` hands off to `loadData()` when the encounter was unpublished, ended or no longer involves the player, so access and encounter selection still go through the full load. New pure `utils/combatRecords.ts`: `planRecordRefresh(previous, next, known, hint)` returns which Digimon ids, Tamer ids, evolution lines and partner list an update touched (a new entity id, a devolve to a held form, a same-id stat edit, a Tamer-rewriting action); `mergeRecordsById` keeps unchanged records by identity. Added `tests/encounterSnapshot.test.mjs` (31 checks). |
| 2026-11-07 | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Completed combat results dismiss themselves after five seconds, and a dodge can only be answered once.** New `composables/useAutoDismiss.ts`: `useAutoDismiss({ key, cleanup, delayMs = 5000 })` starts a timer when `key` becomes non-null, restarts it when the key changes, cancels it when the result goes away, on `dismiss()` and on scope disposal. `dismiss()` is the only path to `cleanup` and ignores a second call while one is in flight, so a Close click that races the timer cleans up once. `player/[tamerId].vue` wires one instance per finished result (Attack Result group, Dodge Result, Intercede Result, and the clash-check result once it is no longer PENDING); prompts awaiting a decision (dodge/counterattack/intercede/Divine Protection, accuracy review) have no key and never time out. The three Close buttons call the same `dismiss()`. Shared `cleanupDodgeExchange(requestId, responseId)` cancels the request, then deletes the response, tolerating 404s; the attack result group now does this for every result in the group (it used to delete only the response, which let the defender's dodge prompt reopen) and the dodge result uses it too. `dismissedResponseIds` stops a stale push rebuilding a result that was already seen. Dismissal never writes the battle log, so every result stays there (Map Mode's log; the card view still has none). **Server:** `responses.post.ts` now runs inside `serializeIntercedeClaim` (same per-encounter queue as the intercede decisions), stamps `request.answeredAt` on the first `dodge-rolled`, and returns the encounter unchanged for a repeat (stamp set or a stored response exists), so a double click, retry or second tab cannot apply the hit twice, even after the response was deleted. New pure `utils/requestState.ts` `isRequestAnswered` is shared by server and player page; `hasUnrespondedDodgeRequest` uses it and the GM pending-request list shows "Answered" instead of Roll Dodge. `pendingRequests[].answeredAt` is a JSONB type addition only (no migration). Added `tests/autoDismiss.test.mjs` (15 checks: timer start/reset/cancel, decision dialogs excluded, click-vs-timer race, real page cleanup functions, page wiring, the real handler's repeat and overlapping submissions). |

It now holds up to 10 tabs (`+` adds one, 🗑 removes the open one), so scenes can be prepared before a session. **Show** puts the open tab up for everyone; Clear still hides the live scene; Cancel became Close because tab edits autosave. On the fullscreen view the GM gets large ◀ ▶ buttons that put the previous/next tab with an image up for everyone the same way; players never see them. They step from the tab last shown (`cursor`), not the one open in the editor, skip empty tabs, don't wrap, and keep their place after Clear. **Storage:** new `campaigns.scenePresets` jsonb (migration `0022_add_scene_presets.sql`, **to be applied to Railway before deploying**, since every `select()` on `campaigns` reads all columns) and new `GET`/`PUT /api/campaigns/[id]/scene-presets`. The PUT takes at most 10 entries (otherwise 400), coerces each to `{imageUrl, caption}` strings truncated to 2048/300, doesn't trim, and doesn't bump `updatedAt`. **Not broadcast and not in `GET /scene`:** the `campaign:{id}` room includes players, so only GMs fetch the tabs, on campaign load and whenever the scene is opened (picking up a co-GM's or another device's edits). **Client (`ScenePanel.vue`):** 600 ms debounced autosave, flushed on Show, on closing the editor and before a campaign switch; one PUT in flight at a time; a refetch never overwrites unconfirmed edits; a campaign with no saved tabs starts tab 1 from the live scene; a `scene-updated` broadcast re-syncs the arrows' cursor to whichever tab is live. `ScenePreset`/`MAX_SCENE_PRESETS` added to `types/index.ts`. | 2026-11-03o | API Schema, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Fix: enemies knocked out outside an attack now leave the encounter.** Before this, an enemy at 0 Wound Boxes was removed only when an attack resolved by `resolveNpcAttack` (or the legacy `npc-attack.post`) knocked it out, or when the GM edited its wounds. Poison and Burn (turn end, round end, the round wrap after a defeated active NPC, Burn on a move or a Knockback/Pull), a Clash Throw, the hit an NPC interceptor takes, Digizoid Armor reflect and end-of-turn falls all left it on the board. **New `server/utils/enemyKnockouts.ts`:** `knockedOutEnemyIds(participants)` and `resolveEnemyKnockouts(state, candidateIds, ctx)`, which treat each knocked-out enemy the way an attack knockout always has: it devolves if it digivolved during combat, triggers an unused [Time Control], or is removed from `participants` and `turnOrder` (its clash partner freed, "was defeated and removed from the encounter!" logged). Removing the active participant ends its turn and starts the next one's, wrapping the round past the last slot, and an enemy knocked out by that round end or by the turn change's fall is resolved the same way. Callers pass only the enemies their own request knocked out, so a downed enemy the GM leaves on the board stays. `resolveNpcAttack`'s defeat block, round reset, turn activation and Time Control moved into this module (same return contract). **Wired into:** the encounter PUT, `/actions/move` (the response gains `defeated`), the Clash Throw, `intercede-claim`, `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup`, the `responses` dodge path, `resolveInstantSupportEffect` (which now returns `nextTurnIndex`/`nextRound`, saved by `intercede-offer`'s three instant branches and `intercedeDecision`) and `npc-attack.post`; each saves `turnOrder`, `currentTurnIndex` and `round` together. **Client:** `EncounterMap.vue` cancels a Charge or `[Pass]` attack when Burn on the move knocked the attacker out. **Side effects:** an attacker that Digizoid Armor reflect knocks out inside `resolveNpcAttack` is removed too; the Time Control log line names the boss (it said "Digimon"); and `intercede-offer`'s area loop now gives each target's `resolveNpcAttack` the turn index an earlier target's removal shifted (it passed the stale one). Verified offline with the jiti fake-db harness: 74 checks (the helper on its own; the PUT at a round end, an EddySoul turn end and a fall; `/actions/move` mid-order and last in the order; Clash Throw; Knockback directly and through `intercede-offer`; both intercede paths and the area group; the `responses` reflect; `resolveNpcAttack` regressions; `npc-attack.post`), plus the 42 Poison, 61 Burn, 12 counterattack and 22 standing-still Burn checks re-run. `nuxi typecheck` is unchanged at the 210-error baseline, and `npm run build` passes. |

| 2026-11-03m | Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Burn's standing-still damage is now reduced by Armor (minimum 1); a new house rule keeps it flat.** This changes the 2026-11-03j default: a burned digimon that doesn't move all round takes its Stage Bonus × 2 at the end of the round, reduced by its Armor (`getBurnArmor`, the same Armor as movement Burn) to a minimum of 1. A Fresh or In-Training digimon (Stage Bonus 0) still takes nothing, and EddySoul `burnPerTwoSpacesMoved` still deals nothing for standing still. The log line notes the Armor ("2 Burn damage (didn't move this round: Stage Bonus × 2, 2 Armor)"). New `HouseRules.burnStationaryIgnoresArmor` (`types/index.ts`; campaign settings → House Rules → "Burn: Standing-Still Damage Ignores Armor") restores the flat Stage Bonus × 2. **Rules plumbing (`server/utils/damageOverTime.ts`):** the now-exported `DamageOverTimeRules` type spans the two EddySoul flags and the house rule, built by the new `damageOverTimeRules(rulesSettings)`. `loadCampaignEddySoulRules` is now `loadCampaignDamageOverTimeRules`, `loadEncounterRules` returns `rules` (was `eddySoulRules`), and `loadDamageOverTimeRules` / `loadRoundEndContext` return the same type; their callers (`move.post`, `clash-action.post`, `pushPull.ts`) follow. | 2026-11-03l | Blast Radius | **Fix: a counterattack that defeated the enemy who attacked left it in the turn order.** When an auto-resolved (NPC) counterattack knocks out the original attacker, `resolveNpcAttack` removes that enemy and returns the shortened `turnOrder` with `nextTurnIndex`/`nextRound` (the next participant activated, or the round wrapped). `triggerCounterattack` passed on the index and round but dropped the `turnOrder`, so its callers saved the new index against the old order: the saved order still held the dead enemy and the index could point at it. `triggerCounterattack` now also returns `turnOrder`, and all four call sites save it together with the index and round: `attack.post` (auto-miss), `intercede-offer.post` (NPC damage miss) and both `responses.post` miss branches. Verified offline with the jiti fake-db harness: 12 checks (`triggerCounterattack` mid-order, last in the order and without a defeat; `attack.post` auto-miss and `intercede-offer` NPC miss end to end), 7 of which fail without the fix, plus the 42 Poison and 61 Burn checks re-run. `nuxi typecheck` is unchanged at the 210-error baseline, and `npm run build` passes. |

| 2026-11-03j | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Feature: [Burn] now deals its damage (DDA 1.4 + an EddySoul variant), and Naturewalk: Fire is immune to it.** Before this, Burn only counted down. **Rules:** a burned digimon takes 1 damage per meter it moves (by choice, Knockback/Pull or being thrown), reduced by its Armor, minimum 1 per move. A burned digimon that doesn't move all round takes its Stage Bonus × 2 at the end of the round (flat; Armor doesn't reduce it). EddySoul `burnPerTwoSpacesMoved`: 1 damage per 2 spaces per move (rounded down), ignoring Armor, and nothing for standing still. Tamers take none. **Burn needs a map:** without one there's no movement data, so Burn only counts down. **`server/utils/damageOverTime.ts`** gains `getBurnArmor` (the target-Armor terms of `computeAttackDamage` minus the attack-only ones), `burnMovementDamage`, `applyMovementBurn` (stamps the new participant field `lastMovedRound` on every mover, applies Burn through `applyWoundDamage`, returns log entries + `damageById`), `movesFromPositions`, `isImmuneToEffect`, `loadStationaryBurnPositions`, `loadRoundEndContext` and `loadCampaignEddySoulRules`. `applyRoundEndEffects` takes `positions` and applies the stationary penalty (core rule, positioned digimon, `lastMovedRound !== endingRound`) before the countdown, and `loadEncounterRules` also returns `mapId`. **Movement hooks:** `POST /actions/move` takes `distance` (path steps) and `noActionCost`. `EncounterMap.vue` sends the distance for every combat move, and now also posts Charge (before/after) and `[Pass]` moves with `noActionCost: true`, awaiting the post before the Charge-before / Pass attack so the two server writes can't overwrite each other. Knockback/Pull use a new `computePushPull` `burn` context: `resolveNpcAttack` passes its round and lazily loaded rules, and `applyPushPullDisplacement` loads them via `loadEncounterRules`. Clash Throw counts the cells flown before the fall and adds the Burn damage to the throw's log line. Intercede claims count the interceptor's repositioning and the target's displacement or throw (diffed via `movesFromPositions`), applied before the hit. GM free-move, placement and gravity falls don't count. **Round ends:** the PUT (`loadStationaryBurnPositions`), `npc-attack.post`, and `resolveNpcAttack`'s wrap all apply the stationary penalty. `resolveNpcAttack` uses its positions merged with its push/pull patch, or the room's via `loadRoundEndContext`, so area-intercede and counterattack wraps apply it too. **PUT** keeps the stored `lastMovedRound` when a client copy's is older, so a PUT sent before the move's broadcast arrives can't cause a false penalty; the phase→'combat' transition clears it. **Naturewalk: Fire** blocks Burn in `computeAttackDamage` (NPC auto-resolve, intercede, area groups), both `responses.post` effect paths and the legacy `npc-attack.post` damage path. New settings toggle "Burn: 1 Damage per 2 Spaces Moved". **Known limits:** enemies knocked out by Poison/Burn aren't auto-removed. A partner knocked out by movement Burn outside an attack (a map move, a Clash Throw, an area-intercede throw) devolves at the next save that runs the auto-devolve check, as fall damage does. GM Quick Apply can still put Burn on a Naturewalk: Fire digimon. Verified offline with the jiti fake-db harness: 61 checks (the helpers, `/actions/move`, the PUT round end under both rules, Knockback, Clash Throw, Naturewalk, `resolveNpcAttack` wraps with and without a map, combat start), plus the 42 Poison checks re-run. `nuxi typecheck` is unchanged at the 210-error baseline, and `npm run build` passes. |
| 2026-11-03i | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius | **Fix: Poison never hurt a partner digimon, and a partner digimon's effects never counted down; Poison now ticks at the end of the round (new EddySoul toggle: at the end of the poisoned unit's turn).** Cause: a partner digimon grouped under its Tamer has no `turnOrder` slot, and `nextTurn()` ran end-of-turn processing (Poison damage + every effect's duration countdown) only for the slot owner — so a grouped partner never took Poison damage and its Stun/Weaken/Fury/etc. never expired (since `1a9fbcd`, the 2026-04-14 row). DDA 1.4 Poison is unalterable Wound Box damage equal to the target's CPU **at the end of each round** (min 3 rounds), which supersedes the 2026-04-14 per-turn timing for Poison. **Poison/Burn now run entirely server-side** in new `server/utils/damageOverTime.ts`, and each tick happens together with its countdown, so a 3-round Poison ticks exactly 3 times under either rule: `applyWoundDamage` (temp wounds first, an emptied Shield removed — same rule as `resolveNpcAttack`), `applyTurnEndEffects` (EddySoul `poisonTicksAtTurnEnd` only: the unit whose turn ended plus, for a Tamer, its grouped partners — not in `turnOrder`, not in reserve), `applyRoundEndEffects` (Poison for every non-reserved participant unless that toggle is on; counts Burn down), `loadEncounterRules`/`loadDamageOverTimeRules`. Poison damage is the digimon's **current** CPU (`getDigimonDerivedStats`), so Quick-Applied Poison (no stored potency) and a digimon that digivolved since being poisoned use the right value; Tamers take none (their Poison still counts down). Each tick writes a named battle-log entry (the old client entry logged the raw participant id). New `DAMAGE_OVER_TIME_EFFECTS` (`data/attackConstants.ts`) is skipped by the client's per-turn countdown. **Hooks:** `[id].put.ts` detects a **turn end** (active participant id changed, or the round advanced, and the outgoing participant is still present — `rebuildTurnOrder()` re-anchors on the active participant, so adding/re-sorting participants or removing someone else doesn't count) and a **round end** (`round` increases while `phase === 'combat'`, round ≥ 1), runs turn-end then round-end effects after end-of-turn gravity and **before** the auto-devolve loop (a Poison KO devolves in the same request), and `getCampaignRules()` now also returns `eddySoulRules`. The defeated-active-NPC round wraps in `resolveNpcAttack.ts` and `npc-attack.post.ts` call `applyRoundEndEffects` before their inline reset (parity with `applyRoundStartQualityTriggers`). `resolveNpcAttack` gains optional `encounterId` (loads the EddySoul rules lazily, only when someone has Poison/Burn), passed by every caller, including through `triggerCounterattack` (new optional `encounterId`). **Client:** `useEncounters.ts` `nextTurn()` no longer applies Poison; the countdown moved into module-level `tickTurnEffects()` (skips permanent and damage-over-time effects), and at a Tamer's turn end it also runs for each grouped partner digimon — after the Stun → `maxPostTurnIntercedes` check, so the post-turn intercede cap still sees this round's Stun. **EddySoul toggle** `poisonTicksAtTurnEnd` added to `EddySoulRules` (`types/index.ts`) and the campaign settings page. Verified offline with a jiti fake-db harness (42 checks) running the real `nextTurn()`/`removeParticipant()`/`addParticipant()` through the real PUT handler, plus the `resolveNpcAttack` wrap; `nuxi typecheck` shows no new errors. |

| 2026-11-03g | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius | **Fix: "Never intercede" opt-outs didn't stick, and queued prompts kept coming; Intercede Options/Targets added to both map views.** Four causes. **(1) Queued prompts were never cleared** — the popup's Never removed only the offer clicked, so a second attack already queued on the same target prompted again at once; the player's batch save only skipped offers whose targets were *all* opted out (mixed area offers kept listing opted-out targets) and the DM's batch save cleared nothing. **(2) Opt-outs were silently reverted** — they lived on the participant rows inside `participants`, which nearly every combat endpoint (and client PUTs) rewrites wholesale from a copy read at request start, so two people answering at once, or the GM attacking while a player clicked Never, put the old list back (the same lost-update race `guidingLight.ts` documents). b7d4b0e had only fixed the save itself. **(3) The DM popup's "Never Intercede" couldn't stop prompts** — it edited a per-character checklist and the server only suppressed the GM offer when *every* tamer/digimon in the encounter was on it, though the checklist only ever listed characters with actions left (and never that attack's attacker). **(4) The player's area-attack "Never intercede for X" opted out of every target in the blast** and dropped the whole offer. **Fix:** new column `encounters.intercede_opt_outs` (jsonb, migration `0021_add_intercede_opt_outs.sql` — **must be applied before deploying**, drizzle selects every schema column) holding `{ targets: { [tamerEntityId or 'GM']: targetParticipantId[] }, gmCharacters: { [targetParticipantId]: participantId[] } }`; only `server/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts` writes it, in a `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` transaction, and `[id].put.ts` drops it from the body. Shared pure readers in `app/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts` (`getTargetOptOuts`, `getGmCharacterOptOuts`, `getOfferTargetIds`, `isOfferFullyOptedOut`) fall back to the old participant-level `intercedeOptOuts` / `gm`-row `gmCharacterOptOuts` until an owner saves again, so live encounters keep their settings (no backfill). The skip → resolve-if-last block moved out of `intercede-skip.post.ts` into new `server/utils/intercedeDecision.ts` (`resolveIntercedeGroupIfDecided`, `pruneOptedOutOffers`, plus `loadIntercedeDecisionContext`/`initialDecisionState`/`persistDecisionState`, which now only writes participants/battleLog/turnOrder when an attack actually resolved). Both opt-out paths prune every pending offer for that owner in the same request: single-target offers for an opted-out target are dropped (resolving the attack if it was the last decision), area offers lose just those targets. `intercede-skip` gains `optOutTargetIds` (per-target; falls back to the offer's targets); `intercede-optouts` takes `{ ownerId, intercedeOptOuts }` (legacy `participantId` still mapped). `intercede-offer.post.ts` reads through the helpers, and the GM checklist now suppresses the offer when every character the GM could actually pick (`hasEligibleInterceptor`, same rules as `getGmIntercedeOptions`) is on it. UI: DM popup gets a per-target **Never intercede for {target}** (single-target, and area once a target is chosen; `handleGmIntercedeOptOut`) and the checklist button is relabeled **Choose characters…** (auto-skips the prompt when no options remain); player area Never sends only the chosen target; both pages auto-skip a prompt whose targets are all opted out (DM via a de-duplicated `autoSkipGmOffer`); the player's client-side skip loop is gone. **Intercede Options** (player, `openIntercedeOptions`) / **Intercede Targets** (DM, `openGmIntercedeTargets`) now also sit just left of ✕ Close Map in each map view. `duplicate.post.ts` copies the column. Verified offline with a jiti fake-db harness (34 checks) plus `nuxi typecheck` (no new errors) and `npm run build`. |
| 2026-11-03f | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Line of sight for single-target ranged attacks + door open/close.** Single-target ranged reticules (`MapCanvas.vue` `updateReticules()`) only checked 3D distance, so a target behind a wall, floor or voxel could still be outlined and clicked. Area shapes did check LoS, but with a Three.js raycast over every `THREE.Mesh` in `buildMeshes`: wireframe space tiles and open door panels counted as solid, and every voxel blocked regardless of material. New pure `app/utils/lineOfSight.ts` is now the single LoS model for both. `buildLosIndex(map, destroyedIds)` turns floors, ceilings, walls (window panes solid until broken; an open doorway clear while its jambs + header stay solid), closed doors and every voxel `voxelBlocksTargeting()` flags into AABBs bucketed per grid cell. `losClear`/`losClip` walk the cells along a line (3D DDA) and slab-test each box: 0.1 trimmed at each end; containment counts, so a unit inside a blocking voxel (e.g. darkness) can neither be targeted nor target out; boxes grown by 1e-4 so seam-aligned lines can't slip between neighbours. `canSeeFootprint` (attacker footprint centre → target footprint centre or any target cell) gates the ranged reticule, and MapCanvas's `hasLineOfSight`/`clipToLos` are now thin wrappers, so AoE previews use the same blockers. Geometry constants live in `MAP_GEOMETRY` there; MapCanvas builds its meshes from them. `mapVoxels.ts`: new `voxelBlocksTargeting(v) = blocksMovement \|\| blocksSight`, so only water and air (wind) let targeting into/through them; Light now defaults to `blocksSightDefault: true`. Stairs and space tiles never block. **Doors:** new pure `app/utils/mapDoors.ts` (`wallFaceKey`, `doorsAdjacentTo`, `closedDoorsCrossedByPath`). The NPC and player radials show `Open Door`/`Close Door` (free action, never disabled) for each door on a side of the unit's footprint cells; hovering tints the door, clicking emits new `door-toggle(doorId, isOpen)`. `EncounterMap.vue`'s `setDoorsOpen()` updates `map.value.doors`, saves via `PUT /api/maps/[mapId]`, then sends `door-toggled` (other clients already refetch the map on it). Moving through a closed door opens it: `onCombatMove` (drawn path, re-derived by `movePathTo` if it doesn't end at the destination), Charge (`onChargeTargetSelected`) and `[Pass]` (`segmentCells` along the line); GM Move, placement and server-side displacement don't. Open doors render swung 90° on their hinge into the wall's own cell. The GM's Door tool on an existing door now toggles it (`useMapEditor.applyDoor`). Reticules also refresh on any position change and every scene rebuild. Removed the unused `hasLineOfSight` copies from `utils/mapGeometry.ts` and `server/utils/gridDistance.ts`. Map targeting only: the server and the GM's list target picker don't check LoS. No DB schema change. |

Being real Armor, Armor Piercing/Tank Buster/attacker Finesse now reduce it, and the battle-log Target Armor/Effective Armor/Formula include it. Dodge/Intercede battle-log entries gain optional `clashArmorBonus` (schema `battleLog` jsonb type + `useEncounters` `BattleLogEntry`) and an effects note `Clash: +N Armor (CPU)` (shown by `MapBattleLog`); the GM card battle-log breakdown in `encounters/[id].vue` shows an "incl. Clash CPU +N" row under Target Armor. Also implements the Selective Targeting clause ("In Clash targeting, enemy only gets own CPU to Armor") that the 2026-06-15 entry listed as unimplemented. | 2026-09-29b | API Schema, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius | **Follow-up: `[Guiding Light]` now recomputes on server-driven displacement too** (Clash Throw, Knockback/Pull, gravity, all three intercede flows), closing the "known gap" the same-day entry below left open. All 8 `broadcastPositionPatch()` call sites (`server/utils/encounterRoom.ts:163`) needed a recompute, but a naive `recomputeGuidingLightForEncounter()` bolt-on (the standalone read-modify-write-broadcast helper `ws.ts` uses) would have been unsafe at half of them — several sit inside a POST handler that does its own later `db.update(encounters).set({ participants })` from an in-memory array computed *before* the broadcast, so an independent recompute write there would just get clobbered by that later write. Three fix shapes, by call site: **(A) safe to bolt on as-is** — `clash-action.post.ts` (Clash Throw) and both branches of `intercede-claim.post.ts`, where `participants` is already persisted *before* the broadcast and nothing writes it again afterward; each gets `await recomputeGuidingLightForEncounter(encounterId).catch(...)` right after `broadcastPositionPatch`, same one-liner `ws.ts` already uses. **(B) fold into the in-memory array before the handler's own later write** — `intercede-offer.post.ts` (both call sites) and `intercede-skip.post.ts`, where the broadcast happens *before* that handler's final persist using the same in-scope `participants`/`result.participants`/`updateData.participants`; each now calls `applyGuidingLightAuras(participants, await getRoomPositions(encounterId), campaignLevel, houseRules)` directly (the pure function, not the wrapper) and reassigns the local variable, so the existing later write picks it up — `intercede-skip.post.ts` needed one added line (`campaignLevel = campaign.level`) since it previously only pulled `houseRules` out of its campaign lookup. **(C) thread through the shared utility itself** — `server/utils/endOfTurnGravity.ts`'s `applyEndOfTurnGravity` and `server/utils/pushPull.ts`'s `applyPushPullDisplacement`/`resolveInstantSupportEffect` broadcast internally but never persist (every caller gets `participants` back and persists it later), so both gained new optional `campaignLevel`/`houseRules` params and now fold the aura recompute into their *returned* `participants` right after their internal broadcast — every caller's existing "receive participants, persist later" flow picks it up with no further change needed at the call site itself, beyond passing the two new params through (`[id].put.ts` hoists its existing `getCampaignRules()` closure above the gravity call to supply them; `npc-attack.post.ts` and `responses.post.ts` gained a `campaignLevel` alongside their existing `houseRules` campaign lookups to supply `applyPushPullDisplacement`'s two direct call sites and `resolveInstantSupportEffect`'s three). One correctness note this surfaced: `applyEndOfTurnGravity`'s docstring previously called out that it *mutates* `participants` in place (true for wound/fall mutations) and callers never bothered reassigning the returned `participants` — that stopped being safe once the aura fold-in can return a *new* array reference (`applyGuidingLightAuras` only mutates on change, otherwise returns the same reference untouched), so `[id].put.ts` and `npc-attack.post.ts` now both capture `gravity.participants`/`npcGravity.participants` back into their local variable, where they previously discarded it and relied on in-place mutation alone. `resolveNpcAttack.ts`'s own internal Knockback/Pull and gravity handling (it calls the *pure* `computePushPull`/`computeGravityDrops` cores directly, not these wrappers) needed no separate fix — its results merge into the same `positionPatch` field that its callers already broadcast at Category A/B sites, so the recompute added there covers it for free. |
| 2026-09-29 | API Schema, Dependency Graph, Blast Radius, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **The `[Guiding Light]` special order (Passive — "+2 Accuracy to allies in burst radius, +1 Dodge per ally in radius") had no mechanical implementation, same class of gap as `[Challenger]` before it: it only existed as descriptive text in `special-orders.ts`, and `special-order.post.ts`'s activation switch falls through to a log-only "GM resolves manually" default for every Passive it doesn't special-case.** Unlike Challenger (a one-shot combat-start grant), Guiding Light's radius depends on live map position, so it needed to be a recomputed aura rather than a single trigger. New `server/utils/guidingLight.ts`: `applyGuidingLightAuras(participants, positions, campaignLevel, houseRules)` is the pure recompute — for every non-reserve Digimon participant whose partner Tamer has unlocked Guiding Light (`getUnlockedSpecialOrders`), finds allies (`isEnemy` match, non-reserve, positioned) within Chebyshev distance `1 + BIT + 1` of it (the same `[Burst]` radius formula as `computeBurstCells`), applies a `'Guiding Light'` buff (`+2 Accuracy`, new `EFFECT_STAT_MODIFIERS` entry) to each ally in range, and a `'Guiding Light (Focus)'` buff (`+1 Dodge`, potency = ally count) to itself. Both effect names were added to `PERMANENT_EFFECTS` (no duration decay) precisely so the recompute is the only thing that adds/removes them — `applyEffectToParticipant`'s permanent-effect branch replaces any existing same-named instance, so overlapping auras on one target take a "first source reached wins" resolution (not stacked, matching how Shield/Demoralize already behave) rather than an arbitrary implementation choice. `applyAura`/`removeAura` helpers do a value-equality check before touching `activeEffects`, so a recompute that changes nothing returns the same array reference — this idempotency matters because the aura is invoked on every map move, not just combat start. Wiring, two integration points: (1) `[id].put.ts` runs one pass at the exact `phase !== 'combat' → 'combat'` transition (alongside the existing Challenger `applyEncounterStartTriggers` call), using room-snapshot positions, so allies already in radius are buffed before anyone has to move; (2) `server/api/encounters/[id]/ws.ts`'s `unit-moved` handler fires `recomputeGuidingLightForEncounter(encounterId)` (fire-and-forget, not awaited, so it can't add latency to the position broadcast) after every live drag-move — this second entry point re-reads the encounter row + `getRoomPositions`, and only if something actually changed does it persist `participants` and broadcast a fresh `encounter-state` message itself (reusing `buildEncounterPayload`/`broadcast`, the same shape `encounter-sync.ts` middleware sends for REST mutations — necessary because `unit-moved` is a raw WS message, not an HTTP request, so the middleware never sees it). Server-driven displacement (Clash Throw, Knockback/Pull, gravity, intercede) did not yet trigger a recompute as of this entry — closed the same day, see the entry below. |
| 2026-08-20 | API Schema, Data Models & Storage, Pages & Components, Dependency Graph, Cross-Cutting Concerns | **Redesign: campaign access control is now character-centric (per-tamer grants + a public-access flag), replacing the old account-level "all tamers" / "one specific tamer" scope.** User feedback: the Settings "Account Access" list felt clunky — one row per account mixing DM role and player scope, and an account could only ever be scoped to exactly one tamer. New model: `campaignAccessGrants` keeps only DM-role grants (co-dm/co-owner); a new many-to-many `tamerAccessGrants` table (`tamer_access_grants`, migration `0020_add_tamer_access_grants.sql`) grants individual accounts access to individual tamers (many accounts per tamer, many tamers per account); a new `tamers.publicAccess` boolean (default **true**) opens a tamer to anyone with campaign access, including password-only sessions with no account. This is a real restriction, not additive — a tamer with no grants and `publicAccess: false` is DM-tier-only. Defaulting `publicAccess` true (for existing and new tamers) was a deliberate compatibility choice so nothing broke on deploy; DMs opt individual tamers into restriction afterward. Migration backfills any pre-existing `playerScope: 'specific'` grant into `tamerAccessGrants` before dropping `campaign_access_grants.player_scope`/`player_tamer_id`; the old `'all tamers'` scope had no migration target and was dropped outright (approved). New endpoints `GET`/`POST /api/campaigns/[id]/tamer-grants`, `DELETE .../tamer-grants/[grantId]`, `PUT .../tamers/[tamerId]/public-access`, all owner/co-owner-gated like the existing `grants/*`. `getMyCampaignAccess()`'s `MyCampaignAccessResult` drops `playerScope`/`playerTamerId` for `accessibleTamerIds: string[]`, consumed by `campaign-access.ts` middleware, `pages/index.vue`, `player/index.vue`'s `visibleTamers`, and `player/[tamerId].vue`'s `enforceSpecificTamerScope` (now fetches the tamer directly in parallel with `my-access`, since it runs before the page's own load populates it). Settings' Account Access section split into two subsections matching the requested layout — **DM Access** (unchanged grant list, player-scope UI removed) above **Character Access** (every tamer, its public checkbox, and its own per-account grant list) — sharing a new extracted `components/AccountSearchInput.vue` for the username-search "Add Account" flow instead of two copies of the same inline block. |
| 2026-08-20 | Pages & Components | **Fix: Scene's "My Character" button didn't show for a character reached without going through the hub's selection click** (common for unowned, shared-pool characters with no account tying them to a browser — most campaigns in practice). `ScenePanel.vue`'s `myTamerId` computed relied solely on the `player-tamer-id-{campaignId}` cookie `player/index.vue` sets on hub selection; a player landing directly on `/player/[tamerId]` (bookmark, shared link) never sets it. Now prefers `route.params.tamerId` (present on that page and its sub-routes, no weaker an identity signal than `player/[tamerId].vue` itself uses) and falls back to the cookie only when there's no `tamerId` in the URL (hub, library, encounters, etc. — unchanged from before). Read-only fallback; nothing writes the cookie. |
