**Request/Response management (player action coordination):**

| Method | Path | Handler File | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/encounters/[id]/requests` | `encounters/[id]/requests.post.ts` | Create pending action request for a player; types: `digimon-selection`, `initiative-roll`, `dodge-roll`, `intercede-offer`, `health-roll`, `recovery-check`, `divine-protection-offer`, `throw-impact-attack`; `recovery-check` data: `{ tamerParticipantId, digimonParticipantId, rookieDigimonId }`; `divine-protection-offer` data: `{ pendingDamage, targetName, attackerName, targetParticipantId, firstUse, inspirationCost }`; `throw-impact-attack` data: `{ thrownParticipantId, thrownName, landingPos, targetIds, attackId: 'basic-ranged', accuracyBonus }` — **not created via this endpoint**; pushed directly onto `encounter.pendingRequests` by `clash-action.post.ts`'s `'throw'` branch (Clash Throw lands adjacent to 2+ opposing participants), targeted at the Controller's tamer (or `'GM'`); resolved by the player via the existing `attack` endpoint (`attackId: 'basic-ranged'`, `areaData: { targetIds }`, accuracy dice pool +`accuracyBonus`), then removed |
| DELETE | `/api/encounters/[id]/requests/[requestId]` | `encounters/[id]/requests/[requestId].delete.ts` | Remove resolved request |

> **Setup prompts are single-slot per tamer (2026-07-29).** When `requests.post.ts` creates a `digimon-selection` **or** `initiative-roll` request, it first removes every existing pending request of *those two types* with the same `targetTamerId`, plus any `requestResponses` entries pointing at them. Re-sending replaces rather than stacks. This is what makes a mis-tapped digimon/solo choice redoable: `responses.post.ts` auto-creates an `initiative-roll` the moment `digimon-selected` arrives, and with nothing ever retiring it, a freshly re-sent picker was covered on the player's screen by the leftover initiative modal (both are `Teleport`ed `fixed inset-0 z-50`, so DOM order decides). It also makes a double-click / second GM tab idempotent. Deliberately scoped to these two types — `dodge-roll`, `intercede-offer`, `clash-check` etc. are per-attack and must never be cancelled by an unrelated request.
| POST | `/api/encounters/[id]/responses` | `encounters/[id]/responses.post.ts` | Player submits response to request; `recovery-rolled` type: `{ tamerSuccesses, digimonSuccesses, tamerDiceResults, digimonDiceResults }` — server recovers wounds on tamer participant and rookie digimon (participant JSON if already at rookie, or DB record if devolved from higher stage), removes request immediately. **Divine Protection**: the `dodge-rolled` damage branch intercepts a hit on a tamer (when eligible) and creates a `divine-protection-offer` instead of applying wounds; `divine-protection-used` negates the held damage (+1 DP use, −2 Insp if not first use, sets next-turn `pendingSimpleActionPenalty`), `divine-protection-declined` applies the held `pendingDivineProtectionDamage`. **Knockback/Pull (map mode)**: after effects resolve on a hit, if `appliedEffectName` is `'Knockback'` or `'Pull'` and `encounter.mapId` is set, loads the map, calls `findPushPullLandingCell` to displace the target by `damageEffectPotency` cells (away for Knockback, toward for Pull), applies fall damage if target lands in air, then broadcasts a `position-patch` event. **Reflect knockouts (2026-11-03o):** when a hit's Digizoid Armor reflect knocks out an enemy attacker, it leaves the encounter (`resolveEnemyKnockouts`, room gravity) and, if it was the active participant, the next one's turn starts; the saved `turnOrder`/`currentTurnIndex`/`round` follow. **Answered once (2026-11-07):** the handler runs inside the per-encounter `serializeIntercedeClaim` queue; the first `dodge-rolled` stamps the request's `answeredAt`, and a repeat (stamp set, or a stored response for that request) returns the encounter unchanged, so a retry or double click cannot re-apply damage even after the response was dismissed |
| DELETE | `/api/encounters/[id]/responses/[responseId]` | `encounters/[id]/responses/[responseId].delete.ts` | Remove processed response |

> **Repeat `initiative-rolled` responses are no-ops (2026-07-29).** If `requestResponses` already holds a response for `body.requestId`, `responses.post.ts` returns the encounter unchanged. Two tabs of the same player could otherwise both submit: the GM processes the first (which cancels the request), and the second became an orphan that `processResponse` could never resolve — permanently blocking `canStartCombat`, which requires `unprocessedResponses.length === 0`. `digimon-selected` needs no such guard: it deletes its own request, so a repeat 404s on the request lookup. Belt-and-braces on the GM side, `processResponse` now `deleteResponse()`s any non-dodge response whose request is missing instead of logging and returning.

**Combat Actions — `server/api/encounters/[id]/actions/`:**

All are POST. Body always includes `encounterId` (path param) + action-specific fields.

**Intercede conflict responses (2026-11-06g):** attempting to reuse an interceptor in the same area cast still returns 409, with message "This character has already interceded for this area attack. Choose a different character." Both clients preserve the server's specific conflict message, including target-claim and spatial-validation failures.

**Intercede concurrency (2026-11-06f):** `intercede-claim` validates and saves under a transaction's encounter-row `FOR UPDATE` lock. Consumed offers, already-claimed area targets and reused area interceptors return 409. `intercede-claim`, `intercede-skip`, `intercede-optouts` and `quick-reaction` share a per-encounter queue through room updates, so a competing decision reads the preceding result before changing offers. Area auto-devolution writes use the claim transaction; claim movement/gravity patches are broadcast only after commit.

| Endpoint | Handler | Key Inputs | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attack` | `attack.post.ts` | `attackerId`, `targetId` OR `targetIds[]` (area), `attackId`, `bolster?`, `isClashAttack?` | Rolls accuracy vs target dodge, applies damage, deducts actions once; when `targetIds` provided (area attack), delegates to `intercede-offer` with all targetIds after single action deduction; handles Bolster, Lifesteal, Huge Power, Signature Move battery, Haste (Complex Action cost, blocks bolster/lifesteal); `[Pass]`-shaped area attacks (`getAreaShape`) without `[Charge Attack]` cost 2 Simple Actions (`isPassWithoutCharge` forces `actionCostSimple = Math.max(actionCostSimple, 2)`); triggers counterattack on miss; creates dodge/intercede request for player targets |
| `npc-attack` | `npc-attack.post.ts` | `attackerId`, `targetId`, `attackId`, `combatMonsterBonus?` | Full server-side attack resolution for NPC→player; rolls dice, calculates net successes, applies wounds; auto-devolves on KO; removes defeated NPCs; if defeated NPC was active turn participant, auto-advances currentTurnIndex to next (wraps and resets round if last). Before its save (2026-11-03o) it also resolves any other enemy the request knocked out (Burn on the Knockback, the wrap's Poison/Burn, the turn change's fall) via `resolveEnemyKnockouts` |
| `intercede-offer` | `intercede-offer.post.ts` | `attackId`, `targetId` (single) or `targetIds[]` (area), roll data, `areaShapeData?: AreaShapeData \| null` (area only) | Single-target: creates one intercede-offer per eligible tamer + GM. Support attacks go through intercede same as damage attacks — only exception is single-target melee self-buff (attacker===target), which resolves immediately; single-target melee additionally validates a `findClosestValidDisplacementPosition` exists for the target via `targetCapsForOffer` before offering. Area attack: deducts actions once, NPC targets auto-resolved via support or damage resolvers; for every (interceptor, target) pair computes `areaIntercedeEligibility` via `movementProfileFor(p)` (budget/caps/bodyStat) and three rules — (1) interceptor's current footprint must not overlap the AoE cells (from `computeAreaCellsFromData(areaShapeData)`), (2) `findAreaIntercedePosition` finds a reachable cell inside the AoE adjacent to the target's footprint, (3) `hasValidThrowOutOfAreaCell` confirms a valid landing cell exists outside the AoE within the interceptor's Body stat. Per-tamer offers carry `data.tamerAreaTargetIds`/`data.digimonAreaTargetIds` (filtered by `tamerOptOuts`, offer skipped if both empty); the GM offer carries `data.npcAreaEligibility: Record<npcId, targetId[]>` (filtered by `gmOptOuts`, only created if non-empty) + `data.gmAreaTargetIds` (flattened union). `body.areaShapeData` (from `MapCanvas.vue`'s `area-attack-confirmed` emit) is persisted verbatim as `data.areaShapeData` on every per-tamer `intercede-offer` request, the GM request, AND the `intercede-group-state` request — `intercede-claim.post.ts` re-derives AoE cells from it for the unified reposition+throw branch. Fallback when no eligible interceptors exist (single-target or per-AOE-target): a player target hit by a positive support effect (buff) resolves via resolvePositiveAuto/resolvePositiveHealth (health-roll) instead of a dodge-roll; negative support/damage attacks still create a dodge-roll. Every per-target `dodge-roll` request created for an area attack carries `data.intercedeGroupId` (the cast's `intercede-${Date.now()}-area` id), letting the player page group all of one AoE cast's results into a single Attack Result popup. **(2026-11-03c)** No longer accepts or forwards `outsideClashCpuPenalty` — outside-clash CPU armor is computed at damage time by `getOutsideClashArmorBonus` (Glossary → Outside-clash armor). **(2026-11-03o)** The three `'instant'` support branches (area loop, melee self-buff, single NPC target) pass `currentTurnIndex` to `resolveInstantSupportEffect` and save the `nextTurnIndex`/`nextRound` it returns when Burn on the Knockback/Pull knocked out an enemy target. The area loop also gives each target's `resolveNpcAttack` the index an earlier target's removal shifted (it passed the stale one). **Passes (2026-11-07c):** the exported handler wraps `createIntercedeOffers` (the previous body, unchanged); if the returned row has an active "Pass until my next turn" owner with a waiting offer it runs `applyActivePasses` inside `serializeIntercedeClaim` and returns the re-read row merged over the original result (so `selectiveTargetingExcludedIds` survives). **Cast id on area dodges (2026-11-07d):** every `dodge-roll` an area attack creates for a player — here on the no-offer path, and in `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup` once the intercede window closes — carries `data.intercedeGroupId`; the player page defends all of one player's requests from one cast in a single panel |
| `intercede-claim` | `intercede-claim.post.ts` | `requestId`, `interceptorParticipantId`, `chosenTargetId?` (area only), `throwAllyLandingPos?: Vec3` (required for area attacks) | Player steps in to take hit for ally; area attacks: 409 if target already covered (`isAreaTargetCovered`), strips chosen target from other group requests (`tamerAreaTargetIds`/`digimonAreaTargetIds` or `npcAreaEligibility`/`gmAreaTargetIds`, removing offers left with no eligible targets), creates dodge-rolls for any uncovered remaining targets. Map is now always loaded (`claimMapRecord`) when the encounter has a map. Every area-attack claim is a unified reposition+throw: 400 if `throwAllyLandingPos` missing ('Choose a valid landing cell outside the blast, or use a normal Intercede instead'); recomputes AoE cells via `computeAreaCellsFromData` from the persisted `data.areaShapeData` (409 if missing); Step 1 repositions the interceptor into the AoE adjacent to the target via `findAreaIntercedePosition(targetPos, targetDims, interceptorPos, budget, caps, interceptorDims, map, repositionOccupied, areaCells)` (409 if null) — interceptors take no fall damage for this reposition (no longer computed); Step 2 throws the target out of the AoE via `findThrowLandingCell(targetPos, throwAllyLandingPos, bodyStat, targetDims, map, throwOccupied, areaCells)` (409 if null) and applies fall damage to the target. Both interceptor/target digimon and tamer records are bulk-fetched via `inArray` into `digimonById`/`tamerById`. The resulting `AreaAttackClaim` no longer has `isThrowClaim` — `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup.ts` now always applies `dodgePenalty + 1` to the rescued/claimed target (the original target always takes a dodge penalty, matching single-target intercede), even though they're physically thrown clear of the hit; the interceptor still takes the AoE hit at 0 dodge via the existing full-swap branch. **Burn (2026-11-03j):** every position the claim changes (interceptor repositioning, target displacement or throw) runs through `applyMovementBurn` (via `movesFromPositions`) before the hit is applied, stamping `lastMovedRound` and logging any Burn damage; the EddySoul rules are loaded with the house rules. **Knockouts (2026-11-03o):** before each save (support and damage branches) the enemies the claim knocked out leave the encounter via `resolveEnemyKnockouts`: the interceptor (the hit, Burn on the way in), the target it moved, and, in the damage branch, an attacker that Digizoid Armor reflect knocked out, which ends the attacker's turn. It starts from the area group's `nextTurnIndex`/`nextRound` when the claim resolved the group |
| `intercede-skip` | `intercede-skip.post.ts` | `requestId`, `optOut?`, `optOutTargetIds?: string[]`, `characterOptOuts?: string[]` (GM only) | Recipient declines an intercede offer. **Plain skip:** removes the offer, then `resolveIntercedeGroupIfDecided` (`server/utils/intercedeDecision.ts`) resolves the attack if that was the last open decision — area attacks via `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup` (dodge-rolls for uncovered targets), single-target via a dodge-roll for a player target (or resolvePositiveAuto/resolvePositiveHealth when a positive support effect hits a player), support resolvers / `resolveNpcAttack` for an NPC target (Knockback/Pull `positionPatch` broadcast + Guiding Light fold in map mode). **`optOut: true` (2026-11-03g):** adds `optOutTargetIds` — filtered to the offer's own targets, falling back to all of them — to the owner's list in `encounters.intercedeOptOuts.targets[targetTamerId]` (row-locked, `server/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts`), then `pruneOptedOutOffers` clears **every** pending offer for that owner about those targets: single-target offers are dropped (resolving their attack if last), area offers lose just those targets and stay open if any remain (the pages' offer watchers return to the target picker). **`characterOptOuts`** writes `intercedeOptOuts.gmCharacters[targetId]` and leaves the offer open. `persistDecisionState` writes `pendingRequests` always, and participants/battleLog/turnOrder only when an attack resolved |
| `intercede-optouts` | `intercede-optouts.post.ts` | `ownerId` (tamer **entity** id or `'GM'` — same key as `pendingRequests[].targetTamerId`), `intercedeOptOuts` (full replacement string array); legacy `participantId` (tamer participant id or `'gm'`) still accepted | Saves an owner's full "Never Intercede" target list from the Intercede Options (player) / Intercede Targets (DM) modals into `encounters.intercedeOptOuts.targets[ownerId]` via a row-locked transaction — no longer on the participant rows, which every combat write can revert (2026-11-03g). 404 if a tamer `ownerId` isn't in the encounter. Then prunes the owner's already-queued offers for the newly opted-out targets in the same request (`pruneOptedOutOffers`) and returns the encounter |
| `intercede-pass` | `intercede-pass.post.ts` | `ownerId` (tamer **entity** id — players only), `action`: `'start'` \| `'resume'` | **(2026-11-07c)** "Pass until my next turn" / "Resume prompting". Runs in `serializeIntercedeClaim`. `start`: `setTemporaryPass` (row-locked; 409 outside combat, 400 for `'GM'`, 404 if the owner's Tamer isn't in the fight or is in reserve) records the Tamer's `turnsStarted`, then `applyActivePasses` answers the owner's waiting offers like Skip (offers with a usable Quick Reaction stay). `resume`: `clearTemporaryPass`, idempotent. Returns the updated encounter |
| `quick-reaction` | `quick-reaction.post.ts` | `requestId`, `tamerParticipantId` | Tamer with Quick Reaction order grants partner +Stage Bonus+2 Dodge Dice; area attacks: server resolves partner from `data.tamerAreaTargetIds`, strips QR target from other requests (same `tamerAreaTargetIds`/`digimonAreaTargetIds`/`npcAreaEligibility` stripping as `intercede-claim`, removing offers left with no eligible targets), then derives uncovered remaining targets as `groupState.data.originalTargetIds` minus already-claimed targets (`groupState.data.claims`), checking coverage via `isAreaTargetCovered` and creating dodge-rolls for any still-uncovered targets |
| `digivolve` | `digivolve.post.ts` | `participantId`, `targetChainIndex`, `rollTotal?`, `force?` | Evolves/devolves digimon in combat; full heal on evolve; restores prior wounds on devolve; validates unlock status, warp DC (vs campaign level), 5/day EddySoul limit; costs 1 simple action. Refreshes Guiding Light from the new form, campaign settings and live room positions before saving (2026-11-06d). |
| `digivolve-fail` | `digivolve-fail.post.ts` | `participantId` | Marks failed warp evolution attempt; sets `hasAttemptedDigivolve` |
| `clash-initiate` | `clash-initiate.post.ts` | `initiatorId`, `targetId`, `bolster?` | Rolls Body + size bonus vs target Agility; sets `clash` state on both; creates pending request for player targets; Wrestlemania: free once/round; Multi-Grappler support. Spatial range check (when encounter has a map) applies to all actor types: digimon actors use Reach quality (`meleeRange = reachRanks*2` or 1); tamer actors always use `meleeRange = 1` (tamers have no Reach). Footprint-aware (mirrors `attack.post.ts`'s melee check): both actor and target footprints are computed via `getFootprintDimensions`/`getFootprintCells` (Large/Huge/Gigantic digimon occupy multiple cells), and the range check uses the minimum Chebyshev distance across all attacker-cell × target-cell pairs, not a single anchor point. In map view, target selection is click-to-target via map reticules (same flow as attacks) instead of the card-view modal's target list; `mapSelectedAttackProp` returns a minimal melee-range-1 object for tamer-initiated clashes (previously returned `null` and skipped reticule filtering) |
| `clash-check` | `clash-check.post.ts` | `requestId`, `participantId`, `roll`, `bolster?` | Player responds to clash attempt; determines controller via opposed roll |
| `clash-action` | `clash-action.post.ts` | `participantId`, `actionType` (`attack`\|`end`\|`pin`\|`throw`), `attackId?`/`accuracySuccesses?`/`accuracyDiceResults?`/`accuracyDicePool?`/`attackName?` (attack only), `endClash?: boolean` (attack only), `landingPos?: Vec3` (throw only) | Controller attacks with half dodge pool for target; controlled tries to break free; resolves outcomes and updates clash state. **`'attack'` (Clash Attack)**: requires a real melee attack-selection + accuracy roll from the client — the controller picks one of their MELEE attacks (incl. Basic Melee), the client rolls accuracy and POSTs `attackId`/`accuracySuccesses`/`accuracyDiceResults`; the endpoint deducts 2 simple actions and delegates to `intercede-offer` with `clashAttack: true` (target dodges at half). Previously this always 400'd because nothing supplied those fields. **Charge/Pass**: `endClash` ends the clash after the attack (charge "move after"); a Pass attack (`getAreaShape(tags)==='pass'`) is server-forced to end it regardless. **No outside-clash dodge** is enforced in `intercede-offer`/`resolveNpcAttack`/`responses` (a clashing target attacked by a non-clash-opponent gets 0 dodge; see `targetCannotDodge`/`cannotDodge`). Tamer participants are assumed to have CPU 1 (no `cpu` stat in `tamers.attributes`) — used for `'pin'`'s `maxPins = max(1, actorCpu - targetCpu)` and the throw-impact secondary attack's `accuracyBonus` when a tamer is the clash controller. `'throw'`: if the encounter has a map and `landingPos` is provided, resolves the Controller's `actorBodyStat` → `maxDistance` and validates the cell via `findThrowLandingCell(targetPos, landingPos, maxDistance, targetDims, map, occupiedSet)`; on success, adds fall damage (`Math.max(0, fallHeight - 1)`, Feather Fall-adjusted) to the Clash Throw damage and moves the target to the landing cell (`applyPositionPatch` + `position-patch` broadcast). If the landing cell is adjacent to 2+ opposing participants ("group of enemies"), pushes a `throw-impact-attack` pending request (see `requests` row above) targeted at the Controller's tamer/GM. Missing/invalid `landingPos` or no map → damage-only (current behavior unchanged). **Response shape for ALL `actionType`s** is now `{ ...updated, participantPositions: room.participantPositions, destructibleStates: room.destructibleStates }` (via `getRoomSnapshot(encounterId)`), not just `updated`. **Burn (2026-11-03j):** a thrown target is stamped `lastMovedRound` and, if burned, takes Burn's movement damage for the cells flown (`chebyshev(targetPos, landingCell)`, before the fall), added to the throw's log line and `damage`. **Knockouts (2026-11-03o):** a thrown enemy knocked out by the throw, its fall or Burn leaves the encounter (`resolveEnemyKnockouts`), saved with the shifted `turnOrder`/`currentTurnIndex` |
| `clash-break` | `clash-break.post.ts` | `breakerId`, `clashParticipantId` | Third party breaks clash with Body opposed roll; costs 2 simple actions |
| `direct` | `direct.post.ts` | `tamerId`, `targetParticipantId`, `bolster?` | Tamer buffs digimon next accuracy/dodge roll; +Charisma (partner) or +Charisma-2 (non-partner); bolster adds +2; applies "Directed" effect (duration 99, consumed on use); once per turn |
| `special-order` | `special-order.post.ts` | `tamerId`, `orderId`, `targetId?` | Validates unlock (attributes + XP bonuses); checks per-battle/per-day limits; executes: Energy Burst (heal 5), Swagger (Taunt), Enemy Scan (Debilitate -2), Tough it Out! (remove debuff); others logged for GM |
| `skill-order` | `skill-order.post.ts` | `participantId`, `orderName` | Homebrew (gated on `rulesSettings.skillOrders`). Validates skill+attribute unlock via `getUnlockedSkillOrders`; per-battle via `participant.usedSkillOrders`, per-day via `tamer.usedPerDaySkillOrders`; deducts `getSkillOrderActionCost` simple actions; all effects log-only (GM resolves); passive orders (e.g. Bravado) repeatable, skip usage tracking |
| `cheer-up` | `cheer-up.post.ts` | `tamerId`, `targetParticipantId` | Requires Positive Reinforcement quality + mood=1; sets mood to 4; costs 2 simple actions (Complex) |
| `mode-change` | `mode-change.post.ts` | `participantId`, `newSwaps` | Requires Mode Change quality; costs 1 simple action — unless EddySoul `modeChangeFreeSwapsPerCombat` is enabled and the digimon has Mode Change X.0 Rank 2, in which case the first 3 uses per combat are free (tracked via `participant.modeChangeFreeSwapsUsed`); sets `participant.statSwaps` (stat pair swap for combat resolution); validates swap against quality rank (MC rank 1: damage↔armor only; rank 2: also accuracy↔dodge; X.0 rank 1: any single pair; X.0 rank 2: any permutation) |
| `spend-inspiration` | `spend-inspiration.post.ts` | `participantId`, `spendType`, `amount` | Tamer-only; deducts `amount` from `participant.currentInspiration` and syncs to tamer DB (base→granted→xp); validates `act-of-inspiration` (=`INSPIRATION_ACT_COST[level]`) / `fateful-intervention` (=`INSPIRATION_FATEFUL_COST[level]`) costs; logs the spend. Effect on the roll is applied by the GM (no action cost — spendable freely). **(2026-11-03h)** `reroll` (amount 1) is also spent by the digivolution Willpower Check's "✦ Spend Inspiration" re-roll on both encounter pages, which re-roll client-side only after this returns successfully |
| `grant-inspiration` | `grant-inspiration.post.ts` | `participantId`, `amount` | GM grants `amount` Inspiration to a tamer participant; increments `participant.currentInspiration` + tamer `grantedInspiration`; logs grant |
| `move` | `move.post.ts` | `participantId`, `distance?` (cells moved — path steps, 2026-11-03j), `noActionCost?` (2026-11-03j) | Deducts 1 simple action from the moving participant (the mover itself — partner digimon actions come from the digimon, not the tamer), unless `noActionCost` (Charge and `[Pass]` moves, already paid for by the attack). In combat with `distance > 0` (2026-11-03j) it stamps `lastMovedRound` and applies [Burn]'s movement damage via `applyMovementBurn` (`server/utils/damageOverTime.ts`), appending its battle-log entry. Called by `EncounterMap.vue` via `$fetch` after every combat map move, replacing a prior client-side-only approach that failed to persist. **Returns `{ success, defeated }` (2026-11-03o):** an enemy mover that the move's Burn knocks out leaves the encounter (`resolveEnemyKnockouts`, room gravity); if it was the active participant the next one's turn starts, and the saved `turnOrder`/`currentTurnIndex`/`round` follow. `defeated: true` tells `EncounterMap.vue` to cancel the Charge or `[Pass]` attack it was moving for |

---

## 4. Data Models & Storage

**2026-11-09k light material:** `flickering-light` is a new string material ID with the existing `light` elemental affinity. Optional light-source metadata lives in the material registry, with intensity/range/flicker defaults derived at render time. Saved voxel objects and database columns are unchanged.

**2026-11-09j mobility:** `DigimonDerivedStats.baseMovement` is an optional runtime field for Basic Movement before Speedy. Numeric movement/swim speeds, jump limits and fall buoyancy are derived from existing character records and qualities. No stored format or database migration changes.

**2026-11-09a:** Existing encounter JSONB carries optional `CombatParticipant.lastShieldRound`, `lagBaseOrder` and `lagOrderRound`, plus `ActiveEffect.appliesRound` for scheduled Lag. Lag's stored potency is the rolled number of initiative places, not BIT; its duration is a one-round marker excluded from ordinary turn countdown. Base-order metadata permits restoration and repeat-safe round resolution. A fresh combat clears the Shield stamp and Lag metadata/effects. Haste uses the existing duration-one/potency action-credit convention so casting after a target has acted carries one action into its next turn; it never inherits Accuracy/Health-based duration. Request JSONB carries `shieldComplex` alongside the existing Lifesteal flag. No columns or migration are added.

**2026-11-08u:** No column or migration change: `total_xp` remains lifetime earned XP, now the authoritative editable budget. For known totals, saved total minus available XP and current XP purchase value retains nonrefundable consumed spending across edits. Updating the budget recalculates the wallet while retaining that history. An unknown legacy total stays null during ordinary spending/refunds; GM initialization uses recorded purchases only and explicitly cannot reconstruct past consumed Inspiration. Imported known history remains preserved, with campaign GM permission required for a nonzero imported budget.

**2026-11-08t:** Reward receipt JSONB payloads optionally store `partnerDigimonIds` and `affectedDigimonIds`, preserving the resolved count and full refresh set even if evolution records change before a retry. Older receipts fall back to their original `digimonIds`. `RewardReceiptPayload` extends the existing request type; no new columns or migration are required. The user reported resolving the database errors after reviewing migrations 0024/0025; this session did not independently verify the live database.

**2026-11-08s:** `reward_grants` stores `id` (client request ID, primary key), `campaign_id` (cascade FK), normalized reward `payload` JSONB, optional `created_by` account ID and `created_at`. Migration `0025_add_reward_grants.sql` creates it idempotently. This persistent receipt distinguishes retries from new grants across processes and restarts. The same transaction owns receipts, Tamer/Digimon increments and active encounter participant updates. Apply 0024 and 0025 before deployment. The configured database was resetting connections during the preceding migration attempt, so live migration/application verification remains outstanding.

**2026-11-08r:** `tamers.total_xp INTEGER NULL` is a separately stored lifetime earned-XP value (`totalXp` in schema/DTO/create data). Migration `0024_add_tamer_total_xp.sql` adds the nullable column idempotently and intentionally leaves legacy rows unknown: consumed Inspiration erased historical purchase information, so existing totals cannot be accurately backfilled. Apply this SQL before deploying code that selects the new field. New forms track starting XP and future manual XP awards; legacy characters have an explicit one-time total entry. Character bundle and library imports/exports preserve known values and mark older files unknown. No other storage or environment changes.

**2026-11-08l:** The existing JSONB pending-request type adds `cleanse-select`; no migration is required. Its data stores caster identity/faction, BIT potency, area/Selective Targeting flags, original target IDs, per-target leftover Accuracy and recipient effect IDs, plus readiness. Interception records the actual interceptor as the recipient while resolving the original target's slot. The cap is the maximum eligible leftover Accuracy, never the sum. A `(participantId, effectId)` pair identifies an individual effect, including same-name effects on separate targets. Only effects with a finite positive duration can be reduced. Existing `usedAttackIds` supports the GM's per-attack Unlock control.

**2026-11-08j:** Taunt's existing `ActiveEffect.source` stores the source participant ID, and its existing `potency` stores that user's CPU x2. It is a `debuff` (`[N]`), including Swagger, so negative-effect stacking, filtering and removal apply. There is no schema migration. Previously queued support attacks missing their effect definition are recovered during decision resolution.

> 2026-11-08c: `CombatParticipant.woundsHistory` remains optional and keeps its existing JSON shape. It stores actual prior combat forms and takes precedence during devolution. No synthetic ancestor snapshots are added at combat entry: when history is empty, partner fallback resolves the direct parent from the evolution line and reads its persisted sheet wounds. Restored entity ID, wound totals and shared line stage follow the chosen form. No schema migration is needed.

> 2026-11-07o: Encounter participant fields are declared once in `types/combat.ts`, including stable `seq`, wounds/history, effects, Clash state, enemy/reserve flags and all Special Order/boss state. `server/db/schema.ts` types the existing `participants` JSONB column as `StoredCombatParticipant[]`, derived from the canonical model with only `currentWounds`/`maxWounds` optional for legacy rows. `actionsRemaining.simple` is authoritative; optional deprecated `complex` accommodates older snapshots/literals and the existing payload normalizer removes it. Type imports/re-exports introduce no database or runtime changes.

> 2026-11-09l: Optional `naturewalkActiveElements` and `naturewalkWaterGranted` are derived/server-owned participant fields stored in the existing JSONB. The first records currently qualifying chosen elements; the second prevents Water's two temporary wounds from refreshing until the next combat. GM Naturewalk settings live in the existing hazards JSONB; no migration or saved-map format change.

> 2026-11-07f: Participant JSONB now carries `lastMovedTurnKey`, `strikeFastReady`/`strikeFastTurnKey`, `finishingTouchReady`, `decimationReady`, `enduringSoulAvailable`/`enduringSoulUsed`, `strikeLastCounter`, `fatefulSpentRollIds`, and explicit minion/summoner flags. `specialOrderDebt` maps due round to the part of `interceptPenalty` that cannot be paid early: ordinary intercede debt still repays at the next turn, while Quick Reaction/Strike Last debt first becomes due next round. New request type `strike-last-offer` preserves a declaration opportunity after an automatic miss. Attack requests retain order snapshots; Fateful choices live on the affected request. Existing fresh duplicates use an allow-list, so none of these per-combat declarations leak into a fresh copy. Enduring Soul and the new ready-state defaults initialize at combat start (and for newly added partner participants). No SQL migration is required.
> Last verified: 2026-04-14

**Sources:** `server/db/schema.ts`, `server/db/index.ts`, `drizzle.config.ts`

**Engine:** PostgreSQL. **ORM:** Drizzle. **Migrations:** `server/db/migrations/` (0000–0023; note: 0002 is missing — sequence gap, appears intentional). No caching layer (no Redis). No message queue.

> **Migration tooling note (2026-07-08):** `server/db/migrations/meta/` snapshots/journal (`drizzle-kit generate`'s bookkeeping) haven't been updated since `0006_add_granted_inspiration` — migrations `0007`–`0014` exist as `.sql` files but aren't registered in `_journal.json`. `npm run db:generate` will misbehave (diffs against the stale 0006 snapshot) until this is reconciled; the actual sync mechanism in practice is `npm run db:push` (schema.ts ↔ live DB introspection, journal-independent). `0014_add_roll_log.sql` was hand-written to match the existing plain-SQL migration style rather than generated, for this reason.
>
> **Applying a new migration (verified 2026-07-29):** `_journal.json` still ends at `0006`, so **`npm run db:migrate` silently skips `0007`–`0018`** — an unregistered `.sql` file is never executed. `run-migrations.mjs` doesn't help either; it only executes `init-db.sql`, not the migrations directory. In practice a new migration is applied either by `npm run db:push` (introspects `schema.ts` against the live DB and generates the diff — journal-independent, but review its plan, it acts on whatever drift it finds) or by running the `.sql` by hand against `DATABASE_URL` (the pattern `migration-fix.sql` in the repo root came from). Hand-written migrations here use `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` so a repeat run is a no-op.

All 34 `jsonb` columns listed below are native Postgres `jsonb` (migration 0013 converted them from `text`); server routes pass/receive parsed objects/arrays directly with no manual `JSON.parse`/`JSON.stringify`. `server/utils/parsers.ts` (formerly `parseTamerData`/`parseDigimonData`/`safeJSONParse`/`ensureArray`) was removed as part of this conversion.

### Table: `tamers`

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | text PK | Nanoid generated |
| `name` | text | — |
| `age` | integer | — |
| `campaignId` | text FK → campaigns | — |
| `attributes` | jsonb | `{agility, body, charisma, intelligence, willpower}` (integers) |
| `skills` | jsonb | `{dodge, fight, stealth, athletics, endurance, featsOfStrength, manipulate, perform, persuasion, computer, survival, knowledge, perception, decipherIntent, bravery}` |
| `aspects` | jsonb | `Array<{id, name, description, type:'major'\|'minor', usesRemaining}>` |
| `torments` | jsonb | `Array<{id, name, description, severity:'minor'\|'major'\|'terrible', totalBoxes, markedBoxes, cpMarkedBoxes?}>` |
| `specialOrders` | jsonb | `string[]` (unlocked order IDs) |
| `xpBonuses` | jsonb | `{attributes, skills, inspiration}` |
| `equipment` | jsonb | `string[]` |
| `usedPerDayOrders` | jsonb | `string[]` (reset on `new-day`) |
| `usedPerDaySkillOrders` | jsonb | `string[]` (skill orders used today; reset on `new-day`; migration 0012) |
| `inspiration` | integer | — |
| `grantedInspiration` | integer | — |
| `xp` | integer | — |
| `currentWounds` | integer | — |
| `digivolutionsUsedToday` | integer | Reset on `new-day` |
| `notes` | text | — |
| `spriteUrl` | text | — |
| `creationRules` | jsonb | `CreationRules \| null` — Workshop (sandbox) characters only: creation-rules snapshot they were built under (migration 0015). Null for campaign characters (rules come from the campaign) |
| `ownerId` | text | Stamped with the current session's account id on every create (`POST /api/tamers`) when logged in — Workshop or campaign-scoped, including bundle imports (migration 0015 added the column; wired up 2026-07-12). Enforcement (edit/delete restricted to the owner) applies only when `campaignId IS NULL` — see Cross-Cutting Concerns → Auth Flow |
| `hidden` | boolean | NOT NULL default false (migration 0017). Workshop-only, owner-only visibility: sandbox list queries return hidden records only to their owner (hidden-without-owner treated as visible); GET-by-id 404s for non-owners; POST persists it only with a session; PUT strips it on unowned records. Ignored entirely on campaign-scoped queries |
| `publicAccess` | boolean | NOT NULL default **true** (migration `0020_add_tamer_access_grants.sql`, 2026-08-20). In-campaign access, distinct from `hidden` above: when true, anyone with campaign access (including password-only sessions with no account) can view/edit this tamer; when false, only DM-tier accounts or accounts with a `tamerAccessGrants` row can. Defaults true for existing and new tamers so nothing broke when this shipped — DMs opt individual tamers into restriction from Settings → Character Access |
| `createdAt` | timestamp | — |
| `updatedAt` | timestamp | — |

> **Sandbox rows (2026-07-11):** Workshop characters are ordinary `tamers`/`digimon` rows with `campaignId NULL` — list them via `GET /api/tamers?sandbox=true` / `GET /api/digimon?sandbox=true`. **Ownership (2026-07-12):** Workshop stays fully public/browsable regardless of `ownerId`; once a sandbox record has an `ownerId`, only that account can edit/delete it via `PUT`/`DELETE /api/tamers/[id]` and `.../digimon/[id]` (`server/utils/ownership.ts`'s `assertCanModifySandboxCharacter`) — unowned (legacy/anonymous) records stay open to anyone, unchanged. Exported bundles (`useCharacterBundle.ts`) never carry `ownerId`; importing a bundle re-stamps ownership to the importer's own account if logged in, so importing someone else's export effectively forks a personally-owned copy.

### Table: `encounters`

**2026-11-10:** Existing participant JSONB stores bounded `attackReceipts`/movement receipts and server-issued `pendingChargeMove` allowances (cast/attack/form/map/turn identity). Generic snapshots cannot author these fields; turn/form/map/phase changes invalidate allowances. No new columns or migration.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | text PK | — |
| `revision` | bigint | NOT NULL/default 0; database-owned snapshot ordering token. Migration 0023 adds `encounters_revision_before_update`, whose BEFORE UPDATE function always assigns `OLD.revision + 1`, including room persistence, preference edits and transaction writes. Returned as a JSON number through Drizzle's numeric bigint mode. Apply the migration before deploying. |
| `name` | text | — |
| `description` | text | — |
| `campaignId` | text FK → campaigns | — |
| `round` | integer | Current round number |
| `phase` | text | `'setup'\|'initiative'\|'combat'\|'ended'` |
| `participants` | jsonb | `CombatParticipant[]` — full combat state per actor (see types). **`inReserve?: boolean` (2026-07-29)**: GM staging flag, no migration needed (jsonb). A reserved participant is excluded from `turnOrder` (via `rebuildTurnOrder`), from the map layer (via `EncounterMap`'s `liveParticipants`), from every target list, and from `canStartCombat`/`pendingSetupTamers`; reserved digimon/NPCs sit at `initiative: -1` and roll on deploy. **`lastMovedRound?: number` (2026-11-03j)**: the round this unit last moved (map move, Charge, `[Pass]`, Knockback/Pull, Clash Throw, intercede), set only server-side; Burn's stationary penalty hits a burned digimon whose stamp isn't the round that just ended. It's a round stamp, so nothing resets it each round; it is cleared on the phase→'combat' transition. **`turnsStarted?: number` (2026-11-07c)**: turns this participant has started this combat, bumped by `recordTurnStart` at every turn start and never lowered by the PUT; "Pass until my next turn" is in force only while it equals the value stored with the pass. Not in the fresh-copy allow-list. No migration (jsonb) |
| `turnOrder` | jsonb | `string[]` — participant IDs in initiative order |
| `battleLog` | jsonb | `BattleLogEntry[]` — complete action history. Damage-breakdown entries (Dodge/Intercede) carry `baseDamage`/`netSuccesses`/`targetArmor`/`armorPiercing`/`effectiveArmor`/`finalDamage`, plus optional `clashArmorBonus` **(2026-11-03c)** — the outside-clash CPU already included in `targetArmor` |
| `hazards` | jsonb | `EnvironmentHazard[]`. Optional `naturewalkEnvironment: { elements, useMapTerrain }` on the permanent `naturewalk-environment` entry stores the GM's encounter-wide environment designation; changes require campaign GM permission. No migration. |
| `pendingRequests` | jsonb | `PendingRequest[]` — awaiting player input (dodge, intercede, clash, recovery-check, throw-impact-attack). `digimon-selection`/`initiative-roll` are single-slot per tamer — see the note under API Schema → Request/Response management. `answeredAt?: string` (2026-11-07) is stamped on a `dodge-roll` when it is first answered and outlives the response. **(2026-11-07d)** area-attack `dodge-roll`s all carry `data.intercedeGroupId` (one per target, array order = defense order) and `data.totalTargetCount`; single-target and counterattack dodges carry no cast id |
| `requestResponses` | jsonb | `RequestResponse[]` — submitted player responses |
| `visibleToPlayers` | boolean | NOT NULL default **false** (migration 0018). GM-controlled publish flag: player pages fetch with `?visibleToPlayers=true`, so a hidden encounter is never sent to a player client at all — no participants, no prompts, nothing. This is what lets a GM stage the next fight while the current one runs (a tamer queued into two encounters previously made the player page latch onto whichever came back first). Backfilled `TRUE` for rows already in `setup`/`initiative`/`combat` at migration time; new encounters and duplicates start hidden. Toggled from the encounter list card and the encounter header; `handleEngageCombat`/`requestSetupForTamer`/`requestInitiativeRoll` offer to publish before sending a prompt into the void |
| `intercedeOptOuts` | jsonb | `intercede_opt_outs`, NOT NULL default `{}` (migration `0021_add_intercede_opt_outs.sql`, 2026-11-03g). `{ targets?: { [tamerEntityId or 'GM']: targetParticipantId[] }, gmCharacters?: { [targetParticipantId]: participantId[] } }` — who never wants to be asked to intercede for whom, plus the GM's per-target "never intercede with these characters" checklists. Written **only** by `server/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts` (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE` transaction) from `intercede-optouts`/`intercede-skip`; `[id].put.ts` ignores it; `duplicate.post.ts` copies it. Read through `app/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts`, which falls back to the **legacy** participant-level `intercedeOptOuts` (tamer row, or the `gm` pseudo-participant) / `gmCharacterOptOuts` until that owner saves again — those fields are now read-only. No backfill; **apply before deploying the code** (every encounter query selects this column). **`temporaryPasses?` (2026-11-07c):** `{ [tamerEntityId]: { characterId, turnsStarted, createdAt } }` — a player's "Pass until my next turn" (`characterId` = the Tamer participant; players only). Written only by `setTemporaryPass`/`clearTemporaryPass`/`clearTemporaryPasses` (same row lock); read through `getActiveTemporaryPass`, which also checks the phase, that the Tamer is present and not in reserve, and that its `turnsStarted` is unchanged. Expired entries stay in the column until the next pass write prunes them but are never "active". Preserved by the opt-out writers (they spread the stored object); no migration |
| `createdAt` | timestamp | — |
| `updatedAt` | timestamp | — |

> **Response-only fields (not columns, 2026-06-13):** `GET /api/encounters/[id]` adds `version` (= `room.version` from `RoomState` — only increments on map events), `_battleLogTotal`, and `_battleLogWasTrimmed` (set when `battleLog` is trimmed to its last 50 entries) — see `server/utils/encounterPayload.ts`. The `encounter-state` WS push carries a separate `version` field set to `Date.now()` at broadcast time (not `room.version`) so the out-of-order-delivery guard works during non-map phases like Setup — see `server/middleware/encounter-sync.ts`. **Action responses and ordering (2026-11-07b):** action endpoints still return the raw `encounters` row (full `battleLog`, DB-snapshot positions, no `version`). Clients shape it with `shapeActionResponse` and order every snapshot — push, GET or action response — by `updatedAt` through `createEncounterSnapshotGate` (`utils/encounterSnapshot.ts`), so every writer of an encounter row must set `updatedAt`; all `update(encounters).set` calls do.

**New columns on existing tables:**
- `encounters.mapId` — text nullable FK → maps
- `encounters.participantPositions` — jsonb `Record<participantId, Vec3>`
- `encounters.destructibleStates` — jsonb `Array<{structureId,currentWounds}>`
- `digimon.giganticDimensions` — jsonb nullable `{width,height,depth}`

**Relations:**
- `campaigns` → `tamers`, `digimon`, `encounters`, `evolutionLines`, `maps`, `rollLog` (one-to-many via `campaignId`; `rollLog.campaignId` has no DB FK constraint, matching the `encounters.mapId` convention)
- `tamers` → `digimon` (one-to-many via `partnerId`)
- `tamers` → `evolutionLines` (one-to-many via `partnerId`)
- `users` → `sessions` (one-to-many via `userId`), `campaigns` (one-to-many via `ownerId`), `campaignAccessGrants` (one-to-many via `userId`), `tamers`/`digimon` (one-to-many via `ownerId`) — none are DB FK constraints, matching this schema's convention throughout
- `campaigns` → `campaignAccessGrants` (one-to-many via `campaignId`)

---

## 5. Pages & Components

**2026-11-09k Flickering Light:** The material picker adds a warm Flickering Light swatch. Existing block/ramp drawing, painting, undo/redo and autosaves retain the new material ID. `MapCanvas.animate` supplies elapsed time to terrain lighting and continues animation while unclipped sources flicker, without marking unchanged portraits/highlights dirty. Ordinary maps and maps containing only the original Light material still render on demand.

**2026-11-09j movement details:** Both encounter pages pass derived Basic Movement, RAM and CPU into `EncounterMap`; preview invalidation tracks these values. The player movement tooltip shows separate jump height/length plus swimming, burrowing, climbing and flight speeds, using shared map formulas. Ordinary water moves retain the normal action cost. Only Extra Movement: Swimmer supplies water support for falling; other Digimon can swim but still fall through water. `MapToolbar` adds Sand, Snow and Soft Metal; the editor accepts material IDs independently of `ElementType`, saves the matching elemental affinity and retains material IDs during repainting, ramp orientation and undo/redo.

Open-air jumps use the selected movement Y plane and reachable path, without requiring a terrain ray hit. Movement-height scrolling follows that plane with the camera for both players and GMs, so high jump/fly/climb previews remain in front of the pointer ray. Total movement spent also applies to jump height/length and to transitions between walking, swimming, digging, climbing and flight.

**2026-11-09f large maps:** `EncounterMap` owns immutable map roots, revision-based movement invalidation, queued autosaves and coalesced peer refreshes. Its optional `initialMap` avoids reloading the library editor's already-fetched map. Saving/Saved/Save failed with Retry reflects real queue state; route guards flush all outstanding maps, failed switches retain edits, stale/null fetches cannot replace the current map, and unmount guards suppress late refreshes. `MapCanvas` delegates terrain to `mapTerrainChunks`, rebuilds legacy structures only when their collections/state change, lazily creates gameplay LoS, and schedules rendering/overlays/highlights from invalidation. Pointer movement is coalesced to one sample per frame; preview meshes and replaced geometry are disposed. Clipping and ghost walls remain view aids only.

**2026-11-09g stair brush:** Stairs use plane-cell picking, draw modes and the shared `rampDirection` Uphill brush rather than wall-edge placement. Wheel rotation emits the existing `rotate-ramp` brush event for both shapes and refreshes the ghost after prop synchronization. `applyStairDraw` retains existing stair IDs while changing direction; `createStairGeometry` renders closed three-tread geometry for both the ghost and saved stairs. `stairDirectionForFace` maps legacy reversed east/west storage in both directions, preserving old maps while matching the visible brush direction on new placements.

**2026-11-09f map closing:** GM/player Map View toggles and Close Map buttons await the exposed `flushMapSaves` before unmounting the map. A failed save keeps the map and Retry visible. Player targeting-picker cleanup runs only after successful closing.

**2026-11-09c:** Both `MapPlayerHUD` views now use the same `hud-actions`/`action-dot` styling and live `actionsRemaining.simple` values: at least two dots, yellow for remaining actions and grey for spent ones, with extra dots for bonus actions. The GM's compact cards remove the text below the health bar and add the dots at the right, with a tooltip and accessible remaining-action count. No component inputs, placement behavior, dependencies or API/storage contracts change.

**2026-11-09b:** `EncounterMap` supplies `combatActive` to `MapCanvas`. For GMs, `canUseTurnRadial` allows the active character and every Digimon linked to the active Tamer, independently of the GM's empty player-ownership list. Eligible player characters use the normal player wheel, NPCs retain their wheel, and GM Move/Manage append to both, including controlled clashes and exhausted actors. Other tokens/phases expose only GM controls; the live menu rechecks turn eligibility before dispatch. The GM page consumes `player-action` for clash/fallback actions and supplies Direct, Special/Skill Order and Mode Change children, in addition to attacks, stances and evolution. Direct aiming highlights in-range Digimon, records the selected Tamer/bolster choice, and clears on turn/phase/map-view changes. Partner evolution availability reads the linked Tamer participant's actions. The extra top-right Fateful selector/button and automatic payer-selection watcher are removed; Orders and attack options explicitly choose the payer and retain `FatefulInterventionDialog`. The player page keeps its existing Orders dialog rather than consuming the new optional order children.

**2026-11-09a:** The third attack tier labels the effect upgrades Double Lifesteal and Triple Shield with their healing/temp-Wound descriptions and `2 Simple (Complex)` costs. Only the corresponding effect offers each option. Either Bolster choice clears the upgrade; selecting the upgrade clears Bolster. Haste/Lag ordinary attack gates and cost labels require two actions. Shield choices become unavailable after that caster's round use, including other Shield attack IDs. Both combat pages retain `shieldComplex` through Accuracy review and single/area submission. Quality descriptions now match the supplied Lifesteal, Shield, Haste and Lag rules; no extra floating map options are introduced.

**2026-11-09:** GM/player map targeting removes the floating `AttackRiderOptions` controls, retaining only a compact attack-name hint and Cancel. Card-view target dialogs retain their controls. Charge attacks add Move Before/Move After to the third radial tier; toggling either replaces the other, toggling it off restores no movement, and Continue with neither targets normally. Ordinary attacks omit these choices. Pass retains its separate two-step movement rather than an extra Charge choice. `EncounterMap` removes its floating Charge picker and target-suppression gate, initializes timing from the selected descriptor, and preserves the current step across equivalent record polls.

**2026-11-08u:** `TamerFormPage` removes both Set XP wallet inputs. Only the GM Library renders the Total XP input, with a minimum covering spent XP, inline validation and disabled saving while an invalid budget is entered. Player/Workshop modes show balances and spending controls without an XP setter. The summary shows Total = Available + Spent and explains consumed Inspiration. `useTamerForm.setTotalXp` uses shared reconciliation to update the available balance; purchases/refunds preserve the total. Initial XP bonuses are hydrated before computing costs, so older purchased upgrades count toward a newly initialized budget.

**2026-11-08t:** Grant Rewards displays one Bonus DP checkbox per partner, shown when its Tamer is selected and the DP amount is positive. Multiple partners under the same Tamer remain individually selectable; stages never appear as separate recipients. Labels/totals explain shared DP across evolution stages. `useRewardGrant.rosterReady` blocks submission until a successful load. Failed loads show a retry panel rather than the empty-campaign message, and a committed grant's success remains visible if refreshing the roster fails; Retry only reloads recipients.

**2026-11-08s:** The DM Library adds Encounters (existing encounter index) and Grant Rewards (`/campaigns/[campaignId]/library/rewards`). The rewards page selects individual, multiple or all Tamers, automatically selects their partner forms and allows exact form exclusions. Inputs are explicitly per Tamer/per form, with group totals, validation, pending state and an inline result. `useRewardGrant` owns selection, normalized payloads, submission guards and a retained request ID for an unchanged retry after a lost response. Reward amounts reset after success. Account GMs enter normally; password GMs can verify their campaign password if the new server credential is missing. The player page subscribes to campaign `rewards-granted` pushes and reloads only for its Tamer; reconnect reloads restore missed rewards. GM encounter pushes refresh the supplied record IDs and resync the encounter.

**2026-11-08r:** The player page's `maxCombatInspiration` reads `tamerStats.maxInspiration` instead of raw base Willpower; both `InspirationPanel` instances and the player sheet receive the same XP-aware cap. GM cards use `getTamerMaxInspiration` and the same calculator. Current encounter Inspiration, including zero and temporary excess, remains authoritative. The player sheet and `TamerFormPage` display stored lifetime `totalXp` alongside available XP. `setAvailableXp` counts manual awards after input change, while purchase/refund helpers leave the total untouched; older records show Not recorded and expose a one-time lifetime entry. `radialActionCosts` receives participants so Digivolve/Devolve displays `Tamer: 1 Simple` only when the server would find the linked Tamer; other Digimon retain their own cost. The player's evolution controls and `DigivolveWillpowerDialog` identify the Tamer's action cost. `MapPlayerHUD` displays each GM roster entry's live remaining Simple Actions, preserving zero, bonus actions and synced updates.

**2026-11-08q:** `MapRadialMenu.vue` renders second-tier attack choices and third-tier option choices simultaneously in separate concentric bands. `branches` retains each valid selected ancestor; `childSectors` and `thirdSectors` share the measured choice renderer through `detailSectors`, with tier-specific clip IDs and selected/expanded states. Root, attack and option pages are independent. Choosing another attack updates the third ring, Back/Escape removes the outermost open tier, and Arrow Left/Right navigates the focused tier. Trees containing third-tier choices reserve that radius before opening, preventing parent geometry from jumping. Ordinary two-tier menus retain their existing radii. `attackRadialOptions` removes None; Continue already sends an empty rider list when nothing is selected. Attack riders, targeting, costs and server behavior otherwise remain as implemented in 2026-11-08p.

**2026-11-08p:** Both combat pages build nested attack choices with `utils/attackRadialOptions.ts`. Each attack opens None, mutually compatible rider toggles and Continue; toggles keep the wheel open and never spend actions. `MapCanvas -> EncounterMap` forwards `attack-rider-selected` and optional rider IDs on `attack-selected`, checked against the current enabled tree and live page rules. None explicitly resets riders; Continue retains them for targeting. `MapRadialMenu` displays `cost` rows with measured child layouts and accessible descriptions; `radialActionCosts` decorates ordinary, clash, Direct, evolution, Mode Change, door and GM controls. Player Fateful opens the existing Accuracy review chooser. GM Fateful uses `usePendingAttackFateful` to hold the outgoing single/area roll, locks the owning deployed Tamer as payer and cancels on dialog cancellation, turn/participant changes or disposal. An NPC without a deployed Tamer has no Inspiration rider choices.

**2026-11-08n:** GM `mapRadialOptions` now supplies Digivolve/Devolve entries for Digimon with assigned lines, using `getParticipantEvolutionOptions` and current action/attempt/turn availability. `MapCanvas` displays those entries for the active NPC, using its own action availability instead of the player-only Tamer/range gate. The encounter page handles `action-selected` for Digivolve, rechecks the live option and dispatches `handleDigivolve`; NPCs use the existing ordinary server flow. Card-view evolution remains available through its existing buttons.

**2026-11-08m:** The GM encounter page's Pending Player Requests list explicitly labels `cleanse-select` as `Cleanse — Waiting for defenses` or `Cleanse — Choose effects`. Only `initiative-roll` receives the Roll Initiative fallback; other unmatched types display Pending request.

**2026-11-08l:** New shared `CleanseDialog.vue` and `useCleanseSelection` serve both encounter pages in card/map views. They offer one shared effect budget across all recipients, preview duration reductions, prevent mixed Dispel/Purify selections, wait for all defenses, suppress duplicate submissions and retain the prompt after a failed save. Cleanse quality text now matches the supplied BIT/leftover-Accuracy rule. The GM Manage modal lists used attacks with individual Unlock buttons; it clears only that attack's used ID. `getDraggableParticipantIds` permits both GM/player dragging only in setup/initiative; the live drop guard rejects a drag whose eligibility expired when combat began. Radial GM Move is unchanged.

**Movement previews (2026-11-08l):** `MapCanvas` renders a second copy of the moving character's portrait/initial and stance border at the destination with opacity 0.2. Its position and scale follow the camera and actual footprint. A dashed vertical guide and metre label measure the gap from the feet to the highest floor/voxel surface below the footprint centre; a grounded preview has no gap, and a column without a surface says "No ground below". Normal movement and unrestricted GM aiming share the preview. The existing destination footprint marker also uses opacity 0.2.

**2026-11-08k:** `MapCanvas` offers GM-only Manage in its generic GM radial and alongside NPC/clash actions; its guarded `gm-manage` event passes through `EncounterMap` to the encounter page's existing `selectedParticipantId` modal. It costs no actions and identifies the exact token chosen from an overlap. `EffectManager` accepts named source options with CPU/BIT, requires a valid source for Taunt/Fear Quick Apply and custom entry, and allows existing source repairs through its `update` event. The GM page computes source options from current participants and effect-adjusted Digimon stats, then persists updates through the existing encounter PUT. Taunt's entered CPU is doubled once; Fear stores entered BIT.

**2026-11-08j:** Shared `combatDice` applies the full Taunt penalty when a cast excludes its source, no penalty for a single-target attack against the source, and `max(0, CPU - 2)` for an Area Attack including the source (even when it is the only target). GM/player callers and counterattacks share this policy. The Taunt quality text now describes the penalty rather than forced targeting.

> 2026-11-08d: Partner evolution choices supply `currentEntityId` to `buildCombatEvolutionOptions`, which resolves the same actual form index used by manual digivolve and fallback devolution. A matching stored index disambiguates repeated entity entries; otherwise the entity lookup repairs stale indices. Missing current entries yield no partner choices. The GM Manage modal uses the same entity resolver for forced form choices. NPC callers continue to use their explicit stage override.

> 2026-11-08c: Both combat pages pass `allowDevolutionWithoutHistory` to the shared evolution-choice builder only for resolved non-enemy partners, independently of whether their Tamer is deployed. `getCombatEvolutionParent` validates the actual branch parent and linked record ID; root/broken/self/out-of-range parents stay unavailable. GM NPC stage overrides are used only for NPCs, so a stale local override cannot hide partner choices after a devolution/evolution cycle.

> 2026-11-08b: `MapCanvas.vue` registers token watchers through `watchParticipantSprites`. A scalar `spriteGeometrySignature` covers placed participant ID/type/entity ID, coordinates and effective footprint size; equivalent replacement character maps, actions, wounds and other overlay-only fields do not rebuild token geometry. Active/partner turn changes close the radial and call `refreshBorderColors`, preserving hover > target > active > default precedence. Actual movement, placement/removal, form and size changes still rebuild and invalidate targeting.

> 2026-11-08: Both encounter pages use `buildMapAttackDescriptor` (`utils/mapAttackDescriptor.ts`) after their own visibility gate, record lookup and role-specific stat calculator. The adapter preserves missing-record defaults, active effects, size offsets, Reach and custom Clash types; `EncounterMap` and `MapCanvas` import its shared descriptor type. `buildCombatEvolutionOptions` (`utils/combatEvolutionOptions.ts`) accepts parsed or legacy JSON chains and supplies unlocked children, wound-history devolution and unlocked warp grandchildren in branch order at base DC +5. The GM keeps `npcStageIndex` (including zero); the player keeps the line's current stage.

> 2026-11-07o: Both encounter pages delegate attack option policies to `utils/combatAttackOptions.ts`: `availableCombatAttacks`, `requiredAttackActions`/`canUseCombatAttack`, `canBolsterCombatAttack` and `hugePowerAvailability`. Custom attacks retain their order followed by unused basics; signature round/Battery checks, Combat Monster's optional complex-action rule, Bolster limits and Huge Power rank/round rules share one implementation. GM/all-record versus player/partner lookup and permission/turn handling remain in page wrappers. Both pages exclude Haste from Bolster choices.

> 2026-11-07n: The player sheet's `tamerStats` computed delegates to its existing `calcTamerStats` import with the current EddySoul rules and retains its null guard. `useTamers.calculateDerivedStats` defaults sparse base/XP stat keys to zero, matching the former player calculation. `MapCanvas`, `MapPlayerHUD`, `encounters/[id].vue` (`getDisplayName`) and `player/[tamerId].vue` (`getParticipantName`) call `buildParticipantDisplayNames` from `utils/participantDisplayNames.ts`: only enemy Digimon are grouped by base name and numbered (stable `seq`, else sorted participant-ID position); tamers and partner Digimon keep the base name and never affect enemy counts; records that cannot be resolved are left out of the map and the pages fall back to the base name (`'Unknown'`). Enemy status is a participant-level `isEnemy` when present (the server does not currently write one), else the Digimon record's `isEnemy`. `useDigimonForm` and `useDigimonStats` import `calculateEffectiveBaseMovement` / `getSpeedyMaxRanks` from `utils/movementQualities.ts`; Blue Armor is part of the shared effective base, so the form's stage-change Speedy cap and the live cap now agree.

> 2026-11-07m: `MapRadialMenu.vue` passes each child sector's geometry to `radialDetailRows` and supplies Canvas text measurements using the inherited font, weights and letter spacing. Rows receive individual horizontal offsets so their measured glyph rectangles stay within the curved wedge. Measurements refresh after font loading and are cached between renders. Per-instance `useId()` clip paths bound attack text in the sector coordinate system; title/ARIA descriptions retain the complete attack information when visible text is shortened.

> 2026-11-07l: Outer-ring radial choices may carry `range`, `aoe`, `effect` and `tags` (`types/mapRadial.ts`). `MapRadialMenu.vue` draws them as stacked rows from `radialDetailRows` (`utils/radialMenu.ts`) beneath the label, using `sector-meta`, `sector-effect` (orange) and `sector-tags` styles in the site palette; choices without details render exactly as before. Both encounter pages build attack items with `attackRadialItem` (`utils/attackBadges.ts`).

> 2026-11-07i: `MapRadialMenu.vue` reads colors from the shared Tailwind palette with `theme('colors.digimon.…')`: dark navy surfaces/borders, pale text and orange hover/selection/current/focus states. The current disabled stance retains its active outline and readable marker.

> 2026-11-07h: `components/MapRadialMenu.vue` renders accessible SVG annular sectors with a transparent token center, nested choice rings, inward-facing edge layouts, long-list paging and wrapped labels. Props: `items: MapRadialItem[]`, `center`, `bounds`, `label`, optional `tokenRadius`; emits `select(item)`, `hover(item | null)`, `close`. `MapCanvas.vue` builds the NPC/player/GM action trees, pins them to `characterOverlays` (including footprint/zoom), groups adjacent doors, and clears menus on turn/tool/attack changes or participant removal. Optional `radialOptions: Record<string, MapRadialOptions>` passes through `EncounterMap.vue`; new `attack-selected(id, attackId)`, `stance-selected(id, stance)` and `action-selected(id, action, choiceId)` events reach the page's existing combat handlers. Both pages build attack/stance choices from live participant rules; the player also supplies Direct, evolution and Mode Change choices. Selected attacks still enter map targeting before spending an action. Complex combat dialogs and legacy picker fallbacks remain available.

> 2026-11-07g: `combatDice.combatAttackStats` includes the participant's stored Combat Monster damage when its current form has that quality, keeping both encounter pages' total damage and bonus displays accurate. A stale bonus on a form without Combat Monster is ignored. Single and area Dodge spends send the exact defense `requestId`; single, area and GM rolls include `getPaidDodgeDiceBonus` after refresh, avoid charging an accepted Act again, and include paid dice when selecting a Fateful pool.

> 2026-11-07f: Both encounter pages use shared `combatDice.combatAttackStats` for normal/counterattack Accuracy and compute attack rows once per state update. `SpecialOrderTargetDialog` + `useSpecialOrderTargets` select a partner, target or negative effect; map-mode Special Orders now opens the actual order list. `ReactionOrdersPanel` + `useReactionOrders` offer Quick Reaction/Strike Last during incoming attacks, including a separate Strike Last offer on automatic misses. `FatefulInterventionDialog` + `useFatefulIntervention` choose signed bonuses and every face for current player Accuracy/Dodge/counterattack/attribute/skill/Torment rolls or pending incoming Accuracy/Dodge; the GM can select a Tamer and pending roll. `useAutomaticDefenses` serializes blocked defenses, suppresses zero-dice roll prompts, preserves optional Strike Last declarations and exposes an explicit retry after a failed send. Area defense uses the same continuation. Chosen Fateful dice are restored from synced requests after refresh. `AttackRiderOptions` displays active attack orders and disables forbidden Huge Power choices.
> Last verified: 2026-04-14

**Intercede conflict feedback (2026-11-06g):** both GM/player claim handlers display the server's specific 409 message, distinguishing an interceptor already used in an area cast from a competing claim or changed map state. If the response has no message, they show "This intercede offer is no longer available."

**Sources:** `app/pages/**/*.vue`, `app/components/*.vue`, `app/composables/*.ts`, `app/layouts/*.vue`, `app/middleware/*.ts`

| `/campaigns/[campaignId]/encounters/[id]` | `.../encounters/[id].vue` | default | campaign-access | Full combat view; map toggle shows 3D map when `mapId` set. **(2026-07-29)** Header gains the same visibility toggle + a ⧉ Duplicate button. `canEngageCombat` now covers `combat` as well as `setup`, and the per-tamer card action splits on `initiative < 0`: **⚔ Engage** sends `digimon-selection` (the full pick → initiative flow — the *only* path that creates the partner digimon participant, so it's what a mid-combat joiner needs) while **🎲 Init** stays a bare re-roll for someone already set up; setup-phase tamers also get **↻ Re-setup** to re-open the picker. `hasSetupRequest()` drives the "Setup pending…" state (previously only checked `initiative-roll`). `ensureVisibleToPlayers()` offers to publish before any prompt is sent, and the setup-status panel warns when prompts are outstanding on a hidden encounter. `handleRemoveParticipant` → `clearSetupExchangeForTamer()` retires a removed tamer's prompts/responses. `processResponse` reconciles partner digimon instead of appending (fixing duplicates), discards orphaned responses, and uses the atomic `deleteResponse` endpoint throughout **(2026-07-29)** Add-Participant modal gains an "⏸ Add to reserve" checkbox; a new Reserve panel under the Turn Order list lists staged characters (`reservedParticipants`, nesting a reserved partner under its reserved tamer via `reservedPartnerFor()`) with Deploy/Remove. `handleDeployReserve()` sends a tamer back through `requestSetupForTamer()` and rolls initiative inline for a digimon/NPC. `canStartCombat`, `pendingSetupTamers` and `getAttackTargets` all exclude reserves; the dead `playerPlacementMode` computed was removed (this page hardcodes `:player-placement-mode="false"`). **(2026-11-03c)** The card-view battle-log damage breakdown shows an "incl. Clash CPU +N" row under Target Armor when `entry.clashArmorBonus` is set (outside-clash CPU armor, already included in Target Armor). **(2026-11-03d)** `handleGmDodgeRoll` rolls 0 dice when the request's `cannotDodge` flag is set or `isAttackedFromOutsideClash(attacker, target)` holds for live participants. **(2026-11-03h)** Willpower Check modal: no Cancel once rolled; a failure offers "✦ Spend Inspiration" (1 of the rolling tamer's Inspiration → re-roll), shown only when that tamer has Inspiration. **(2026-11-07b)** Pushes and action responses go through `applyEncounterUpdate` (snapshot gate, see Cross-Cutting Concerns → Encounter Snapshot Ordering), which refetches only the Digimon, evolution lines and Tamers the update touched (`planRecordRefresh`, merged into `digimonList`/`allTamers`), so a player's digivolve updates the card Digivolve/Devolve buttons and the Manage modal's force options without a refresh. |
