## 7. Blast Radius

**ORM dependency (2026-11-10):** Validate runtime field metadata, database transaction proxies, SQL update-returning and locked command adapters when changing Drizzle. Offline fake-database regressions and TypeScript passed for 0.45.4; no production database was contacted.

**CSP/image changes (2026-11-10):** Validate hydration, HTTPS sprites, map overlays, blob/data previews, inline styles and WebSocket connections in report-only and enforced modes. Do not enable cross-origin isolation. CSP enforcement is an explicit runtime rollout step after staging verification.

**Security boundary (2026-11-10):** Changes to campaignSecurity/entitySecurity affect every scoped API and navigation/WS admission; changes to attackCommand/databaseScope/encounterRoom affect transaction rollback, retries, displacement durability and publication ordering. Movement changes must preserve mixed movement, jump/fall/water/underground rules, large-map performance and GM positioning. Release HTTP and WS protection together.

**2026-11-09l Naturewalk and jump launches:** `CombatParticipant.naturewalkActiveElements` and `naturewalkWaterGranted` are server-owned optional JSONB fields; stale roster saves cannot replace them. Environment refreshes update conditional stats after movement, forced displacement, terrain edits, form/roster changes and GM hazard overrides. Water's two temporary wounds require Advanced Swimmer and cannot replenish until combat restarts. Fire prevents incoming and stale Burn, Light/Thunder add Resistant duration reduction, Wood changes Poison's minimum, and conditional Ice armor applies to ordinary, reflected and Burn damage. Darkness sight bypasses only non-solid Darkness volumes. Naturewalk difficult-terrain immunity respects GM settings and never grants a missing movement capability or passage through Steel. Jump searches use actual supported launch cells across mixed movement; water launches require Extra Swimmer and airborne movement cannot reset a jump's origin. Existing forced displacement and end-of-turn falling remain active.

**2026-11-09k flickering sources:** New Flickering Light voxels use the same non-solid/transparent/sight-blocking defaults as the existing Light volume, so portraits remain visible and gameplay targeting retains those material rules. Matching adjacent source voxels share persistent, non-shadow-casting PointLights per chunk/elevation; reach includes the combined volume's extent. Animation changes source colors and intensities only, preserving alpha, the global transparent batch, unchanged geometry and material boundaries. Clip changes, source replacement, map switching and disposal remove or hide emitters. Existing Light and other materials have no source metadata and keep their rendering behavior.

**2026-11-09j swimming/jumping:** `movementRules.ts` owns `calculateSwimSpeed`, `calculateJumpProfile`, water detection across ground/space/voxel layers and optional numeric movement speeds. `getMovementCostProfile` counts total movement spent and checks it against the destination movement type's speed; `pathfinding.ts` passes accumulated distance to the optional step-cost callback and uses heap searches for terrain costs. Walk 7 / Swim 4 allows 2 walking + 2 swimming + 3 walking, while 4 walking leaves no swimming. Soft Metal consumes two movement units per entry. `useMapMovement.ts` and server `mapMovement.getReachableCells` pass the move origin into jump traversal and use these shared costs. Manual/projectile capabilities without numeric mode speeds keep uniform distance; preferred forced Intercede displacement strips all numeric mode speeds. `EncounterMap.npcMoveCaps` receives `baseMovement`, `ram` and `cpu` from both encounter pages; Tamer capabilities explicitly opt out of innate Digimon movement. `useDigimon.calculateDerivedStats` exposes Basic Movement and includes Improved Derived Stat/System Boost in CPU/RAM. `combatSpatial.getMovementProfile` mirrors movement/quality/effect formulas for spatial support; `getFallerProfile(..., selfMoved = false)` preserves forced-fall damage, supplies bounded `safeJumpHeight` only for turn-end innate jumps, and derives `floatsOnWater` exclusively from Extra Movement: Swimmer. These floaters remain at depth and stop incoming falls at the water surface with applicable fall damage; ordinary and Advanced-only swimmers fall through water. Existing server support targeting and forced-displacement callers consume these shared profiles; ordinary move action charging is unchanged. No migration or new dependency.

**2026-11-09j digging/climbing/flight:** `calculateSpecialMovementSpeeds` supplies quality-derived speeds; capability flags retain the ability to hover/cling while their movement allowance is zero. `getDigTerrainMultiplier` distinguishes soft ground, advanced hard surfaces, difficult Soft Metal and blocked Steel/energy solids. Burrow endpoints and valid vertical-wall/advanced-ceiling supports are landable and support gravity. `hasClimbSupport` checks wall faces, neighbouring solid voxels and advanced overhead support; walls/ceilings still block crossing. The shared movement cost profile chooses the applicable mode and caches terrain checks within one synchronous search, with a fresh profile after edits. New `mapVoxels` materials use existing string IDs and `getVoxelMaterialElement` keeps legacy elemental fields valid.

**2026-11-09j movement query performance:** `utils/movementMapIndex.ts` builds ephemeral query views with coordinate indexes for terrain, stairs, wall faces, ceilings and doors. Only search views are WeakMap keys; mutable application maps are never globally cached. Client reachable/fallback searches and server candidate/displacement searches share one view per search while cache invalidation retains the original map identity. Fresh searches see edits, duplicate cells retain first-match semantics, and serialization/voxel selection identity stay unchanged. An Advanced Jumper benchmark (Movement 6, CPU 2, height 16/range 8) retains 9,537 reachable cells: a 10,000-cell floor improves from 10,543 ms to 614 ms; a 50,000-cell floor takes 635 ms, including index construction. Numeric fallback searches reject impossible distances before traversal and use finite movement allowances instead of the legacy 200-expansion cutoff, preserving high-jump/platform paths.

**2026-11-09i camera/range overlays:** `MapCanvas` refreshes camera matrices before projection/raycast work and immediately after right-drag/Q/E azimuth changes, including the GM height-scroll path. This prevents stale camera rotation from offsetting or resizing HTML token and movement-ghost portraits relative to the rendered map. Range/effective-limit rings use `attacker.y + TILE_H + 0.015`, keeping them above floor slabs at all elevations without changing attack distances.

**2026-11-09h portrait visibility:** `mapTerrainChunks.isOccluded` only treats effective opacity-one voxels as portrait occluders. `MapCanvas.updateCharacterOverlays` consequently keeps HTML token portraits visible behind translucent terrain. The manager still checks later opaque cells along the camera ray, respects clipping and leaves picking/targeting independent.

**2026-11-09f large maps:** Shared GM/player rendering, picking, token overlays, movement/attack previews and map-edit persistence are affected. Atomic revisions replace deep-map JSON serialization; explicit frame invalidation must cover camera, health/stance, targeting, token/HUD drag, clipping and editing. Terrain updates preserve untouched chunk identities; doors/structures retain the existing renderer and gameplay geometry. Saved JSONB and default map PUT responses remain compatible; compact acknowledgement is opt-in. Existing single-editor save semantics remain; this does not add concurrent collaborative editing.

**2026-11-09d ramps:** Changes affect GM map drawing, saved voxel metadata, and voxel mesh rendering for both roles. Older maps default to blocks. Ramp edits use the existing map save/refetch and undo/redo paths; no endpoint or migration change. Movement, support, placement and targeting continue to treat ramps as integer voxel cells. **(2026-11-09e)** Ramp wheel rotation only changes the brush until placement, leaves existing voxels intact and is captured on the canvas alone; movement and attack aiming retain wheel priority.

**2026-11-09b:** GM access to player turn actions must not extend ordinary player ownership or allow another Tamer's partners to act. Match every linked partner to the current Tamer rather than relying on the single secondary highlight. Keep action limits, used attacks/orders, Direct range and once-per-turn state, Tamer-paid evolution, Mode Change free swaps and clash roles. Stale attack/rider/stance/order/Direct choices stop after a turn or phase change. Both normal and GM controls remain distinct: paid Move follows movement capabilities/budget, while GM Move/Manage remain free and available on other turns or during clashes. Fateful's payer comes from the clicked Tamer or attacking partner rather than a global auto-selected Tamer.

**2026-11-09a:** Preserve upgrades through both intercede Skip and Claim and every area recipient; the caster pays once at declaration. Lifesteal heals on one damage, remains capped by damage and respects Immunity. Shield absorption must not add Combat Monster count; combat-end cleanup removes Shield/temp Wounds without changing real Wounds or unrelated effects. Haste grants one action rather than a duration-scaled series of actions. Lag uses the full dBIT roll without a choice dialog, never adds multiple same-round shifts, applies to the owning Tamer's slot for a grouped partner, and restores base order after its affected round. Authoritative round saves preserve pending Lag without resurrecting a cleansed copy from stale client state. Ordinary turn advances and knockout round wraps share the same ordering rule.

**2026-11-09:** Charge movement is optional and must not appear on ordinary attacks or add a second movement step to Pass. Empty Continue must never leave targeting blocked or reuse an earlier timing. Preserve the moving partner's identity, movement save before a single-target attack, post-attack placement, cancellation and polling stability. Keep card-view riders, radial costs and attack submission behavior intact when removing the duplicate map controls.

**2026-11-08u:** Every XP budget change must move available XP by the corresponding amount after accounting for purchases and prior consumed spending. Never grant a player a new budget through a raw wallet PUT, a changed/null total, or nonzero character creation/import. Workshop characters have no campaign GM authority to assign XP; use the campaign GM workflow for nonzero budgets. Preserve ordinary zero-XP creation, player spending/refunds, GM rewards, imported consumed-history balances and unrelated character edits. Lock the latest Tamer row before validating so a stale character save cannot overwrite a committed reward. Unknown legacy consumption must not be invented.

**2026-11-08t:** Selecting any stage grants once to the whole owned partner, including branches, while other partners, Tamers, campaigns, enemies and unowned records remain excluded. Conflicting historical stage pools use the highest saved value, never a sum or reduction. Preserve atomic updates, receipt replay metadata and notifications for every changed stage. Failed recipient loads must not be mistaken for an empty campaign or allow a new grant. A refresh failure after a successful POST must retain the success and retry only the GET.

**2026-11-08s:** Grants must increment each unique recipient once, preserve unknown lifetime totals, leave unselected partners/other campaigns alone and never convert free Inspiration into refundable XP purchases. Validate every recipient before any committed reward; failed group writes must include receipt rollback. Combat Inspiration reads zero as a real balance, shares decision queues and locks encounter rows before character rows. Publish after commit only. Receipt conflicts distinguish a harmless replay from changed reuse. Reward API authorization must not trust the existing unsigned boolean UI cookies; verified GM-password credentials expire and invalidate on password changes. Ownerless campaigns without a GM password retain their existing open GM-tools policy. Grants do not consume character actions.

**2026-11-08r:** Inspiration maximum changes are display calculations; do not replace an encounter's current balance with the sheet pool, clamp temporary grants, or change spend/cost rules. Lifetime XP must never be inferred from the remaining Inspiration pool or reduced by purchases/consumption/refunds. Missing historical totals stay null until entered; imports must preserve this distinction from zero. Older API callers' refunds must not count as awards; budget reconciliation excludes CP and free GM grants and honors Torment receipts. Apply migration 0024 before any new-column queries. Evolution labels and GM HUD action counts are display changes and do not alter deductions, permissions or NPC behavior.

**2026-11-08q:** The third-tier correction is confined to radial rendering/navigation. Preserve both visible ancestor tiers, distinguish second/third clip coordinates, retain the attack page while paging or closing options, and close only invalid descendants when live options change. The menu remains anchored to the token and fits against the full outer radius. No page event, action payload, package or storage changes.

**2026-11-08p:** Rider toggles are declarations until target confirmation. Preserve action budgets, combined Inspiration affordability, owning-Tamer payment, Bolster limits/cooldowns, mutually exclusive variants and Huge Power suppression. None must clear prior flags; stale disabled branches cannot dispatch. Fateful must finish before outgoing submission and cancel cleanly. Child layout must preserve both the action name and cost in narrow fans. Direct-GM cost deductions affect single/area offers only; already-paid player attacks must never pay twice.

**2026-11-08o:** Blast, Burst, Close Blast, Cone, Line and Pass share the area forced-movement fix. Preserve per-target hit/damage eligibility, intercepted-recipient identity, sequential updated occupancy, Immunity, fall/Burn/knockout processing and aura refresh. Claim transactions must never broadcast their tentative movement. Pure group resolution returns a patch; its decision/claim caller owns publication. This is forced-movement coverage, not a complete audit of every AoE effect's bespoke mechanics.

**2026-11-08n:** NPC map evolution must preserve assigned-line gating, unlocked branches, per-NPC `npcStageIndex`, history-based devolution, own-turn/action limits and the once-per-turn evolution attempt. GM force evolution remains a separate Manage control.

**2026-11-08m:** Immunity changes are limited to the legacy GM NPC attack endpoint, including effect-triggered side effects and Cleanse prompts. The early target read replaces the previous support/damage target reads rather than adding another query. Request-label changes are display-only.

**2026-11-08l:** Cleanse changes normal support/damage exchanges and delayed intercede/defense resolution. Preserve the one-cast maximum, actual intercepted recipient, damage gates, immunity, finite-duration eligibility and once-only selection consumption. It does not imply that every other effect's bespoke mechanics have been audited. GM unlocking preserves action counts, Battery and other cooldowns. Drag restrictions apply to placed tokens; unplaced reinforcement placement and explicit radial GM Move remain available. Movement gap rendering is a visual aid and does not alter movement legality, falling, terrain capabilities or occupancy.

**2026-11-08k:** Manage remains GM-only, available without action/turn/clash restrictions. Source identity affects Taunt/Fear targeting in GM/player ordinary attacks and shared counterattack/clash adapters. Source labels distinguish same-name tokens by participant identity. Source repair updates potency when derived stats are available; other effects keep their existing source-free Quick Apply behavior.

**2026-11-08j:** The queue metadata fix applies to all attack definitions, with real single/area regressions for Taunt, Stun, Poison, Shield and Fury. Taunt's `[N]` classification also affects effect badges, Selective Targeting, generic debuff stacking and removal. This addresses queued-definition loss; it does not establish complete mechanical support for every effect. Cleanse has a separate missing resolver and is being handled independently.

**2026-11-08i:** The AoE rendering change covers Blast, Burst, Close Blast, Cone, Line and Pass previews through `updateAoeHighlight`. It changes fill geometry, opacity, outlines and preview-resource cleanup. Gameplay coverage and blocked-cell targeting semantics are preserved, and transparent map-voxel material boundaries retain their existing rendering policy.

**2026-11-08h:** Size occupancy changes affect normal/charge/Pass movement previews and paths, setup placement and dragging, GM destination checks, intercede reachability/landing/displacement and throw/push/pull validation. The ally pass-through and two-size-difference exceptions are replaced by the user's reciprocal Tiny/Small rules. The token chooser runs after movement/placement/AoE handling and before single-target selection or token actions, preserving those mode priorities. GM Move retains its free WebSocket position update and now owns scroll height while active; cancellation/completion restores camera zoom.

**2026-11-08g:** Equal-length path selection in `utils/pathfinding.ts` affects movement previews and committed anchor paths through `useMapMovement.ts`/`EncounterMap.vue`, including ordinary moves, charges and thrown-unit aiming. Reachable-cell sets, movement cost and terrain/capability validation are unchanged; necessary upward/downward travel remains available. The map-signature cache from 2026-11-08f is untouched.

> 2026-11-08d: Canonical auto-devolution changes apply across all damage/turn paths. Keep exactly one restoration per damage event, transaction-bound record/stage writes, partner shared-line versus NPC local-index isolation, optional legacy totals, history priority and existing aura/save order. The helper's failure path must leave the participant and history unchanged. Partner stage resolution must precede direction/cost/snapshot decisions and agree between UI, manual API and fallback resolver.

> 2026-11-08c: Devolution changes affect both manual pickers and partner knockouts across single/area attacks, intercedes, turn effects, movement and throws. Preserve saved-history precedence, stored parent wounds, branch-parent matching, shared-line synchronization, transaction-bound reads/writes, action costs and NPC history rules. A later knockout pass must exclude a participant already auto-devolved by the same damage event. Partner names remain unnumbered; partner tokens and turn slots remain present.

> 2026-11-08b: Bulk-cache changes affect shared combat record loading, including callers outside End Turn. Retain pending-read sharing, negative caching, retry cleanup, invalidation and request isolation; restored forms are keyed by their own entity IDs. Keep gravity, damage, knockout, random quality triggers and aura passes in their existing order. Token watcher changes affect map updates for both roles: equivalent snapshots must preserve mesh identity, real layout changes must rebuild, and highlight precedence/radial closure must remain correct.

> 2026-11-08: Map attack adapter changes affect both map targeting previews; preserve role-specific stat calculation, missing-record defaults, effect adjustments and Reach/size semantics. Evolution helper changes affect both combat form pickers; preserve NPC overrides, history gating, branch order and warp DC. Turn eligibility changes affect five attack/Clash endpoints; preserve participant-ID matching, partner Tamer matching, lazy cached reads and route-specific errors/exceptions.

> 2026-11-07o: Canonical combat types affect client/composable/schema participant contracts; retain every persisted optional field and the legacy storage distinction when editing them. Attack option helpers affect which attacks/riders either page offers; keep record ownership in wrappers and preserve signature cooldowns, action costs and rank/round semantics. Calculation/resolution endpoints retain their existing behavior; the player Haste/Bolster choice is aligned with the GM.

> 2026-11-07n: Movement-quality helpers affect form stage constraints, effective base movement and Speedy rank limits; Blue Armor's +4 must stay inside the shared helper, before the minimum clamp, so the stage-change cap and live cap agree. Display-name changes affect token health/menu labels, HUD names, the encounter participant list and the player page; only enemy Digimon are numbered, so anything that relied on numbers to tell same-name partners or NPC tamers apart (tokens, HUD, battle-log text from `resolveParticipantName`) now shows identical names. Server naming remains a separate implementation because it groups by entity ID; `resolveParticipantName` is covered by `tests/participantNameIntegration.test.mjs`. The Tamer calculator is shared by sheet/summary/combat displays, so sparse-record and EddySoul-switch coverage protects those consumers together.

> 2026-11-07m: Attack detail layout depends on each child's actual annular sector and measured SVG font styles. Keep the component's Canvas font settings aligned with its text CSS; validate glyph bounds, diagonal/narrow sectors and font changes when updating either. Plain choice layout and combat event routing are unchanged.

> 2026-11-07l: Changing attack text rows on the radial outer ring affects the GM and player map attack pickers. The tallest case (2-line name, split range/AoE rows, effect, 1 tag line) must stay inside the 80px ring at the top, bottom and side positions of the fan; re-check dense attack lists and edge-fitted fans. Keep `attackRadialItem` the single source for attack entries so the two pages cannot drift apart.

> 2026-11-07h: Radial changes affect map battle action selection on the GM and player encounter pages. Keep `MapCanvas` and `EncounterMap` choice events aligned with both pages, preserve action availability and the live-tree dispatch guard, and verify token anchoring, edge fits, keyboard navigation and long-list paging when changing the renderer/geometry. Card combat and server action resolution retain their existing handlers.

> 2026-11-07g: Queue changes affect single/area automatic misses, Counterattack continuations and nonzero attacks delegated to intercede-offer; preserve request additions and answered/removal state in either queue order. Dodge caps cover Clash, Counterattack and Strike Fast once, while request-bound paid additions and fixed Fateful faces must survive refreshes and retries. Combat Monster display bonuses are shared by normal attacks and counterattacks on both encounter pages. The legacy NPC route's Enduring Soul check must run on `finalParticipants` before devolution/removal; its saved roster and turn order retain the protected target until a later lethal hit.

> 2026-11-07f: Shared dice changes affect player/GM single attacks, area defenses, counterattacks, Clash Attacks, NPC auto-resolution and submitted defense recounts. Order hooks also cover encounter start, turn/round advances, direct damage, interceding, DoT, falls and knockout/devolution ordering. Bastion/Debilitate modify core combat stats and derived movement/range/SPEC values while leaving Health unchanged. Keep mutable participants/effects sequential even when record reads are cached. Movement search reuse must retain invalidation on origins, capabilities, geometry, destroyed structures, occupied footprints and faction changes.

**Shared combat workflows (2026-11-06c):** `useDigivolveWillpower`, `useClashAttack`, `DigivolveWillpowerDialog` and `ClashAttackDialog` affect both GM and player views. `utils/attackAccuracy.ts` also feeds all ordinary single-target/area rolls and clash-controller accuracy; changes must retain player roll review, GM logging, Charge resolution-before-movement and Pass opponent inclusion. Regression checks are committed under `app/tests/`.

## 8. Cross-Cutting Concerns
> Last verified: 2026-04-14

**Sources:** `middleware/campaign-access.ts`, `middleware/dm-access.ts`, `middleware/settings-access.ts`, `server/utils/password.ts`, `server/utils/session.ts`, `server/utils/campaignAuth.ts`, `server/utils/ownership.ts`, `server/api/auth/*`, `server/api/campaigns/[id]/verify-password.post.ts`, `server/api/campaigns/[id]/my-access.get.ts`, `server/api/campaigns/[id]/grants/*`, `server/api/campaigns/[id]/tamer-grants/*`, `server/api/campaigns/[id]/tamers/[tamerId]/public-access.put.ts`, `nuxt.config.ts`

### Auth Flow

**2026-11-10: HTTP and WebSockets share authoritative admission through `server/utils/campaignSecurity.ts`.** Public campaign listings retain their existing fields. Protected content requires a signed player/GM credential or a stored account grant. Boolean UI cookies convey no server permission. SSR middleware forwards cookies through `useRequestFetch()` and checks `my-access` before navigation. Once admitted, players remain trusted to control all campaign player characters; NPCs, enemies and GM-addressed prompts require GM access. Hidden encounters are GM-only.

- Anonymous GM access exists only when the campaign has no owner, no player password and no GM password. Owned settings/deletion/grants require owner/co-owner access; co-DMs and GM credentials retain ordinary GM tools. Ownerless protected campaigns without a GM password remain locked. `scripts/secure-campaign-gm.mjs` repairs only that state offline, using an explicit operator-supplied database URL and a dry run unless `--apply` is supplied. No public claim or backfill route exists.
- Password verification issues role-bound, signed, 30-day `httpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, secure-in-production credentials against the persisted password hash. Async PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 uses 220,000 iterations and versioned hashes, with constant-time comparison and conditional legacy upgrades on successful login. Password changes revoke old credentials and connected peers. Account sessions/grants are rechecked, including expiry and logout.
- `entitySecurity.ts` derives scope from stored campaign, partner and evolution relationships. Reads cover direct IDs and filtered/unfiltered collections. Runtime operation allowlists reject unknown/authority fields; linked writes are authorized before mutation. Unowned Workshop records remain publicly editable; owned Workshop records require their account owner. Legacy null-campaign maps/encounters are readable but cannot grant anonymous GM write access. New maps/encounters require an authorized campaign. Changed sprite, scene and preset URLs, including imports, require absolute HTTPS URLs.
- `encounterCommand.ts` / `attackCommand.ts` serialize commands and use a database transaction, row lock, fresh authorization and deferred room publication. Generic encounter replacement is GM-only. Dedicated turn, stance, card, log, movement, door and GM evolution operations derive effects and spending from stored state. `participantActionLog.ts` resolves stored identities and saves mechanical stance/card-movement logs together with costs. Server-only services replace public bypass flags. Bounded JSONB replay receipts prevent repeated spending; no schema migration is introduced.
- Normal movement submits participant, destination, optional path and operation ID. Server checks mixed budgets, jump launches, placement, occupancy, terrain and landing. GM positioning retains its unrestricted distance/elevation rules. Charge-before/Pass previews remain local until final attack confirmation and commit movement, crossed doors, Burn and attack preparation together. Cancelling discards previews. Charge-after uses a one-use allowance bound to actor/form/map/turn/attack; cancelling skips only the remaining movement. Door operations patch only current `isOpen` values; geometry saves preserve existing door states.
- State-changing HTTP requests require a configured allowed Origin. WebSocket admission precedes room joining/snapshots; messages have typed operation permissions, expiry/revocation checks, a 64 KiB limit, 30/s with burst 60, and 100 connections per trusted IP. Client-authored movement/notifications are rejected. Authentication closure clears queued writes, ends reconnect loops and returns to access verification.
- Password failure limits apply per IP and campaign-plus-IP, without campaign-wide lockout. Principal write limits are 120/minute, map saves 300/minute, and creation/deletion 10/minute, with shared-IP headroom. Socket IPs are the default; Railway header trust requires operator verification first.
### Security headers and images (2026-11-10)

Nuxt Security **2.7.0** supplies SSR nonces, SRI and security headers. `nuxt.config.ts` permits HTTPS images, data/blob previews, required inline styles and approved application sockets. Scripts require a fresh nonce; eval and script attributes are disallowed. COEP/COOP/CORP and Origin-Agent-Cluster isolation are disabled to preserve external sprites. `server/plugins/security-csp.ts` finalizes configured WebSocket origins at runtime and selects report-only versus enforcement without rebuilding. Keep report-only until actual staging flows have been inspected; local browser verification does not replace that operator check.

`utils/imageFallback.ts` validates rendered saved image URLs and supplies a local placeholder without rewriting legacy data. Dynamic image bindings use `displayImageUrl`; the image error plugin also handles failed HTTPS loads. Explicit generated previews may use local blob/raster-data URLs. HTTP and imports reject new/changed non-HTTPS sprite, scene and preset URLs. This affects forms, token overlays, HUD, evolution selectors, Workshop, scenes and campaign sheets.

Verification uses an isolated Node 24 binary when the host default is older. The production container also uses Node 24. No production database access or migration is needed for hardening. `SECURITY_DEPLOYMENT.md` describes staging CSP checks, exact Origin configuration, spoofed-header diagnostics, offline credential repair and remaining operator tasks.

Local browser smoke passed on Node 24.22.2 and isolated headless Chrome against production output: unique SSR nonce/header agreement, Vue hydration/form submission, cross-origin local HTTPS sprites, data/blob previews and a same-origin WebSocket handshake. Both report-only and enforcement had zero unexpected CSP violations. An injected parser script without a nonce ran only in report-only mode and was blocked by enforcement. Login/image fixtures are local; real account authentication, staging gameplay and container execution are not claimed by this smoke test.

### Error Handling

- API handlers use Nuxt's `createError({statusCode, statusMessage})` / `throw createError(...)` pattern
- No global error page configured beyond Nuxt defaults
- Client-side errors surfaced via composable `error` refs (e.g., `const error = ref<string | null>(null)`)
- No error reporting service (no Sentry, etc.)

### Logging & Observability

- No structured logger (no winston, pino, etc.)
- Combat history is recorded in `encounters.battleLog` (persistent per-encounter action log)
- `console.log` / `console.error` only for development debugging

### Testing Strategy

**Offline verification is mandatory (2026-11-10).** `npm test`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` preload `scripts/offline-env.mjs`, replace any production URL with an inert localhost endpoint, and reject unexpected DB access. Fake DB/peer and Vue harnesses cover admission, linked writes, real route boundaries, command rollback/replay, movement, expiry, flooding and reconnect. Legacy business fixtures explicitly use a trusted admission adapter; security suites exercise actual guards. Never run verification against production.

- Naturewalk/jump launches (2026-11-09l): `naturewalkRules`, `naturewalkCombat`, `naturewalkEncounter` and `naturewalkMovementEnvironment` cover free/paid element selection, environment detection/GM overrides, movement and combat bonuses, resistance, darkness blockers, stale-save protection and Water's once-per-combat wounds. `jumpLaunch` checks solid footing, water launches with/without Swimmer, mixed paths and client/server agreement. Existing movement/intercede tests retain valid grounded launch fixtures and continue to check gravity, barriers, occupancy and effect resources. All 1,309 regression tests and Nuxt TypeScript checks pass. Synthetic innate-jumper reachability takes 167 ms on a 10,000-cell floor and 127 ms on a 50,000-cell floor (7 walking, 519 reachable cells each); these are local query measurements, not Kamo River frame timings.

- Flickering light (2026-11-09k): material/persistence tests cover source defaults, light affinity, custom colors, carved windows, toolbar selection and block/ramp repaint/undo. `flickeringLightFrames.test.mjs` checks demand rendering with real terrain managers: ordinary/old-Light maps idle, flicker frames leave cached portraits/highlights alone, and clipping/removal/disposal stop animation. `flickeringLightTerrain.test.mjs` covers intensity bounds, local illumination, transparent sorting, ramps, opaque overrides, chunk/resource reuse and source cleanup, including 120 animation frames and 30 edit cycles with stable resources and unchanged saved data. All 1,278 regression tests and Nuxt TypeScript checks pass.

- Movement qualities (2026-11-09j): `swimMovement.test.mjs`, `jumpMovement.test.mjs`, `jumpPathfinding.test.mjs`, `swimmingPathfinding.test.mjs`, `specialMovementRules.test.mjs`, `specialMovementPathfinding.test.mjs`, `serverSwimMovement.test.mjs` and `mobilityStatsIntegration.test.mjs` exercise half Basic/full Movement, real RAM/CPU bonuses, vertical water and surfacing, cumulative mixed routes (including both Walk 7 / Swim 4 examples), cached/uncached budgets, jump-origin bounds, elevated platform landings, digging material restrictions, double-cost Soft Metal, wall/ceiling supports, destroyed-support gravity, forced falls and Tamer exclusions. `jumpAirTargeting.test.mjs` runs real player/GM wheel, hover and click handlers against empty-air targets above the camera. `voxelMobilityMaterials.test.mjs` and `rampEditor.test.mjs` cover material identities, element affinities, toolbar choices and block/ramp paint/undo/redo. Existing movement, occupancy, Intercede, knockout and turn-end tests cover shared callers.

  `movementMapIndex.test.mjs` and `movementMapViews.test.mjs` cover fresh-view lifetimes, duplicate and destroyed-structure rules, mutable-map edits, geometry parity, saved appearance and selection identity. Indexed searches retain the same reachable sets as direct geometry queries.

  Final validation: all 1,265 regression tests and Nuxt TypeScript checks pass. Synthetic 10,000/50,000-cell jump searches include index construction and retain identical reachable cells; live Kamo River profiling remains outside this offline validation.

- Camera/range overlays (2026-11-09i): `tokenCameraSync.test.mjs` uses real Three.js cameras and OrbitControls to check token centers/sizes and movement ghosts against renderer-refreshed projections during rotation. `rangeRingHeight.test.mjs` verifies real ranged/area rings stay above floor slabs at positive/negative elevations and voxel surfaces while preserving radius/colors. Frame scheduling and movement/interaction regressions remain covered.

  Validation: all 41 focused camera/range/frame/interaction/portrait tests and TypeScript checks pass. An in-memory simulation of the old camera behavior reproduces approximately 47px of right-drag portrait offset; current camera synchronization passes the same regression. Wide/tall viewports, four portrait sizes, Q/E turns, movement ghost/gap labels and single/area range rings are covered.

- Transparent portraits (2026-11-09h): 34 terrain renderer regressions cover transparent block/ramp materials, windows, custom/zero opacity, opacity-one overrides, opaque blockers behind transparent terrain and clipping. `mapTokenVisibility.test.mjs` executes the real portrait-overlay handler with the real terrain manager and confirms portraits stay visible while picking and attack visibility retain their material rules.

- Stair placement (2026-11-09g): `stairEditor.test.mjs`, `stairGeometry.test.mjs` and the stair case in `rampScroll.test.mjs` cover atomic draw modes, rotation/no-op/undo, closed tread/riser geometry and all cardinal directions, saved-stair appearance compatibility, stationary/drag ghosts, cell picking and the direction saved through EncounterMap.

- Final map performance/stair validation: the full 1,099-test suite passes, plus two real GM/player close-view save regressions in `mapCloseSaves.test.mjs`; Nuxt/TypeScript checks pass. Latest headless benchmarks: 1000-cell strokes on 50k existing voxels draw/paint in about 23–24ms with one revision per operation; 10k/50k floors use 98/448 terrain batches instead of 20k/100k individual terrain objects. Timings are CPU/scene measurements, not browser GPU FPS.

- Large maps (2026-11-09f): `atomicMapEditor`, `mapAutosave`, `mapSaveIntegration`, `mapTerrainChunks` and `mapFrameScheduling` regressions cover atomic commits/no-ops/delta history, save ordering/failure/retry/navigation/stale fetches, 10k/50k batching, local chunk identity, transparent boundaries/depth ordering, clipping, ramp/spawn picking and resource stability over 50 edit/clip cycles. Existing movement/ramp/interaction suites use immutable revisions and the shared renderer. `node scripts/benchmarkMapEdits.mjs` measures 1000-cell draw/paint/delete/undo on 10k/50k voxels; `node scripts/benchmark-map-terrain.mjs` measures CPU build/edit/pick time and rendering-object allocation. Initial headless results: 10k floor 98 terrain batches vs 20,000 individual objects; 50k floor 448 vs 100,000; one-cell edits rebuild one chunk. GPU/browser frame times and the live Kamo River map remain unmeasured because database reads reset connections.

- Ramp wheel rotation (2026-11-09e): `rampScroll.test.mjs` executes the real editor and canvas wheel/hover handlers with asynchronous Vue prop synchronization and Three.js geometry. Five regressions cover both directions/wraparound, stationary and drag previews, placement direction, event consumption, ignored inputs and movement/attack priority. All 42 focused ramp/map interaction tests and `npm run typecheck` pass.

- Ramp voxels (2026-11-09d): `rampEditor.test.mjs` exercises real editor drawing/conversion, all draw modes and directions, painting, spawn flags, undo/redo/deletion, encounter persistence/broadcasts, grid movement/support and toolbar rendering. `rampGeometry.test.mjs` checks wedge bounds/volume/normals/raycasting, map and hover meshes, slope spawn markers, transparent refresh and clipping. All 114 focused map regressions and `npm run typecheck` pass.

- GM action dots (2026-11-09c): updated the existing real `MapPlayerHUD` renderer regression to verify spent/unspent dots and accessible counts at zero, normal and bonus actions, after spending and after a round reset. All 13 `mapHudPlacement.test.mjs` tests pass, including both views' placement/dragging/minimize behavior. This template-only change was not checked in a live browser; the full suite was not rerun.

- GM player turns (2026-11-09b): `gmMapPlayerActions.test.mjs` executes real Canvas selection/menu/dispatch and GM page option/action handlers for active Tamers, multiple partners, NPCs, player ownership, off-turn/reserve/noncombat restrictions, exhausted/controlled actors, Direct targeting/budget/source, Fateful/Special/Skill Orders, Tamer-paid evolution, Mode Change, clash forwarding and paid versus free movement. Template wiring retains the Fateful dialog while removing its floating payer control. Existing map/NPC evolution harnesses inject the new dependencies; `mapRadialActions.test.mjs` adds a real handler regression for combat ending with the same active token. All 1,012 tests and typecheck pass, with no tests skipped; no live browser/database check was performed.

- Effect actions and Lag (2026-11-09a): real radial/page/endpoint regressions cover optional upgrades, Bolster exclusion, mandatory Complex costs, Shield round limits, one-damage/capped healing, Immunity and Support/damage resource application through single/area and deferred defenses. Intercede tests cover intercepted effects, Shield absorption and Combat Monster counts. `lagInitiative.test.mjs`, turn/knockout and PUT regressions cover dBIT/full-roll/max-not-sum behavior, next-round scheduling, grouped partner slots, restoration, repeated round saves and stale cleansed effects. Combat-end tests cover temporary-Wound cleanup. All 1,000 tests and typecheck pass; no tests skipped. No live browser/database check was performed.

- Radial Charge (2026-11-09): `chargeRadial.test.mjs` compiles the real EncounterMap watcher/handlers for ordinary Charge targeting, before/after movement, partner identity, move-save ordering, AoE polling and cancellation, and renders each real map hint without rider inputs while retaining card controls. Shared radial tests cover Charge-only visibility, mutual exclusion, mixed options, costs and Pass rejection; real GM/player handlers and descriptor tests verify propagation and clearing. All 958 tests and typecheck pass; no live browser check was performed.

- GM XP budget (2026-11-08u): `tamerResources.test.mjs` renders the real accounting section with a GM-only Total editor and player read-only balances; exercises total changes, purchases/refunds, legacy purchased upgrades, invalid/minimum budgets and consumed Inspiration; and executes real POST/PUT handlers for derived wallets, invalid/inconsistent balances, player/GM authorization, zero-XP creation and a stale save following a reward before row-lock acquisition. Existing reward, import and combat resource regressions remain green. All 945 tests and typecheck pass; no live browser/database check was performed.

- Partner rewards and load failures (2026-11-08t): `rewardPartners.test.mjs` covers branches, legacy line-only relationships, stable roots, cycles, broken links, ownership boundaries and distinct partners. Real endpoint tests in `rewards.test.mjs` verify all-stage updates, choosing a second partner independently, stage-alias deduplication, highest-pool reconciliation and replay after records change. Real SFC rendering in `rewardsPage.test.mjs` verifies per-partner totals, failed-load recovery and retained success without reward resubmission. All 940 tests and typecheck pass. The user reported the live database error resolved; no further schema changes or live database checks were performed.

- DM rewards (2026-11-08s): `rewards.test.mjs` executes real GET/POST handlers against isolated transactional storage and SQL-expression adapters, covering individual/group grants, duplicates, concurrent retries, conflict reuse, bad amounts/foreign/enemy/incorrect-owner recipients, rollback, combat decision queues, post-commit publication, exact form selection, client retries and live refresh handlers. `campaignDm.test.mjs` covers account roles, rejection of unsigned UI cookies, signed password credentials/expiry/scope/password changes and issuance only after valid verification. `rewardsPage.test.mjs` renders real Library/reward SFCs for links, amounts/totals, selected forms, success and GM verification. All 931 tests and typecheck pass. Live database/browser validation was not possible with the configured database resetting connections; migrations 0024/0025 remain deployment prerequisites.

- Tamer resources/payers (2026-11-08r): `tamerResources.test.mjs` executes real form spending/refunds, Tamer POST/PUT persistence/validation and bundle round trips, preserving unknown legacy totals and lifetime XP across Inspiration consumption. It also compiles real player computeds, renders `InspirationPanel` as 7/7 then 0/7 and exercises GM maxima. `attackRadialOptions.test.mjs` verifies Tamer-paid evolution choices and NPC/absent-owner fallbacks; `mapHudPlacement.test.mjs` renders live GM action counts through zero, bonuses, spending and reset snapshots. All 903 tests and typecheck pass. Applying/verifying migration 0024 against the configured database was blocked by repeated `ECONNRESET`, including TLS checks; the migration remains required before deploying the new schema. No browser smoke test was performed.

- Third radial tier (2026-11-08q): the real component renderer now asserts simultaneous root/attack/options sectors with strictly increasing radii, stable parent paths, selected-attack highlighting, costs and rider toggles. Five new regressions cover attack switching and Back, independent attack/option paging, keyboard navigation, stale-branch pruning, and clip/text/viewport geometry at all eight edges/corners. Utility, real Canvas and page-handler tests cover an ordinary attack via Continue with an empty rider list; the menu has no None choice. Validation: all 891 tests and typecheck pass.

- Radial options/costs (2026-11-08p): `attackRadialOptions.test.mjs` covers combinations, None, limits, affordability and measured cost layouts across orientations. Real menu/Canvas/page-handler tests cover the third layer, retained toggles, live-tree validation, selection propagation, player Fateful review and paused/cancelled GM submissions. `tauntAttacks.test.mjs` also executes real single/area offer deductions for ordinary, Bolster, Lifesteal, Haste, Pass, Charge Pass and already-paid declarations. All 886 tests and typecheck pass; no browser smoke test was performed.

- Area forced movement (2026-11-08o): `areaPushPull.test.mjs` executes real group/NPC/damage/push-pull functions with isolated storage and room adapters. Its 31 cases cover Knockback/Pull across six shape tags and support/damage attacks, a last-Skip broadcast, sequential Pull occupancy, hit interceptors, player defenses and Immunity. Group resolution returns unbroadcast patches and leaves its supplied board snapshot intact. Existing claim rollback/post-commit tests still pass. All 854 tests and typecheck pass.

- NPC map evolution (2026-11-08n): `npcMapEvolution.test.mjs` compiles real GM/Canvas computeds and handlers. It verifies unlocked NPC menu choices, ordinary endpoint submission without Tamer/force, stale/off-turn/exhausted/attempted rejection, absent-line hiding and local-stage devolution. All 48 targeted evolution/radial tests and typecheck pass.

- Legacy Immunity (2026-11-08m): fifteen additional endpoint regressions in `tauntAttacks.test.mjs` cover support/damage Taunt, Fear, Stun, Shield, Burn, Knockback and Cleanse, plus deferred support Stun. They preserve existing effects, ordinary damage and target actions, reject false applied-effect logs, and ensure immune displacement/Cleanse never runs. All 819 tests and typecheck pass.

- Cleanse/map controls (2026-11-08l): `cleanse.test.mjs` covers the supplied multi-target example's shared maximum, individual effect counts, BIT reductions/removal, mutually exclusive modes, misses/interception, stale/duplicate/permanent effects, no-op casts, endpoint ownership/replay and failed/double UI submissions. Real attack-route tests cover single/area support/damage offer/Skip flows and player defenses. `gmAttackUnlock.test.mjs` verifies locks become selectable individually and failed unlocks preserve them. Placement tests enforce the combat drag restriction. `movementGhost.test.mjs` checks nearest lower floors/voxel tops, footprint centres, portrait/stance projection, gap geometry and grounded/bottomless cases; existing movement/path/GM-scroll tests still pass. All 804 tests and typecheck pass. Live browser interaction was not exercised.

- GM source management (2026-11-08k): `effectManagement.test.mjs` executes real Quick Apply/custom/source-edit handlers, covering required sources, same-name tokens, raw CPU versus stored Taunt potency, Fear BIT, immutable updates and ordinary effects. `mapRadialActions.test.mjs` verifies GM dispatch, repeat selection and player/removed-token rejection. `combatDice.test.mjs` checks directional Fear penalties; attack-route tests verify automatic Fear identity/potency. All 784 tests and typecheck pass.

- Deferred effects/Taunt (2026-11-08j): `tauntAttacks.test.mjs` covers real intercede-offer/Skip flows with GM/player roll-only payloads, single/area NPC targets, old incomplete pending definitions, canonical intercede calculations, player defense responses, legacy GM attacks, CPU potency/source identity, other support resolution families and Selective Targeting. `combatDice.test.mjs` checks the supplied CPU x2 / area CPU -2 rule and the zero-penalty floor; Swagger's debuff type is checked in `specialOrderCombat.test.mjs`. All 773 tests pass. Live browser interaction was not exercised.

- AoE voxel previews (2026-11-08i): `tests/aoeVoxelPreview.test.mjs` executes the actual renderer with Three.js geometry. Fourteen checks cover full-height bounds, 30% fill opacity, removal of horizontal/vertical shared faces and seams, concave outlines, hit/blocked color boundaries, deduplication, cached equivalent previews, geometry/material disposal and unchanged cell coverage for all six AoE shapes.
