### CI/CD Pipeline

- **No repository CI workflow detected** — no `.github/workflows/`, no `.gitlab-ci.yml`, no `Dockerfile`, no `docker-compose.yml`
- Deployment is manual (likely direct push to Railway based on `DATABASE_URL` pattern)

### Real-Time Map Sync (Encounter Positions/Destructibles)

**2026-11-10:** WebSockets are authenticated notification channels, plus validated GM-only structure damage. Normal movement, placement, unrestricted GM positioning and doors submit authorized HTTP commands; peers cannot author `unit-moved`, door or map-edit broadcasts. Accepted positions and participant state are persisted under the encounter command lock; scoped RoomState overlays let nested attack services read committed-intent positions without publishing rejected previews. Notifications publish after successful persistence. Disconnect/authentication rejection clears pending client writes and stops reconnecting until access is verified again. `encounter-sync.ts` sends accepted encounter snapshots after successful HTTP responses. Map autosaves notify after persistence and preserve independently updated door states. The deployment remains single-process; multiple instances require shared locking/pub-sub.
### Guiding Light (updated 2026-11-06d)

- Sources are active, positioned partner Digimon whose Tamer has unlocked Guiding Light. Only positioned allied Digimon outside reserve qualify as recipients; the source does not count itself. Tamers receive neither bonus, and stale aura effects are removed even when no active Digimon remain.
- Range comes from `computeAreaCells('burst', 'ranged', ...)`: the same spherical `BIT + 1` coverage used for map attacks, emitted from every source footprint cell. An ally qualifies if any cell of its full 3D footprint intersects. Each ally counts once; overlapping sources still grant at most +2 Accuracy per recipient.
- Encounter PUT recomputes after final combat roster changes, including removal, reserve/allegiance edits, auto-devolution and combat start. The digivolve endpoint recomputes using the new entity form and live room positions before its existing encounter write.
- `loadCampaignCombatContext` centralizes campaign level/settings defaults for Guiding Light, encounter PUT and digivolve. `loadEncounterCombatContext` shares the active-encounter gate for both standalone aura refresh paths. PUT reuses one campaign read across gravity, damage-over-time, start triggers and its final aura pass.
- `refreshGuidingLightForParticipants(encounterId, participants, positions?)` refreshes an action's own roster using shared `combatContext.ts` encounter/campaign loaders and explicit positions or live room state. It performs no independent encounter write or broadcast, so caller wounds, form changes and turn state are saved together. Knockout resolution, NPC attacks, dodge responses and intercede devolution use it; area groups refresh before subsequent uncovered targets resolve. The current claim's positions are forwarded when not yet broadcast.
- NPC automatic counterattacks add `getEffectStatModifiers(...).accuracy` before rolling, so Guiding Light and other accuracy effects apply consistently. Player counterattack prompts retain their existing roll flow.
- Regression coverage runs without a live database or server: aura targeting/counts/geometry and cleanup; current action state versus stored state; endpoint save ordering; knockouts/devolution/intercede ordering; NPC counterattack dice and prompt routing.

### Damage Over Time (Poison / Burn)

- **(2026-11-03i/j) Poison and Burn are resolved entirely server-side** in `server/utils/damageOverTime.ts`. A tick and its countdown always happen together, so an N-round effect resolves exactly N times. The client's per-turn countdown (`useEncounters.ts` `tickTurnEffects()`) skips `DAMAGE_OVER_TIME_EFFECTS`. This supersedes the 2026-04-14 per-turn Poison timing.
- **Poison:** the target digimon's current CPU as unalterable wound damage (temp wounds absorb it first) at the end of each round. EddySoul `poisonTicksAtTurnEnd` moves it to the end of the unit's own turn (for a grouped partner digimon, its Tamer's turn).
- **Burn while moving:** every movement path calls `applyMovementBurn`: `/actions/move` (map moves, Charge, `[Pass]`, clash repositioning), Knockback/Pull (`computePushPull`), Clash Throw and intercede claims. It stamps `lastMovedRound` on every mover, burned or not. Distance is counted in cells (Chebyshev): the client sends path steps, and displacements use `chebyshev(from, landing)` before any fall. GM free-move, placement and gravity falls don't count as moving.
- **Burn while standing still:** at a round end, a burned, positioned digimon whose `lastMovedRound` isn't that round takes Stage Bonus × 2, reduced by its Armor (`getBurnArmor`, as for moving) to a minimum of 1; a Fresh or In-Training digimon (Stage Bonus 0) takes nothing (2026-11-03m). House rule `burnStationaryIgnoresArmor` keeps it a flat Stage Bonus × 2. Core rule only: EddySoul `burnPerTwoSpacesMoved` deals nothing for standing still. Every round-end path passes map positions: the PUT and `npc-attack.post` via `loadStationaryBurnPositions`, and `resolveNpcAttack` via its own positions or `loadRoundEndContext`. Without a map there are no positions, so Burn only counts down.
- **Rules (2026-11-03m):** the campaign rules this module reads (`DamageOverTimeRules`: EddySoul `poisonTicksAtTurnEnd` and `burnPerTwoSpacesMoved`, house rule `burnStationaryIgnoresArmor`) come from `damageOverTimeRules(rulesSettings)`. Every loader returns them (`loadCampaignDamageOverTimeRules`, `loadEncounterRules().rules`, `loadDamageOverTimeRules`, `loadRoundEndContext`), and the PUT and `npc-attack.post` build them from the campaign they already loaded.
- **`lastMovedRound` is server-owned.** The PUT keeps the stored stamp when the body's is older, and clears it on the phase→'combat' transition. **A new movement path must call `applyMovementBurn`** (or at least stamp `lastMovedRound`), or the burned units it moves will also take the stationary penalty.
- **Partner digimon** have no `turnOrder` slot, so their timed effects count down at the end of their Tamer's turn (`tickTurnEffects`).
- **Knockouts (2026-11-03o):** an enemy that Poison or Burn knocks out leaves the encounter like any other knockout (see Enemy Knockouts below).
- **Partner knockouts (2026-11-08c):** movement/throw knockout resolution now devolves eligible partners in the same action, using combat history or their linked parent sheet. GM Quick Apply can still put Burn on a Naturewalk: Fire digimon.

### Encounter Snapshot Ordering (2026-11-07b)

A client receives the encounter three ways — WS `encounter-state`, the encounter GET, and the response of an action it just performed — and they can arrive out of order. Every one passes `createEncounterSnapshotGate()` (`utils/encounterSnapshot.ts`) before it replaces page state:
- **Ordering (2026-11-07e):** the row's database-owned `revision` takes precedence across push, GET and action response. Lower revisions are rejected; equal revisions can be reapplied. The UPDATE trigger orders writes even when request-start `updatedAt` timestamps run backwards. Legacy timestamp/push-version fallback is used only until an authoritative revision has been seen; an unversioned response cannot overwrite a revisioned row. The gate resets when the encounter id changes.
- `updatedAt` remains a display/legacy field; it is not a commit-order token. `tests/encounterSnapshot.test.mjs` still verifies that writers stamp it, and additionally checks reversed timestamps against increasing database revisions.
- `shapeActionResponse` normalizes all applied payloads, preserves already-trimmed log totals, and uses incoming live room state only when its room version is at least as new as the held one. Raw rows without a room version retain the client's newer geometry. New log entries are counted from `_battleLogTotal`.
- A rejected snapshot restores the newest accepted/displayed `currentEncounter`, including state eagerly assigned by a composable, while still processing action-specific record invalidations. All GM direct-result assignments now use the gate. A snapshot that unpublishes the encounter, ends it or removes the player triggers the full `loadData()` instead, so access rules and encounter choice are unchanged.
- `planRecordRefresh` decides which records to refetch (new/changed entity id, same-id stat edit, form change, Tamer-rewriting action hints); an ordinary attack or movement fetches none. Record reads and their assignments share one per-page queue with full record loads, and `mergeRecordsById` refuses older timestamps.
- Recovery: `useMapWebSocket.onReconnect` triggers one gated resync; the 120 s poll (full load) stays the backstop once the socket has given up after 5 retries. `encounter-updated` has no sender today; its debounced handler remains a fallback.

### Deferred Intercede Actions (2026-11-06e)

`interceptPenalty` records actions borrowed after a participant's turn. `applyDeferredIntercedeCost` deducts that debt from Simple Actions at the next turn start and clears it even when the cost exhausts the pool. Client `activateParticipant`, shared server `startTurn` and the legacy NPC-attack activation apply it to the active Tamer/standalone Digimon as well as the existing grouped partner. Round resets preserve debt until activation; removing an unrelated participant does not repay it. Divine Protection's separate `pendingSimpleActionPenalty` still applies independently.

### Area Defense Panel (2026-11-07d)

When an area attack hits several of one player's characters, the player defends them in one panel instead of a modal per target.
- **Grouping:** `groupDodgeRequests` groups by `data.intercedeGroupId`, which every area dodge carries (no-offer path in `intercede-offer.post.ts`; `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup` after the window). The panel opens for the first cast with two or more of the player's unanswered requests; a lone request, a single-target attack and a counterattack keep the one-at-a-time modal, and the requests the panel owns are kept off it. Array order is the defense order.
- **Per target:** its own Dodge pool (`computeDodgeDefense`, the same formula the single modal uses), modifier breakdown, Act of Inspiration choice, roll, optional Re-roll / +1 die, status and result. **Roll all unrolled** and **Submit ready defenses** act in defense order; individual controls remain.
- **Inspiration:** the pool is shared by the Tamer and partner. Every spend is awaited and accepted by the server before the dice change (Act: +5 dice, paid once per target; Re-roll and +1 die: 1 each), spends are serialized, and a refused spend changes nothing. A roll is never re-rolled for free: asking again does nothing.
- **Retries and updates:** a failed send keeps its roll and resends that roll; the server answers a dodge once (`answeredAt`, queue), so a retry after a lost reply or a second tab cannot repeat damage, and a request the server already answered counts as submitted. Updates keep rolls; a protected or removed target becomes "no longer needed" and nothing is sent for it. NPC targets of the same cast still auto-resolve on the server, unchanged.
- **Closing:** Close appears only when every defense is submitted or no longer needed; the panel then closes after five seconds or on Close through the same cleanup as a single dodge (cancel each request, delete each response). The battle log keeps every result.
- **Shared math (2026-11-07f, corrected 2026-11-07g):** Quick Reaction is added before successive-attack penalties and lasts until round rollover; Clash/Counterattack/Strike Fast halve the assembled pool only once. The server caps submitted halved pools to the canonical pool plus accepted request-bound Act/+1 bonuses; excess unpaid dice are trimmed from stored results and logs. Brown Armor automatic successes remain separate from the dice. Inspiration spends are awaited before adjustments and accepted bonuses survive refreshes without another Act charge; a paid Fateful choice fixes the selected faces for its request and includes earlier paid dice only once. An additional cast opens after the current batch closes.

### Pass Until My Next Turn (2026-11-07c)

A player's temporary, encounter-scoped "stop asking me to intercede". Separate from the permanent "Never intercede for X" lists, which it never edits.
- **Storage and ownership:** `encounters.intercede_opt_outs.temporaryPasses[ownerId]` (`ownerId` = the Tamer entity id offers are addressed to). Only the `updateLocked` helpers in `server/utils/intercedeOptOuts.ts` write it (row `FOR UPDATE`), the PUT ignores the column in the body, and the opt-out writers spread the stored object, so a pass, an opt-out save and a stale PUT cannot erase each other. Players only: the GM has no pass.
- **Expiry is derived.** `recordTurnStart` bumps the starting participant's `turnsStarted` at every turn start; a pass stores its Tamer's counter and `getActiveTemporaryPass` treats it as in force only while the counter is unchanged, the phase is `combat` and the Tamer is present and not in reserve. No timer, no cleanup write, and the turn change that expires it is the same write that bumps the counter. The PUT keeps the counter monotonic and clears all passes when combat is no longer running; a fresh duplicate drops them, a snapshot keeps them.
- **Applying a pass:** `start` and every newly created offer go through `applyActivePasses` → `resolvePassedOffers`, which removes the owner's whole offer and resolves its attack exactly as Skip does (`resolveIntercedeGroupIfDecided`: area group, dodge-roll for a player target, support effect, NPC auto-resolve). `intercede-pass` and the post-offer step run in `serializeIntercedeClaim`, so a pass is applied between decisions: a claim racing it either wins (the pass then finds nothing) or finds its offer gone (409); a concurrent Skip and pass do not overwrite each other. Claims are never blocked by a pass.
- **What a pass leaves alone:** other players' and the GM's offers; offers that carry a usable Quick Reaction (a different decision, applied by the GM on the player's behalf — the player page has no Quick Reaction control); permanent opt-outs.
- **Known gap:** `createIntercedeOffers` reads the encounter without a lock, so an offer created in the same instant a pass starts is handled by the client watcher (`autoSkipSuppressedOffer`) rather than the server.

### Atomic Intercede Claims (2026-11-06f)

The claim handler holds an encounter-row lock from its fresh read through offer validation, action/damage changes and persistence. Area claims reject a target or interceptor already recorded in the cast. An invalid/failed claim rolls back, leaving the next claimant able to try; an already-consumed offer returns 409 without further action, wound or log changes. Area auto-devolution shares the transaction for evolution-line writes. Knockout gravity returns positions to the handler, which broadcasts them and refreshes Guiding Light after commit. A per-encounter queue includes those room updates and the full Skip, opt-out and Quick Reaction workflows, preventing local stale decisions from restoring offers and ensuring later area claims see earlier movement. Different encounters use independent queues. These queues are process-local; the database row lock additionally serializes claim transactions across workers. Live room state remains process-local as described below.

### Enemy Knockouts (2026-11-03o)

- **Every knockout, whatever caused it.** An enemy (`isEnemy`, not `inReserve`) whose Wound Boxes are all filled leaves the encounter: an attack, Poison, Burn, a Clash Throw, a fall, interceding or reflected damage. `resolveEnemyKnockouts(state, candidateIds, ctx)` (`server/utils/enemyKnockouts.ts`) handles each one in this order: it devolves if it digivolved during combat, triggers an unused [Time Control] (restored to max − 3 wounds, +BIT to all stats), or is removed from `participants` and `turnOrder`, with its clash partner freed and "was defeated and removed from the encounter!" logged.
- **The turn moves on.** Removing an earlier slot shifts `currentTurnIndex` down one. Removing the active participant ends its turn: whoever now holds the slot starts theirs (`startTurn`, the server copy of the client's `activateParticipant`). Past the last slot the round wraps first: round-end Poison/Burn (`applyRoundEndEffects`), the round reset, then `applyRoundStartQualityTriggers`. An enemy knocked out by that round end, or by the fall at the turn change, is resolved the same way.
- **Only this request's knockouts.** Each caller passes the enemies its own request knocked out, against a `knockedOutEnemyIds` snapshot taken before any damage (take it before code that mutates participant objects in place, like a round wrap). An enemy already at 0 when the request arrived is left alone, so the GM can keep a downed enemy on the board and the GM page's own wound-edit removal works as before. Partner digimon are never removed; they devolve through the auto-devolve paths.
- **Callers:** the encounter PUT (the turn change's fall, Poison, Burn), `resolveNpcAttack` (the target on a hit, an attacker killed by reflect), `/actions/move` (Burn), the Clash Throw, `intercede-claim` (interceptor, moved target, attacker killed by reflect), `resolveAreaIntercedeGroup` (interceptors), the `responses` dodge path (reflect), `resolveInstantSupportEffect` (Knockback/Pull Burn) and `npc-attack.post`. **Each must save `turnOrder`, `currentTurnIndex` and `round` together** from the result (as the counterattack path does, 2026-11-03l).
- **Gravity at the new turn** (`ctx.gravity`): a map + positions computes the drops and returns them as `positionPatch` (`resolveNpcAttack`); `'room'` reads and broadcasts the room's positions (`applyEndOfTurnGravity`); omitted means none (the PUT already ran gravity for its turn change, and area-group interceptors are never the active participant).
- **No pending-request cleanup**, as with attack knockouts before: the GM page treats intercede, dodge, clash-check and health-roll requests as blocking, so no turn ends while one is open, and a dodge whose attacker has since been removed still resolves (`responses.post` reads the attacker null-safely).
- **Client:** `nextTurn()` already skips removed participants, and the PUT response carries the server's turn state. `EncounterMap.vue` cancels a Charge or `[Pass]` attack when `/actions/move` reports its mover `defeated`.

### General "Encounter Changed" Push (`encounter-state` / `encounter-updated`)

- The WS room broadcasts two kinds of "something changed" messages whenever any encounter action endpoint completes (`WebSocketMapMessage` union, `app/types/index.ts`), separate from the map-specific `position-patch`/`unit-moved`/`structure-damaged` messages above:
  - **`encounter-state`** (primary, 2026-06-13): `{ type: 'encounter-state', encounterId, encounter, version }`, where `encounter` is the full `buildEncounterPayload()` result (`server/utils/encounterPayload.ts`) — actionsRemaining-migrated, `RoomState` position/destructible snapshot merged in, `battleLog` trimmed to the last 50 entries (`_battleLogTotal`/`_battleLogWasTrimmed`). `version` is set to `Date.now()` at broadcast time in `encounter-sync.ts` (not `encounter.version`/`room.version`, which only increments on map events and would be stuck at 0 during non-map phases like Setup). Both pages apply `msg.encounter` directly to their encounter ref (`currentEncounter`/`activeEncounter`, `as any` cast — the WS-side `Encounter` interface in `app/types/index.ts` omits `pendingRequests`/`requestResponses`/`campaignId`, which only exist on the schema-inferred `Encounter`), guarded by `encounterId` (resets the version tracker on encounter switch) and a monotonic `version` (drops messages with `version < lastEncounterStateVersion`) — **no refetch**.
  - **`encounter-updated`** (fallback): `{ type: 'encounter-updated', encounterId }` — generic "encounter JSON changed, refetch" push, covering edge cases the direct-apply path might miss. Both pages debounce (~200ms) a full `fetchEncounter()`/`loadData()` on receipt.
- `server/utils/encounterRoom.ts` exports `notifyEncounterUpdated(encounterId)`, a thin `broadcast()` wrapper used for the `encounter-updated` fallback.
- **`app/server/middleware/encounter-sync.ts`** is a global Nitro server middleware (distinct from the client route middleware in `middleware/campaign-access.ts`/`middleware/dm-access.ts` — see Dependency Graph). It runs on every request; for any `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE` to `/api/encounters/[id]` or any subpath of it (`path.match(/^\/api\/encounters\/([^/?]+)(?:\/.*)?$/)`) that finishes with a 2xx status, it extracts `encounterId` from the path and, on `res.on('finish')`, calls `buildEncounterPayload(encounterId)` and broadcasts `encounter-state`. This generalized match (2026-06-15, previously only enumerated `POST .../actions/*` and bare `PUT /api/encounters/[id]`) covers all ~23 action endpoints, the bare encounter PUT (`nextTurn`/`endCombat`/all other `updateEncounter()` call sites — map placement, wound edits, etc.), `POST .../requests` and `.../responses` (digimon-selection requests from Engage Combat, initiative-roll and other player responses), and `DELETE .../requests/[requestId]` / `.../responses/[responseId]` (GM/player dismiss-response flows) — all in one file, with no per-endpoint wiring. Since `broadcast()` doesn't exclude the sender, the acting client also self-corrects via this push. `POST /api/encounters` (create, no id segment) doesn't match; `DELETE /api/encounters/[id]` would match but `buildEncounterPayload` returns `null`/`undefined` for a deleted encounter, making it a no-op via the existing `if (!encounter) return` guard.
- Both the GM encounter page (`encounters/[id].vue`) and the player page (`player/[tamerId].vue`) open their own WS connection to `/api/encounters/[id]/ws` (independent of, and in addition to, the connection `MapCanvas`/`EncounterMap` opens when a map view is active).
- Polling remains as a fallback but is lengthened from `30000`ms to `120000`ms on both pages, since `encounter-state` now handles normal-case responsiveness without any HTTP round trip; polling only catches eventual consistency after a dropped WS message or reconnect gap.
- **`MapCanvas.vue`** (2026-06-13b) has a watcher on `props.participants.map(p => \`${p.id}:${p.entityId}\`).join(',')` that calls `buildSprites()` when any participant's underlying entity changes (e.g. digivolve/devolve reassigns `entityId` to a different `Digimon` record with its own sprite/stage) or participants are added/removed — previously only `participantPositions`/`digimonMap`/`tamerMap`/active-participant changes triggered a rebuild.
- **`player/[tamerId].vue`** (2026-06-13b) gained `syncEncounterDigimon()`, mirroring the GM page's existing helper: on `encounter-state` (and in `loadData()`), it fetches via `/api/digimon?ids=...` any Digimon referenced by `activeEncounter.participants` that aren't yet in `allDigimon` (e.g. a digivolved entity's new `entityId` wasn't in the original fixed-id-list fetch), so other players' maps can resolve the new sprite/stage.

### Encounter Setup Handshake (GM ⇄ player prompts)

> Last verified: 2026-07-29

The setup exchange is a per-tamer state machine spread across three files. Read together before changing any of them.

1. **Queue.** GM adds a tamer via "Add Participant" → `createParticipant('tamer', …, initiative: -1, …)`. `initiative < 0` is the "queued, not yet set up" marker (`pendingSetupTamers`).
2. **Prompt.** "⚔ Engage" (bulk, or per-card) → `POST .../requests` with `digimon-selection`. Available in **both** `setup` and `combat` phase — a mid-combat joiner must go through this, because a bare `initiative-roll` has no `data.digimonId` and so never creates the partner digimon participant.
3. **Pick.** Player answers → `responses.post.ts` intercepts `digimon-selected`, deletes the picker request, and immediately creates an `initiative-roll` carrying `data.digimonId` (or `{}` for solo). No response row is stored for this step.
4. **Roll.** Player answers `initiative-rolled` → stored as a response for the GM to process. Repeat submissions for the same `requestId` are no-ops.
5. **Commit.** GM clicks Process → `processResponse` updates the queued tamer participant in place, **reconciles** their partner digimon (updates an existing participant by `entityId`, keeping its id so its map position survives; drops any other partner of that tamer; appends only if none existed), rebuilds turn order via `rebuildTurnOrder`, then cancels the request and deletes the response through the atomic endpoints.

Invariants worth preserving:

- **Setup prompts are single-slot per tamer.** Creating either prompt type retires the other for that tamer (see the note under API Schema → Request/Response management). Re-sending is the supported way to let a player redo a mis-tapped digimon/solo choice.
- **The digimon picker outranks the initiative modal on the player page.** Both are `Teleport`ed `fixed inset-0 z-50`, so DOM order alone decides which is visible; `hasUnrespondedInitiativeRequest` explicitly yields to `hasUnrespondedDigimonRequest` rather than relying on the requests never overlapping.
- **Never PUT a client-filtered `pendingRequests`/`requestResponses` array.** Use `POST .../requests`, `DELETE .../requests/[id]`, `POST .../responses`, `DELETE .../responses/[id]` — they read fresh server-side and merge. The whole-array PUTs that `processResponse` used to do silently dropped anything that arrived between snapshot and write, which is what made concurrent tabs lose prompts.
- **An unprocessable response blocks the encounter.** `canStartCombat` requires `unprocessedResponses.length === 0`, so any response whose request has vanished must be deleted, not skipped.
- **Removing a tamer must retire their prompts** (`clearSetupExchangeForTamer`), or the leftover initiative modal keeps covering the player's screen.
- **Players only ever see published encounters** (`encounters.visibleToPlayers`; player fetches pass `?visibleToPlayers=true`). A tamer legitimately queued into several encounters is resolved on the player page by: pending request for me → `phase === 'combat'` → most recently updated.

### Reserve Roster (GM staging)

> Last verified: 2026-11-07

`CombatParticipant.inReserve` marks a participant the GM has staged but not yet brought into
the fight. It is a **display/eligibility** flag, not a separate collection — reserves live in
the same `participants` array as everyone else.

- **One choke point hides them from combat**: `rebuildTurnOrder()` (`useEncounters.ts`) drops
  `inReserve`, and everything turn-order-derived follows — `sortedParticipants`,
  `hierarchicalParticipants`, the GM participant list, the turn tracker, the map HUD.
- **One choke point hides them from the map**: `EncounterMap.vue`'s `liveParticipants` feeds
  `MapCanvas` and `MapPlayerHUD`. `MapCanvas` would skip them anyway (`if (!pos) continue` in
  `buildSprites`/`updateCharacterOverlays`), as would the occupancy and throw helpers.
- **Direct `participants` reads need explicit exclusion.** The ones that matter today:
  `canStartCombat`, `pendingSetupTamers`, `getAttackTargets` (GM page); `myParticipants`,
  `getEnemyTargets`, `getClashTargets`, `getPlayerDirectTargets` and the Enemy Scan / Intercede
  Options lists (player page, via `visibleParticipants`). **Any new list built from
  `encounter.participants` rather than `turnOrder` must filter `inReserve`.**
- **Deploy is type-split** (`handleDeployReserve`): a tamer clears the flag and re-enters the
  normal setup handshake above (`requestSetupForTamer`), which is what creates their partner and
  their initiative; a digimon/NPC rolls initiative inline, or inherits its partner tamer's
  initiative when that tamer is already in the fight. A reserved tamer and their reserved partner
  always deploy together — a partner acts on its tamer's turn and can't stand alone in the order.
- **Deployed ≠ placed.** Deploy deliberately leaves the participant without a map position, so it
  shows up unplaced (📍) on the `MapPlayerHUD` cards, which are the placement control (2026-11-07j;
  the separate placement panel and its collapsed pill are gone). Placement is available for the GM
  in any non-`ended` phase (narrowed to unplaced units outside setup, because clicking a placed
  card *lifts it off the board*); players get it mid-combat while any of their characters is
  unplaced. Click a card then a tile, or drag the card onto the map. **The HUD auto-expands when
  placement becomes active**, so a reserve deployment needs no digging, and its minimised icon
  carries the count of characters still to place.

> **Player visibility is client-side redaction.** A player's own reserves are shown greyed
> ("⏸ Waiting to join"); enemy reserves are filtered out of every player-side view. But the WS
> room has no per-peer identity (`ws.ts`'s `joinRoom(encounterId, peer)`) and
> `encounter-sync.ts` broadcasts a single `buildEncounterPayload()` result to every peer, so a
> staged enemy **is** present in the payload the player's browser holds — it is simply never
> rendered. Same trade-off `MapBattleLog.vue` already makes redacting NPC dice, and consistent
> with the all-client-side model documented under Auth Flow. Enforcing it for real needs peer
> identification at WS join plus per-role payloads.

### Campaign-Wide Roll History (`roll-logged` WS push)

- **(2026-07-08)** A second, encounter-independent WS channel exists for `RollLogPanel.vue`, since skill/attribute/torment rolls happen both inside and outside encounters and the panel is mounted globally (`app.vue`), not just on encounter pages.
- `server/api/campaigns/[id]/ws.ts` joins the peer into `server/utils/encounterRoom.ts`'s existing room registry under the key `campaign:{campaignId}` (not `{encounterId}`) — the registry was already keyed by an arbitrary string, so no changes were needed to `encounterRoom.ts` itself. These rooms carry no map state: no `full-state` send on connect, no inbound `message` handling, and (unlike encounter rooms) no debounced DB persistence — the roll log's durability comes from the `roll_log` table writes in `rollLog.ts`, not from `RoomState`.
- `server/utils/rollLog.ts`'s `appendRollLogEntry(campaignId, entry)` is the single write path (used by both `rolls.post.ts` and `new-day.post.ts`): insert → prune to the newest 50 rows for that campaign → `broadcast(campaign:{id}, {type:'roll-logged', campaignId, entry})`.
- Client: `RollLogPanel.vue` opens its own connection via `useMapWebSocket(campaignId, 'campaigns')` (the composable's `basePath` param, added alongside this feature) and unshifts incoming entries into its local list (capped at 50) — no polling fallback, since the panel already does an authoritative `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/rolls` on mount/campaign-change.

### Ambient Scene Image (`scene-updated` WS push)

- **(2026-08-19)** A GM-set image (+ optional caption) shown to everyone in a campaign as a roleplay visual aid — deliberately unrelated to, and independent of, the 3D combat map (`maps` table/`MapCanvas.vue`). Reuses the same `campaign:{id}` WS room `RollLogPanel.vue` already established (no new room/channel), since this needed the identical "push a small live update to every page in this campaign" shape.
- Storage is two nullable columns directly on `campaigns` (`sceneImageUrl`/`sceneImageCaption`, migration `0019_add_scene_image.sql`) rather than a new table — there is only ever one active scene per campaign (no history, no per-scene CRUD), so no `roll_log`-style append-only table was needed.
- `PUT /api/campaigns/[id]/scene` is the single write path: updates the `campaigns` row, then `broadcast(campaignRoomKey(campaignId), {type: 'scene-updated', campaignId, sceneImageUrl, sceneImageCaption})`. `imageUrl: null` clears both fields (used for "Clear").
- **No server-side DM check** — same "client/page-level enforcement only" model as the ~40 existing combat/GM-action endpoints (see Auth Flow). Deliberately **not** routed through `campaigns/[id].put.ts`, since that endpoint requires owner/co-owner once a campaign has an owner (`requireOwnerOrCoOwner`), which would wrongly exclude co-DMs and DM-password-only GMs from setting a scene.
- Client: `ScenePanel.vue` (mounted globally in `app.vue`, alongside `RollLogPanel`) opens its own `useMapWebSocket(campaignId, 'campaigns')` connection — a second, independent socket to the same room RollLogPanel already holds open, matching the existing precedent of multiple independent WS connections per room (e.g. an encounter page and its `MapCanvas` both connect to the same encounter room) rather than a shared/deduped connection. `isDm` is the same client-side `campaign-dm-{id}` cookie check `encounters/[id].vue` uses for its own `isDm` computed.
- **(2026-11-03) Prepared scene tabs.** Up to 10 GM-prepared scenes live in `campaigns.scenePresets` (jsonb, migration `0022_add_scene_presets.sql`), written only by `PUT /api/campaigns/[id]/scene-presets`. Unlike the live scene they are **never broadcast** and not returned by `GET /scene`, because the `campaign:{id}` room includes players. GMs fetch them on campaign load and each time the scene is opened, which picks up a co-GM's or another device's edits; concurrent edits are last-write-wins on the whole array. Showing a tab, from the editor or the GM's ◀ ▶ arrows, still goes through `PUT /scene`, so players only ever receive the one live scene via `scene-updated`.

### Feature Flags

- **EddySoul house rules** (`EddySoulRules` in `types/index.ts`): toggleable rule variants stored in `campaigns.rulesSettings.eddySoulRules` — controls accuracy/damage/armor remapping, charge cost, digivolution limit (5/day), warp evolution, enemy double wounds, `directRangeOverrides` (custom Direct/Bolster Direct/Digivolve map ranges, default 15/10/15), `modeChangeFreeSwapsPerCombat` (Mode Change X.0 Rank 2: first 3 swaps per combat cost no Simple Action, tracked via `participant.modeChangeFreeSwapsUsed`), etc.
- **House rules** (`HouseRules`): 7 toggles — stun max duration, max temp wounds, signature move battery, healing rules, allow duplicate stat max values (tamer creation), flexible CP splits (tamer creation), Burn's standing-still damage ignoring Armor (`burnStationaryIgnoresArmor`, 2026-11-03m)
- **Torment requirements** (`TormentRequirements`): Mode + minimum severity counts
- **Skill renames** (`SkillRenames`): Per-campaign renaming of tamer skill labels
- **Skill Orders** (`rulesSettings.houseRules.skillOrders: boolean`): House rule. When enabled, each tamer skill unlocks a Skill Option once the skill hits its threshold (4/5/6) and the governing attribute has its first Special Order (3/5/6). Surfaced on tamer sheets + an in-combat action panel via `skill-order` endpoint. Toggle is now part of the House Rules section in campaign settings.
- All flags read from `campaign.rulesSettings` passed into composables and server action handlers; no external flag service

---

## 9. Quick Reference
> Last verified: 2026-04-14

### How to Run Locally

```bash
# 1. Install dependencies
npm install

# 2. Set env var (create .env.local)
echo "DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@host/db" > .env.local

# 3. Apply migrations
npm run db:migrate

# 4. Start dev server
npm run dev
# → http://localhost:3000
```

### How to Run Tests

From the `app` directory:

```bash
npm test
npm run typecheck
npm run build
```

The combat regression suite uses Node tests and the installed TypeScript/Vue compiler tooling without a database. See Testing Strategy for coverage and the optional Node 22 in-process invocation.

### How to Deploy

```bash
npm run build
# Outputs to .output/server/index.mjs

node .output/server/index.mjs
# Requires DATABASE_URL in environment
```

### How to Inspect the Database

```bash
npm run db:studio
# Opens Drizzle Studio web UI connected to DATABASE_URL
```

### Key Files to Read First (Onboarding Order)

1. [types/index.ts](app/types/index.ts) — All types, enums, rule constants (STAGE_CONFIG, BASE_STAT_RANGES, etc.). Read this first.
2. [server/db/schema.ts](app/server/db/schema.ts) — Exact database shape for all 5 tables.
3. [data/qualities.ts](app/data/qualities.ts) — Quality system (DDA 1.4 rules §3.03–3.16); critical for DP and combat.
4. [data/attackConstants.ts](app/data/attackConstants.ts) — Attack tag definitions; used everywhere in combat.
5. [server/utils/applyEffect.ts](app/server/utils/applyEffect.ts) — Effect stacking/mutation rules.
6. [server/utils/resolveNpcAttack.ts](app/server/utils/resolveNpcAttack.ts) — Full server-side attack resolution; the most complex combat logic.
7. [server/api/encounters/[id]/actions/attack.post.ts](app/server/api/encounters/[id]/actions/attack.post.ts) — Player attack flow; traces through intercede, bolster, counterattack.
8. [composables/useEncounters.ts](app/composables/useEncounters.ts) — Client-side encounter state and all combat action API calls.

### Domain Glossary (DDA 1.4 TTRPG Terms)

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Wounds | HP/health damage (not called HP in this system) |
| DP | Digimon Points — build budget for qualities and stat bonuses |
| Bolster | Spend extra action for +2 accuracy or alternative stat bonus |
| Clash | Grapple mechanic — controller vs controlled |
| Clash Attack | Clash sub-action (2 simple actions): the controller uses one of their MELEE attacks (incl. Basic Melee) on the opponent; target dodges at half. Client picks the attack + rolls accuracy (`startClashAttack`→`confirmClashAttack`), then POSTs `clash-action` `actionType:'attack'`. A **charge** (non-area) attack opens a two-button choice in the picker (`pickClashAttack`→`clashChargeChoice`): **Attack & stay** (`chooseClashCharge(false)`, keeps the clash) or **Attack & move** (`chooseClashCharge(true)`, ends the clash then triggers a free charge move via `encounterMapRef.startChargeAfterMove(controllerId)`). A **pass** attack always ends the clash (server-enforced). **No one may intercede a single-target clash attack** — `intercede-offer` suppresses all interceders when `body.clashAttack` is set. |
| Clash Attack (Pass / area) | When the chosen clash attack is an AREA attack (`getAreaShape(attack.tags) != null`, e.g. Pass), `confirmClashAttack` does NOT take the single-target path — it sets `clashAreaAttack = { controllerId, opponentId }`, switches to map view, and calls `selectAttackAndShowTargets` so the normal area-aim flow renders the highlight, lets the controller aim, and **moves** the attacker (`EncounterMap.onAreaAttackConfirmed`→`computePassLanding`). `confirmAreaAttack` then force-includes the clash opponent in `targetIds`, passes `clashNoInterceptTargetId: opponentId` (GM → `intercede-offer` directly; player → `performAttack`→`attack.post.ts`→`intercede-offer`), and ends the clash via `executeClashAction(controllerId, 'end')` after submit. `intercede-offer` excludes `clashNoInterceptTargetId` from `areaIntercedeEligibility`, so third parties may intercede for OTHER area targets but **not** the clash opponent. The player path passes through `attack.post.ts`, whose in-clash guard (`if (actor.clash) → 403 "Cannot use regular attacks while in a clash"`) is bypassed when `clashNoInterceptTargetId` is present. |
| Clash reposition (clash-move) | Map-only controller sub-action: the controller may move while staying in footprint contact with the opponent. The radial "Reposition" button emits `clash-move`, handled internally by `EncounterMap.onClashMove`, which filters the normal reachable-cell set to cells keeping min-Chebyshev footprint distance ≤ contact range (reach-aware). The clash is preserved; the controlled participant cannot reposition. |
| No outside-clash dodge | A clashing participant cannot dodge attacks from anyone who is not their clash opponent (their own opponent's Clash Attack uses half-dodge instead). **(2026-11-03d)** Derived from live clash state by the shared pure predicate `app/utils/clashRules.ts` → `isAttackedFromOutsideClash(attacker, target)` wherever a dodge is rolled or resolved — server: `resolveNpcAttack` (pool 0, no Brown-armor auto success) and `responses.post` (submitted successes forced to 0); client: the player dodge modal (`dodgeBlockedByClash`: 0d6, banner, no Act of Inspiration) and GM `handleGmDodgeRoll` (0 dice) — so dodges created after an intercede window, Quick Reaction or a counterattack are covered. The `cannotDodge` request flag set by `intercede-offer` (`targetCannotDodge`) is only the attack-time snapshot, OR'd in. Quick Reaction isn't offered for a target that can't dodge. Outside-clash armor (below) still applies on top. |
| Outside-clash armor | **(2026-11-03c)** A clashing target hit by an attack from outside its clash (attacker not in the same `clashId` — `isAttackedFromOutsideClash`) adds its own CPU + its clash opponent's CPU to Armor; a Selective Targeting attacker faces only the target's own CPU; tamers count as CPU 1 (same as Clash Pin). Computed at damage time from live clash state by `server/utils/clashArmor.ts` → `getOutsideClashArmorBonus`, called inside `computeAttackDamage` (all `resolveNpcAttack` paths + `intercede-claim`) and the `responses.post` dodge-roll calc — nothing is threaded through request data. Because it is real Armor, Armor Piercing / Tank Buster / attacker Finesse reduce it too. Logged as `clashArmorBonus` + effects note `Clash: +N Armor (CPU)` on the Dodge/Intercede battle-log entry. |
| Clash Throw | Clash sub-action: controller hurls the controlled target up to their Body Stat (meters) to a player-aimed landing cell, dealing Damage Stat + fall damage; if it lands the target adjacent to a group of 2+ opposing participants, triggers a secondary `throw-impact-attack` (basic Ranged, +CPU/Body accuracy) |
| Area-Attack Intercede | Claiming a target in an Area Attack always repositions the interceptor into the AoE adjacent to the target (taking the hit at 0 dodge) AND throws the target to a player-chosen landing cell outside the AoE (Clash Throw positioning rules, fall damage only — no Damage Stat hit, no secondary attack); the old separate "soak the hit"/"Throw Ally Out of the Blast" claim types are unified into this single flow |
| Group of Enemies | 2+ opposing participants within 1 cell (Chebyshev) of a Clash Throw's landing footprint — triggers the throw's secondary impact attack |
| Digivolve/Devolve | Evolve to higher/lower form in combat |
| Warp Evolution | Skip stages during digivolution (requires Willpower DC) |
| EddySoul | Homebrew rule variant set for DDA 1.4 |
| Special Order | Tamer combat ability unlocked by attribute scores |
| Battery | Signature Move resource (charges per stage) |
| Positive Reinforcement | Quality giving tamer a mood meter (1–6) for digimon |
| Combat Monster | Quality that accumulates bonus damage each round, capped at `totalHealth` (= Base Health + Bonus DP Health); cap is restored from the pre-evolve snapshot on devolve |
| Counterattack | Quality that lets digimon auto-attack when opponent misses |
| Data Optimization | Quality that boosts one specific stat |
